Term counts on ethane, propane, propene and butane are asserted in `verify_mm_ff.js` §1.

  **Hybridisation now reads the coordination number, so a tetrahedral P/S/Cl with one double bond is not flattened** — "any multiple bond makes the centre sp²" is right for a two- or three-coordinate π centre and wrong for everything else: a sulfoxide, a phosphine oxide, a sulfone and a chlorate all carry short terminal-oxygen bonds and are pyramidal, their extra bonds being dative/π* descriptions rather than a second half-bond in the plane. Such a centre was bent toward 106° and — at three neighbours — given an out-of-plane term that forced the pyramid flat. A multiple bond now reaches sp² only at two-coordinate centres or at a three-coordinate centre of an element whose three-coordinate π centres really are planar (B, C, N, O); everything else with three or four σ-neighbours stays sp³. Sulfur keeps 98°, phosphorus 100°, chlorine the tetrahedral default.

  **A conjugated nitrogen is planar, because the topology now says so** — an amide, a carbamate, an aniline and a hydrazide write their C–N bond as a single bond, so the bond orders alone gave the nitrogen the sp³ target and a pyramid with no out-of-plane term at all. The hybridisation pass now promotes a nitrogen of two or three single bonds that hangs off a π centre: it takes the sp² angle, gains the improper, and its bond to that centre becomes the conjugated two-fold torsion — which is the whole of what "the lone pair is in the π system" means to this field. Only nitrogen is promoted (a two-coordinate oxygen's own angle is already the table's, and a sulfur or phosphorus oxide is pyramidal by the rule above).

  **The hydrogen bond exists when only the donor may move** — the D–H···A term is a three-atom term, so it moves with the donor even when the hydrogen and the acceptor are both fixed; but the non-bonded pair list only carried pairs with a mobile end, and the pair the well rides on is the hydrogen–acceptor one. A run that was given just the donor therefore had no hydrogen-bond term at all, and the plain Lennard-Jones pair then pushed the hydrogen away from its partner — a partial optimisation could tear a hydrogen bond apart. A second sweep adds those pairs (hydrogen with a mobile donor, acceptor of N/O/F, not bonded and not 1-3), and only those: an H···H pair with no acceptor would be a fixed–fixed Lennard-Jones term, which this module's accounting deliberately leaves out.

  **`maxStep` is a distance, not a prefactor** — the trust radius was computed as the cap divided by the largest preconditioned component and the line-search prefactor was then *multiplied* into it, so an accepted step could travel up to four times the cap (0.6 Å an atom at the default 0.15) — a different molecule. The prefactor is now clamped by the cap instead of scaling it, so no atom moves further than `maxStep` in one accepted step whatever the search does, and the growth on accepted steps only ever recovers a step the line search had previously halved. `verify_mm_ff.js` §5 walks a run one step at a time and measures every displacement.

  **Every 1-3 pair is carried, so a clash cannot be pushed in unseen** — the clash guard between two atoms sharing a neighbour was only admitted to the pair list when the pair was *already* inside 0.6 σ at build time, but the list is rebuilt only after an atom has drifted 0.5 Å: a pair that was two ångströms apart when the list was built and collided before the next rebuild had no repulsion at all, which is the one case the guard exists for. The pair is now carried whenever it is within the list's reach — the smooth switch in the energy (zero above 0.45 σ, full strength below 0.35 σ) is what decides what it contributes, so a distant pair adds nothing and a near one can only push the two apart.

  **An answer, a pause and a progress chunk now quote numbers measured at the geometry they return** — `finish()` recalled the energy and forces recorded when the best geometry was accepted, but those numbers are only valid against the pair list they were taken with, and a rebuild between that step and the call left them describing a geometry the answer no longer stands on. The reported geometry is now re-measured (moved to, pair list rebuilt from it, energy and forces read there) whenever the stored reading is no longer current, which is a no-op in the ordinary case and the whole of the guarantee when it is not; `step()` takes the same measurement so a streamed chunk and a pause cannot disagree. `converged` is read from that same measurement — it said "converged" beside a force above the tolerance whenever the search had stopped at a point a rebuild had since moved — and the message names the rare case where the force target was met at an earlier point.

  **The metal table covers the rest of the d block and the lanthanides** — Y, Tc, Hf, Ta, W, Re, Os, Ir, Pb, Bi and La–Lu were not metals to this field, so a coordination term was never built for them and their ligands were bent toward the generic 109.47° default; their electronegativity and static polarisability were also missing, so their bonds carried no partial charge and the polarisation term had nothing to work with. The tables are extended (Pauling values and static dipole polarisabilities), no already-tabulated value is changed, and calcium — an alkaline earth, not a transition metal — still owns no coordination term.

  **The panel's description is generated from the constants it describes** — it claimed a 5–12 Å cutoff where the option accepts 4–14, called 30° the only angle cap (the code has 30° compressed and 60° open), and described the taper as a bare switch-off. Every window, cutoff, barrier and well depth in those lines is now read off the constant it describes, so the panel cannot print a field the optimiser does not use.

  **The option clamps are the ones the panel can reach** — `gradientTol` accepted 1000 (a target every structure meets before its first step) and `maxStep` accepted 2.0 Å (a step that can tear a structure apart); they are now 1e-4–1 and 0.01–0.5 Å, with the loose ends of the panel's own choices (0.4 and 0.30) inside them.

  **The 1-4 half-scale no longer halves the hydrogen-bond well** — the well was scaled along with the Lennard-Jones part it replaces, so a D–H···A contact whose hydrogen and acceptor are three bonds apart was given half the well depth it has at any other separation. The scale belongs to the Lennard-Jones term (it is what the blend subtracts); the well is the well.

  **Deuterium and tritium are hydrogen** — an element symbol is normalised at the door, so a D or T bonds, bends, stretches, hydrogen-bonds and clashes exactly as H does. Left as their own symbols they took the generic van der Waals radius, well depth and covalent radius (a D–O bond relaxed to ~1.4 Å instead of 0.96) and were not hydrogen to the torsion, the donor walk or the hydrogen-bond term.

  **A bondless ion carries its formal charge** — the charge model shifts charge along bonds only, and the "neutralise everything" step after it was dead arithmetic: the transfers conserve the total by construction, so the mean shift was always zero, while a structure whose parts the bond list does not connect (a salt pair, a lone counter-ion) left every charge at zero and the Coulomb term with nothing to compute. An atom the bond list does not reach now takes the formal charge of the ion it can only be (alkali +1, alkaline earth +2, halogen −1) and whatever that adds to the total is taken back from the bonded skeleton, so a carboxylate with a sodium reads as −1 and +1. No bonded structure changes.

  **A ghost atom does not polarise towards an ion** — its Lennard-Jones well was already zero, but its polarisability was the table default, so the induced-dipole term pulled a counterpoise ghost and a nearby ion together through a term that has nothing to polarise. It is zero now, like the well.

  **An empty selection means the whole structure in both entry points** — `evaluateForceField` read it as "everything is mobile" (the documented meaning, and the only one that gives a structure's energy without building an index list) while `beginOptimization` refused it, so "no selection" was either everything or nothing depending on which function a caller reached for. Both read it as everything now; the panel still asks for a selection before it starts a run.

  **The Low preset leaves every plane and dihedral term off** — it kept the out-of-plane restraint while its own description promised "bonds and angles only", and an improper is a plane constraint: a run at Low was still flattening sp² centres. It is off, the description says so, and a Low run is now bonds and angles and nothing else.

  The three harnesses that guard this module are green: `verify_mm_switch.js` (**22**, the 1-3 switch, its force and the pause geometry), `verify_mm_hybrid.js` (**22**, the cumulated centre, SO2, and the angle targets that must not move) and the new `verify_mm_ff.js` (**78**, one section per defect above — the torsion flanks, the P/S/Cl targets, the amide and aniline, the donor-only hydrogen bond, the step cap walked atom by atom, the carried 1-3 pair, the answer's numbers against a fresh evaluation of its own geometry, the metal table, the generated description, the clamps, the well depth, D/T, the ion pair, the ghost, the empty selection and the Low preset). `mutate_mm_ff.js` puts each defect back into a private copy of the compiled module and asserts the harness notices: **15 of 15** mutations caught, and the unmutated copy still passes. `npm run compile` is clean and the panel's description still renders in its scrolling block.

- **2026-11-16 — Sixteen trajectory, dataset, script and cell defects: a frame layout taken from a field that does not state it, a script's molecule argument skipped, a value dropped for not reading — and each one now says what it did** — Every entry below is a place where a reader trusted a *statement about* the data instead of the data: a header field that names the header flavour and not the record layout, a command word whose molecule argument was never looked at, a column filtered out of the list when it did not parse. The files themselves read as before wherever they were read correctly; what changes is the files they used to lose.

  **DCD — the frame layout is probed, because the version field does not state it** — `src/parsers/trajectoryParser.ts` decided whether a frame carries a CHARMM unit-cell record from `charmm = version !== 0`. The VERNUM field says which *header* flavour a file has — X-PLOR writes 0, CHARMM and NAMD write 24/32/36, and a CHARMM header stores `DELTA` as a float and uses `ICNTRL(9)` as its crystal-type flag — and says nothing about the frames: LAMMPS writes a version-0 header and still writes the six-double cell record in front of every X/Y/Z triple. Such a file had its 48-byte cell record read as the X coordinates, the first marker check failed, and *every* frame was dropped with a "could not be decoded" warning that named the wrong cause. The scan now tries both readings of the records and keeps the one that walks the file — to its end, or to the frame count the header states — so the wrong reading is recognisable (it runs out of records a frame early or lands on a marker that is not the one it needs) instead of being trusted. The header's own flag stays the *prior*, which is also what resolves the one case the record lengths cannot: a 12-atom file, where a coordinate record is exactly as long as a cell record. When the probe has to overrule the header the panel says so (`the DCD header flags no unit-cell record, but every frame states one: the cell is read (LAMMPS writes a version-0 header)`), and a CHARMM flag with no cell record is reported the same way. A dead `natoms = 0` local in the same header went with it.

  **TRR — the `top` and `sym` blocks are skipped** — the frame header states thirteen sizes and the reader skipped `box`, `vir` and `pres` before the coordinates, leaving out `top_size` (XDR offset 20) and `sym_size` (24). A file that writes either decoded its coordinates that many bytes early — a frame of plausible-looking noise rather than an error, because the block sizes still added up. Both are now read, validated (non-negative, aligned to the frame's float size) and skipped; the block that follows is where the coordinates really start.

  **Sampling — the last frame of a run is added, not substituted** — `chooseTrajectoryFrameIndexes` walked the frames at a stride and then *overwrote* the last chosen index with `count - 1`, which left a gap of up to two strides at the end of every sampled trajectory (11 frames under a limit of 4 gave `0, 3, 6, 10`). The last index is now appended, and the stride is raised if that extra frame would exceed the limit — so the same case gives `0, 4, 8, 10`, a limit of one frame still keeps the frame the run ended on, and a file inside the limit still keeps every frame. The stride is computed from the limits rather than counted up to them, so a hundred-thousand-frame file no longer builds a hundred thousand throwaway lists.

  **A cell whose vectors have no length reports no angle** — `latticeToCellParams` divided by `b * c || 1`, so a degenerate cell (a zero `b`, a zero `c`) came out as a clean 90° cell in the cell panel and in every report; the three angles are now NaN when a vector they need has no length, which is what a lattice that cannot be described by six numbers actually is. Every reader already gates on `isUsableLattice` before a cell is built, so no panel shows it. That gate and the trajectory reader's own cell check also disagreed on the volume cut (1e-8 against 1e-9), so a box one accepted the other refused to invert; both now read `CELL_VOLUME_EPSILON` in `src/tools/crystalGeometry.ts`. A site stated in Cartesian coordinates keeps the position the file gives it: folding an unwrapped MD frame would tear a molecule that straddles the boundary in half and rewrite coordinates no user asked to change, so the builder does what every molecular viewer does and a site that lies outside the cell is *reported* instead (`N sites lie outside the cell the file states: they are drawn where the file puts them, not folded into the cell`) instead of passing in silence. A site written on the far face counts as inside, as it does everywhere else.

  **Extended XYZ — the frame boundary is the declared count, and a row that does not read is counted** — `src/parsers/extXyzParser.ts` read exactly `count` non-empty rows for the first frame and skipped any row it could not parse, so a file with one damaged row showed one atom fewer with no word about it, and the count the comment line declares was never checked. Rows are now read frame by frame against their own count line: an unreadable row still consumes its slot, is counted, and the first one is named by line number; a frame the file cuts short is reported as such (`the file ends after 3 of the 5 atom rows the first frame declares`). Detection also stopped depending on the first two lines being exactly where it expected them — a byte-order mark or a blank line in front of the count line turned a valid extended XYZ into a plain one, whose `Lattice` is then thrown away — and `Lattice` is accepted only when all nine components parse, because filtering a bad component out shifted every number after it and built a different, plausible cell. The frames after the first are no longer counted and discarded: `MolecularData.steps` carries them (each with its own cell, sampled and de-duplicated by `structureSteps`), so an ASE or OVITO trajectory opens with the frame navigation a dump has, and a per-frame `energy=` or a force column is named in the panel instead of vanishing.

  **DeepMD — a single-frame `(natoms, 3)` `coord.npy` reads without `type.raw`** — the reader told one frame written per atom from one atom written per frame by comparing the first array axis with the length of `type.raw`, and a dataset without that file fell into the second reading: a four-atom single frame became one atom (with the other three coordinates dropped) and the panel said nothing. `parseSetup.ts` now also hands over the row count of the companion `box` (a new `deepmdBoxFrames`), which is written once per frame and therefore answers the question the type list would have; with neither file present the array is read as one frame of `n` atoms, because a one-atom dataset is not a trajectory anyone writes. A `(frames, 3N)` array, a `(frames, natoms, 3)` array and a `type.raw` that matches are read exactly as before.

  **VMD scripts — the braced word is literal, the molecule argument decides, and `mol material` is used** — four independent misreadings of the script language itself. `tokenize` applied C-style backslash substitution *inside* `{…}` and `"…"`, where Tcl substitutes nothing in braces, so `{C:\data\run.pdb}` became `C:` and a tab and the structure the script names could not be found; braces are now literal (a backslash no longer escapes the closing brace either) and a quoted word applies only the escapes that decide where it ends (`\"`, `\\`, `\$`, `\[`, `\{` …), because the words this parser reads are file paths and selections and `\t` in `"C:\temp\x.pdb"` must not become a tab. `mol addrep`, `mol modstyle`, `mol modcolor`, `mol modselect`, `mol modmaterial` and `mol scaleminmax` all name the molecule they address, and all of them were applied to whichever molecule the last `mol new` opened — with several structures in one script, representations and their styles, colours, selections and colour-scale ranges landed on the wrong one; each now resolves its own molecule and counts the representation index inside it. `mol material` was parsed, carried in the payload and then never read: the structure panel now lists the materials in use, and a material whose meaning is see-through (`Transparent`, `Glass`, `EdgyGlass`) is reported as drawn opaque, which is the one difference a user can otherwise not see. `color scale method` was stored and displayed but never applied; `RGB` and `BGR` now reach the `Beta` ramp (`BGR` exchanges red and blue, which is the whole of what that method does), and `RYB`/`HSV` are named as not implemented rather than silently replaced by `RGB`. `color Display Background` knew seven names, so `ochre`, `iceblue` or `red3` left the background alone while the rest of the recipe was applied; all 33 palette names are accepted, as are a palette id and a `#rrggbb` / three-number form, and the name is resolved after the whole script has been read so a `color change rgb` written later still repaints it (the single visible consequence: `gray` is now the reference grey `#595959` instead of the CSS grey the old table invented). And the legacy group list — the per-representation `ColorID` + `index …` pairs the difference view colours from — stripped a leading `not`, so `not index 0 to 5` coloured exactly the five atoms the script excluded; a negated selection cannot be expressed without an atom count, so it forms no group at all now. In the page (`media/viewer.js`) the panel gained the material line, and its copy of `scalarColor` takes the same optional method argument as the reader's, so the two stay byte-for-byte equivalent as their comment requires.

  Verified with a new harness, `.workbuddy/tools/verify_parser_trajectory_vmd.js` (**116 checks** on the compiled readers): synthetic DCD files with and without cell records in both header flavours, a 12-atom file, a truncated last frame; a synthetic TRR with `top` and `sym` blocks; the sampling indexes at three limits; an in-cell, an out-of-cell and a fractional site plus a degenerate lattice; a BOM-prefixed extended XYZ, a `Lattice` with one bad component, an unreadable row, a cut-off frame, two frames with their own cells and a per-frame energy; a `(4, 3)` and a `(2, 6)` `coord.npy` with and without `type.raw` and a box row count; and VMD scripts covering braced and quoted Windows paths, three `mol`-argument forms, a see-through material, `BGR` against `RGB`, `ochre` with a later `color change rgb`, and a negated index selection. The readers it sits beside stay green: `verify_parser_formats` 116, `verify_parser_defects` 87, `verify_parser_hardening` 89, `verify_reader_fixes` 123, `verify_reader_records` 93, `verify_steps_frames` 21, `verify_trajectory_companions` 10, `verify_crystal` 254 (its one old source-text check aside), `verify_cif` 276, `verify_vesta` 66, `verify_space_group` 173, `verify_symmetry_check` 127 — and the page script still parses (`syntaxcheck_client.js`, 20 759 lines).

- **2026-11-16 — Sixteen per-format reader defects: a stated cell read as a molecule, a dataset's geometry lost between datasets, coordinates shifted by a field width — and every step of a multi-step run exposed as a frame** — These are the defects a *format's own* syntax leaves behind: a cell spelling the reader did not know, a value a later keyword inherits, a fixed column that moves with an option, a column that means one thing in one `atom_style` and another in the next. Each reader now reads the spelling the program documents, and says what it had to assume instead of substituting a default.

  **CP2K — the cell the file states is the cell on screen** — `src/parsers/cp2kParser.ts` built a `&CELL` only from three-number `A`/`B`/`C` vectors or three `ABC` lengths. The length spelling CP2K's `ABC` keyword stands for, written one number per edge (`A 10.0` / `B 11.0` / `C 12.0`), and the cubic shorthand `ABC 7.5` were both read as "no cell at all": the run came back as a molecule with the warning that the input states no `&CELL`. All of them now build the cell, with `ALPHA_BETA_GAMMA` honoured (90° when unstated) and `&CELL UNIT` still scaling it. `MULTIPLE_UNIT_CELL` was ignored, so a 2×2×2 run was drawn as its base cell and every periodic image the run saw was missing; the cell is now the repeated one, the repetition is named in the panel, and a `SCALED &COORD` is still placed as a fraction of the cell that was *written* — CP2K's topology multiple is a separate keyword, and using the supercell there would have moved every scaled atom.

  **ABINIT — a dataset inherits the geometry of the one before it** — `datasetKey` looked for `KEY<n>` and then the unsuffixed `KEY`, which is not ABINIT's rule: a variable a dataset does not state keeps the value of the *previous dataset*, and only a dataset that inherits nothing falls back to the global value. A three-dataset input that stated `xred2` showed dataset 3 with no atoms at all; the search now runs downwards from the dataset being read, so inheritance works and a value written for a later dataset cannot leak backwards (both the value and its file position, which is what the `xred`/`xcart`/`xangst` choice compares). The `cartesian coordinates (angstrom) at end` table was also abandoned at a blank line or a rule between its header and its rows, which left the *input* geometry on screen as if it were the relaxed one; separators above the table are skipped and one after it still ends the block. And a `typat` index beyond `znucl` is now the dummy element its warning names, instead of silently becoming the first element.

  **Quantum ESPRESSO — the monoclinic angle is the one `latgen.f90` reads, and a missing lattice parameter is stated** — the reported defect was that `ibrav = -12`/`-13` take their angle from `celldm(5)` and should take it from `celldm(4)`. `Modules/latgen.f90` says otherwise — `IF (abs(celldm(5))>=1.d0) … a3(1) = celldm(1)*celldm(3)*celldm(5)` is the `-12`/`-13` branch, and `abc2celldm` maps `cosac` onto `celldm(5)` for exactly those two — so the mapping is kept (`12`/`13` read `celldm(4)`, `-12`/`-13` read `celldm(5)`, the triclinic `14` reads all three the other way round) and is now pinned by a test against those vectors. What *was* wrong is that a cell missing its own angle was drawn as if the file had stated a right angle: `ibrav = -12` without `celldm(5)` now says which number is missing, as do `12`/`13`/`5`/`-5` and the triclinic cell. A file that states no lattice parameter at all (`celldm(1)`, `A` or `alat`) was likewise read with the 1 Å stand-in and no note; both the Bravais cell and a card written in `alat` units now state that, which is the case QE itself refuses to run.

  **LAMMPS — the Atoms layout, the Atoms header and the dump's own counts** — three column layouts were guessed by width alone, so a `sphere` row (`id type diameter density x y z`) was read as a `full` one and its *diameter* became the atom type — every atom of the cell relabelled, and with it its element, radius and bonds. `sphere`, `ellipsoid`, `body` and `dipole` are now named layouts (from the `Atoms # …` header or the input script's `atom_style`), and the width guess asks the file's own numbers: a type column is a positive integer within the declared `N atom types`, and the older `full` reading only stands where that is possible. The same evidence decides `charge` against `molecular` for the 6- and 9-column rows: a third column that is a valid type throughout is the type, so a molecular file with a single molecule ID is no longer read as a charge file with its molecule ID as the type (a fractional, zero, negative or out-of-range column is still a charge). The `Atoms` section was looked for from line 0 with a two-word test, so a data file whose title reads `Atoms of the water box` had its section start on the title line and came back with no atoms; the header is now the word alone, as `Bonds` already was. A dump whose first block is short — one appended after an interrupted run, or written by two runs — made every complete frame that followed "a different atom count" and dropped all of them: the count every frame must share is the one most blocks state. And a dump written without a `type` column (`dump … custom … id x y z`) labelled every atom type 1, which with a companion data file meant every atom became that type's element; it now reads as the dummy element and says so.

  **GROMACS — the coordinates are the numbers after the serial, not columns 20–44** — a `.gro` field is `%8.3f` only by default: `-ndec 6` widens each one by three characters, and a value that overflows its field (`-1000.000`) swallows the space before the next, so the fixed slices this reader took landed inside the wrong field — the first atom of such a file was placed at a mixture of its neighbours' digits, and a `-ndec 6` row came back with `y = 0`. The position is now the first three *numbers* after the serial, which is independent of the field width and of the token count, and stays anchored on the serial because `mdrun` appends three velocity columns that are not coordinates at all. The label block keeps its fixed columns where the serial really is in its own.

  **ABACUS — a section states its own mode, and the last table must be whole** — the `ATOMIC_POSITIONS` reading mode was declared once outside the section loop, so a second section that did not repeat its mode inherited the first one's: its Cartesian numbers were read as fractions (or the reverse) and the block was stacked at the origin. The mode is now per section, with the "no mode stated" fallback still reported. The `DIRECT COORDINATES` tables of a log were searched from the end for the first one holding *any* row, so a run cut off after one row drew that row as the final geometry. Every table is read and the last one whose row count matches the atom count the log states is the structure; a log with no complete table shows the rows it printed and says they are partial.

  **A multi-step run opens as frames, not as one snapshot** — a CP2K output, an `MD_dump` and a Quantum ESPRESSO output that prints a coordinate block per step describe one system over time, which is what the page already navigates for a text dump; only the last (or first) geometry used to survive. `src/parsers/periodicUtils.ts` now reduces the geometries a reader collected to Cartesian frames (`structureSteps`), dropping a geometry that repeats the one before it — a variable-cell relaxation that converged in its first step prints the same block twice and must stay the structure it is — and sampling a long run to the same frame and atom limits a binary trajectory uses. `MolecularData.steps` carries them, `src/webview/trajectoryDocuments.ts` builds the document the dump path already builds (`loadStructureStepsDocument`: template from the structure the reader chose, each frame with the cell it was computed in), and the open path in `src/webview/molecularViewer.ts` takes it for any reader that states more than one geometry. A file with one geometry keeps the crystal path it had, cell panel included; a converted document draws its box per frame and therefore writes no `CrystalData` — the same rule a multi-frame dump follows. The readers that gain frames are the ones whose output really repeats a table: an ABACUS `MD_dump` (every `MDSTEP`), a Quantum ESPRESSO relax/MD output (every `ATOMIC_POSITIONS` block, each in the cell printed above it, labelled `BFGS step n`), and a CP2K output that prints more than one `ATOMIC COORDINATES IN …` table. A CP2K molecular-dynamics output prints its coordinates once and leaves the trajectory in `water-pos-1.xyz`, which the viewer opens as a multi-frame XYZ.

  Verified with `.workbuddy/tools/verify_parser_formats.js` (**116 checks** on the compiled readers: the three CP2K cell spellings, `UNIT bohr`, the cubic shorthand, `MULTIPLE_UNIT_CELL` on the body and on the section line with a scaled site, `PERIODIC NONE` still a molecule; ABINIT's dataset inheritance in both directions, a final table after a blank line and after a rule, an unlisted type as `X`; QE's `-12`/`-13` vectors against `latgen.f90`'s formulas with `celldm(4)` kept a right angle, the missing-angle and missing-lattice-parameter notes, and the 1 Å Bravais cell; `-ndec 6`, an overflowed field, a whole-number row, a velocity row and the reference `si.gro`; `sphere`/`dipole`/`molecular`/`charge`/`full` row layouts with and without their header, a title line beginning with `Atoms`, a dump whose first block is short, a dump without a type column; a second `ATOMIC_POSITIONS` section with and without its mode, and a cut-off log's last table) and `.workbuddy/tools/verify_steps_frames.js` (**21 checks**: frames, labels, per-frame cells, the template's elements and bonds, sampling, a frame with the wrong atom count, a molecular run without a cell), plus `verify_parser_hardening` 89, `verify_parser_defects` 87, `verify_reader_fixes` 123, `verify_reader_records` 93, `verify_trajectory_companions` 10, `probe_smoke_parse` (79 files, 38 380 atoms, no failures) and the reference files themselves: `abacus/05_md_dump_si` opens with three frames, `qe/03_pw_vc_relax_nacl` stays one crystal (its two blocks state the same geometry), and the LAMMPS, GROMACS and CP2K examples still read the atoms and cells they did.

- **2026-11-16 — Sixteen record-level reader defects: an alternate conformation drawn twice, an NMR ensemble folded into one model, a POSCAR counts line read as an atom, and a cube grid the header contradicted** — Every one of them is a record whose *scope* was misread: a line taken as an atom, an atom taken as a whole site, a site taken as the structure. The structure that opened therefore held atoms — or bonds, or elements — the file never described.

  **A PDB atom is decided by the whole file, not by its own line** — `src/parsers/pdbParser.ts` read one `ATOM`/`HETATM` record at a time and pushed it, so a residue refined in two conformations appeared twice, at 60 % and 40 % occupancy — both drawn, both bonded, and the formula and the electron count doubled. The records are now collected first and reduced afterwards: a group is the format's own identity for an alternate conformation (chain, residue number, insertion code and atom name; the residue *name* is deliberately left out, so alternate residues collapse too), the surviving conformer is the one with the highest occupancy, a tie goes to the blank altLoc and then to the file order, and the records and sites dropped are one warning. A file with no `altLoc` column at all — every atom blank — takes the same path and loses nothing.

  **An ensemble is not a structure** — `MODEL`/`ENDMDL` were skipped, so every model's atoms were pushed and the serials were deduplicated against each other: a 20-model NMR deposition came back as its first model (with a duplicate-serial warning that blamed the wrong thing) or, when the serials were trimmed differently, as a mixture of models. `MODEL` now counts, every atom after the first model's `ENDMDL` is skipped, and one warning states how many models the file holds and how many atom records were left out.

  **A restarted serial is not a duplicate** — the reader kept one index per serial and dropped every later atom with the same number, which is exactly what a writer produces when it restarts numbering per chain, per model, or by concatenating two files — so the atoms after the restart disappeared without a trace. A repeated serial is a duplicate now only when the record beside it is the same atom (same name, element, residue and position within 0.001 Å); a repeated serial naming a *different* atom keeps both, and `CONECT` bonds the first of each. A `TER` record is the chain break and carries no atom, which is why the numbering restart beside it is now legal rather than destructive.

  **`SSBOND` states a bond** — the record was in the reader's skip list, so a disulfide the file declares was left to distance perception alone and lost whenever the two sulfurs sat outside the covalent cutoff. Both cysteine residues are resolved to their SG atoms and the bond is carried as a *stated* bond (`MolecularData.statedBonds`, a new field): `ensureBonds` merges it over the perceived list instead of replacing it — stating it as `hasExplicitBonds` would have claimed the file lists every bond, and a protein would have opened with two. A record whose partner is a symmetry image is reported and left out, because the page's molecule graph has no cell to place that image in. The symmetry operators are read as the tokens before the S–S distance rather than from columns 44-46 and 51-53: the blocks in the wild — the format's own printed example included — do not line up with those columns, and a misread column silently turned a real disulfide into a "symmetry bond" that was then thrown away.

  **`CRYST1`'s trailing Z is not the symbol's last number** — when a collapsed line forced the token reading, the last bare integer was removed as Z unconditionally, so `P 21 21 21` without a Z came back as `P 21 21` — a name no table knows, with the setting the reader needs gone. The built-in table now settles it: a trailing token is read as Z only when removing it leaves a symbol the table knows and the untrimmed line is not one. `P 21 21 21`, `P 21 21 21 4` and `P 1 21/c 1 4` all read back to their own symbol.

  **A POSCAR counts line is not a coordinate row** — when the counts line did not parse, `src/parsers/vaspParser.ts` kept the symbols, then discarded them and fell back to "read every remaining line as a site, all `X`" — starting *on the counts line itself*, so a bare `1 1` became an atom at (1, 1, …) and every real site became a dummy. A counts line is now recognised by what it is — a list of bare integers with at least one site — and it is consumed even when its length disagrees with the species count: fewer numbers pad the missing species with zero sites, more numbers extend the species list with the dummy `X`, and both are reported. A line with decimals is a coordinate row and is left where it is.

  **A vacancy is a site** — occupancy was accepted only in `(0, 1]`, so a POSCAR site written with occupancy 0 (a vacancy the file still lists) was promoted to a full atom. Zero is now read as the value it is, in both spellings (after the coordinates, and after the selective-dynamics flags).

  **One POSCAR, one structure — and it says so** — a file that concatenates several structures (a CONTCAR history, a hand-assembled relaxation) was read as its first and said nothing, which looks exactly like a file that held one. The reader now counts the coordinate-mode lines after the first frame and reports `the file holds N structures (a POSCAR states one): the first is shown`; the frame's velocity block is stepped over first, since it carries a mode line of its own and is not a frame.

  **A cube grid with a zero count is not a cell** — the three voxel counts were used without a check, so a grid line written `0 …` (VESTA leaves one behind on a grid it never filled) multiplied its vector away and produced a cell with no volume at all, in which every fractional coordinate is undefined. The counts must now be three positive whole numbers *and* the resulting vectors must span a volume (`isUsableLattice`, the check every other reader uses); otherwise the cell is not built from the grid, the reason is reported, and the atom bounding box is used.

  **The cube header states its own unit** — a size heuristic overrode the `NATOMS` sign (a step vector longer than 25 was taken as Ångström), which read a *coarse* Bohr grid as Ångström — 30 Bohr between points is ordinary for a large cell — and shrank the whole structure by 0.529 without a word. The sign is the file's own statement and is now the only thing consulted.

  **An atom row may be wider than five fields** — `parts.length === 5` dropped any row with a trailing label or comment column, and the loop then treated the rest of the atom table as grid data: the atoms after it vanished and the file was reported as truncated. The first five fields are read and the rest ignored.

  **A label names an element by how its column is written** — `src/parsers/cifParser.ts` read every all-capitals two-letter label by its first letter (the PDB atom-name rule that keeps a protein's `CA` a carbon), so an inorganic CIF with no type column turned `CU1` into carbon, `NA1` into nitrogen and `CO1` into carbon — three metals drawn as organics, with no bonds and no valence. The label column's own shape now decides: a column that numbers its sites (`CU1 O1 O2 C1 H1`, at least 80 % of the labels letters-then-digit) is naming elements, while a column of PDB atom names (`N CA C O CB`, mostly without a serial) keeps the old reading. The file's type column still wins over both, and `O1W` is unchanged.

  **A quoted value ends at a quote and a blank** — the tokenizer ended it at the first occurrence of the quote, so `'O'Brien'` became the value `O` and `Brien'` was re-read as further values, shifting every column of that row and dropping the atom. The value now ends where CIF says it does — at the quote followed by a blank or by the end of the line.

  **A loop can be one line** — a value on a key's own line was always taken as the `_key value` spelling and the loop was abandoned, so a legal `loop_ _a _b 1 2` (and the single-line form of any table) was discarded, while `loop_` followed by `_a 1` / `_b 2` items still needs that recovery. Which one it is, is only knowable after the body has been read: the loop is abandoned only when no record ever completed, which is exactly what the item spelling does. Both spellings now read.

  **An occupancy the file does not state is not full** — `_atom_site_occupancy` written `?` or `.` became 1, so a structure whose occupancies were never refined claimed every site was fully occupied; and a site the file writes with occupancy 0 was kept, adding an atom — and its scattering — to a picture the file says is empty. A missing occupancy is left undefined and a zero-occupancy site is left out, with the count reported.

  **A CIF with `_geom_bond` says it has bonds** — `hasExplicitBonds` was hard-coded false, so the one thing the file states most directly was not reported to anything downstream. It is now `true` when the bond table produced a bond, exactly as a PDB's `CONECT` records mark their file.

  **`GROUP` is checked, and the operations say where they came from** — `src/parsers/vestaParser.ts` took whatever followed the first two fields as a space group, so a damaged or truncated row became a group string that named no group; and `symmetrySource` was never set, so a save-back or a diff could not tell where the cell's operations came from. A name is now kept only when it has the shape of a Hermann-Mauguin symbol, its number is compared with the table's own and its operation count with the file's, and the provenance is recorded as `'file'`.

  **A merge between two sites is not a special position** — both cases landed on "an image was merged into an existing atom", which is the wording for symmetry doing its job. The reader now tracks which site placed each atom, exactly as the CIF reader does: an image of the *same* site landing on it is silent (a special position), an image of *another* site is reported with both labels and the occupancy the merge cost.

  Verified with the new `.workbuddy/tools/verify_reader_records.js` (**93 checks**: two conformers of one site keeping the 60 % one, a blank altLoc winning a tie, a 3-model ensemble read as model 1, a serial restarted across `TER` keeping both atoms, an exact duplicate record dropped, `SSBOND` merged by `ensureBonds` and cleared from the payload, a symmetry-related `SSBOND` left out, three `CRYST1` token readings, a zero and a negative voxel count refused, a coarse Bohr grid staying Bohr, a six-field atom row, a 3-for-2 counts line, a missing counts line, occupancy 0 in both POSCAR spellings, a two-structure POSCAR and a velocity block that is not one, `CU1`/`NA1`/`CO1` against a protein label column and against a type column, a quoted `O'Brien`, a single-line loop beside the item spelling, `?`/`0` occupancy, `_geom_bond`, the `GROUP` name and its number, a cross-site merge, and the stated-bond merge including a duplicate and a ghost), plus the existing suites — `verify_reader_fixes` 123, `verify_cif` 276, `verify_vesta` 66, `verify_parser_hardening` 89, `verify_parser_defects` 87, `verify_crystal_bonds` 44, `verify_symmetry_check` 127, `verify_space_group` 173, `verify_crystal` 254 (its one long-standing page-source assertion still fails, unchanged), a smoke parse of all 79 structure files under `test/` (38 380 atoms) with no throw, and a clean `npx tsc -p ./`.

- **2026-11-16 — Sixteen reader defects, each one a value taken from the wrong place: a MOPAC geometry scaled to 0.529, a Z-matrix drawn as an invented molecule, ORCA jobs mixed into one another, and a charge table that belonged to another structure** — Every one of them is a quantity read at the wrong moment or in the wrong column, so the structure that opened was quietly not the structure in the file.

  **MOPAC's optimisation flag was read as a unit** — `src/parsers/mopacParser.ts` treated the integer after each coordinate (`C -4.1918 0 2.5617 1 1.3518 0`) as a unit marker: a `0` was taken for Bohr and scaled that coordinate by 0.529177249, a `1` for Ångström. That number is MOPAC's *optimise / hold fixed* switch, so a molecule whose coordinates were all frozen opened at 0.529 of its size, and a mixed flag set mixed two units inside one structure — no shape survived either. A bare `C x y z` row (legal input, no flags) was dropped outright, because the reader looked for the coordinates in the three odd columns of the flagged form. Both shapes are now read positionally, the flags are flags, and a MOPAC geometry block is Å. A Z-matrix (internal-coordinate) input still comes back as an explained empty structure, and the output reader — whose `CARTESIAN COORDINATES` rows carry a serial number and state their unit in the header — is untouched.

  **A Z-matrix opened as an invented Cartesian structure** — `src/parsers/gjfParser.ts` handed every atom-block line to the Cartesian reader, which read a Z-matrix row's reference atoms and angles as coordinates: `C 1 1.50 2 109.5` became an atom at (1.50, 2, 109.5), and a block of them a molecule that exists nowhere. A row is now recognised as a Z-matrix row before the Cartesian reader sees it — the label is followed by `(reference, value)` pairs, the references are 1-based integers that point at an atom already defined, and a value is a number or a variable such as `R1` — and when the block is one, no atom is drawn and the panel says the internal coordinates are not converted. The backwards-pointing reference is also what keeps an integer Cartesian row with fixed-atom markers (`C 1 2 3 1 1 1`) out of the Z-matrix branch.

  **One charge for a multi-fragment input** — A Gaussian input states one charge/multiplicity pair per fragment (`0 1 0 1`), and only the first pair was kept: bond-order perception is given the system's total charge, so every multi-fragment file was solved as the first fragment's ion and could come out with the wrong bond orders and formal charges. The charges are summed into the system's own; the first multiplicity is kept, because per-fragment multiplicities do not add up to one number. The charge line is also read in the comma spelling Gaussian accepts and several writers emit (`1,2`) — ten of the bundled Gaussian inputs state their charge that way and none of them had it read.

  **A blank line after Link 0 no longer makes the route the title** — A file written as `%chk=…`, `%mem=…`, blank, `#p …`, blank, title read the route card as its title (the header loop ended at the first blank), which left the title wrong, the charge line unread and the first atom-row check starting on the wrong line. Link 0 lines, the route section and the title are now recognised by their own markers wherever the blank lines fall, and a wrapped route card is still collected whole.

  **ORCA's job boundary is a boundary for everything, not only the charge** — `src/parsers/orcaOutParser.ts` reset only `charge`/`multiplicity` when the echoed input started a second job, so the second job's steps continued the first job's numbering, its first step carried the first job's `FINAL SINGLE POINT ENERGY`, its first geometry could be labelled with the first job's last cycle (the pending banner was never cleared) and the Route panel showed both jobs' keywords concatenated into one card. Each job now owns its charge, multiplicity, step series, convergence thresholds and route section; the banner that names a geometry is consumed by the one block it names; an energy is bound only to the frames of the job that printed it; and the chart draws the last job that actually printed a convergence table, with each frame linked to the step whose geometry it is. `dropOddSizedFrames` — the guard that drops a half-written coordinate block — compared every frame of the file against one majority atom count, which deleted a whole job of a compound run whose molecule had a different size; it now decides per job.

  **A frequency set belongs to the geometry it was printed for** — ORCA's displacement matrix was grouped into per-atom vectors with the atom count of the file's LAST frame, so a frequency job followed by any other job produced modes with no vectors at all; the count is now taken when the `VIBRATIONAL FREQUENCIES` block is met, from the geometry above it. `src/parsers/logParser.ts`'s Gaussian sections are checked the same way in the other direction: a mode whose displacement list covers fewer atoms than the geometry it was printed for cannot be animated on any structure, so that section is not attached — a later truncated table no longer replaces a complete one.

  **A `--Link1--` file keeps every job's charge tables** — `src/parsers/logParser.ts` cleared the charge drafts on every route card, so only the last job's population analyses survived even though the earlier tables still named frames on the list. Tables are now kept per job (the last table of a scheme *within* a job still wins, which is what an optimisation that prints one per step needs), the job the file ends on comes first so the panel still opens on the structure on screen, and a scheme both jobs printed says which job it belongs to (`Mulliken charges (job 2)`) instead of appearing twice under one name.

  **A broken-symmetry UHF/UKS singlet is not a closed shell** — `src/parsers/fchkParser.ts` decided "closed shell" from `nAlpha === nBeta`, multiplicity 1 and `IROHF = 0`, which every broken-symmetry singlet also satisfies: its beta orbitals were dropped and two electrons forced into every alpha level. The checkpoint states the spin kind by storing a second set of MO coefficients (and its own orbital energies), so the presence of that channel decides now; such a file keeps both channels with one electron per level. ROHF's beta occupations were also counted twice — the paired beta spin orbitals now hold one electron each, since the alpha row already states the pair.

  **`%coords` states its own charge** — An ORCA `%coords` block carries `Charge` and `Mult` keywords, which fell through as unreadable lines: a charged or open-shell `%coords` input opened as the default neutral singlet and bond perception solved the wrong system. Both keywords are read (as are `CTyp`/`coords`, which are structure of the block, not atoms).

  **A SYBYL type is not an element** — `src/parsers/mol2Parser.ts` took the MOL2 type column verbatim, so `LP` (a lone pair), `Any`, `Het`, `Hal` and a bare `X` became elements the viewer drew and valence-solved as atoms. The column is a SYBYL type: only a real symbol (from `C.3`, `N.pl3`, `Cl`) becomes an element, `Du` stays the dummy point it already was, and a pseudo-atom is left out of the structure with the file's own defect reported.

  **A file nothing recognises says so** — `tryAutoParse`'s last resort returned an empty XYZ structure with no warning, which reads exactly like a file that was read and found to hold no atoms. It now reports that no coordinate table, trajectory frame or supported program output was found.

  Verified with the new `.workbuddy/tools/verify_parser_defects.js` (**87 checks**: the MOPAC flag/unit and bare-row cases, the Z-matrix block and the integer-Cartesian counter-case, the multi-fragment and comma charge lines, the Link 0 blank line, a two-job ORCA run with different molecule sizes, per-job steps/energies/labels/routes/links, a frequency set kept for its own geometry, `--Link1--` charge tables on two frames, a synthetic broken-symmetry UHF singlet / RHF / ROHF checkpoint, `%coords` Charge/Mult, the MOL2 pseudo-atoms, and the unreadable-file warning), plus the existing suites — `verify_orca` 173, `verify_log_charges` 231, `verify_log_optsteps` 42, `verify_log_optstep_link` 31, `verify_log_energy` 23, `verify_parser_hardening` 89, `verify_reader_fixes` 123, `verify_fchk_edges` 21 — a smoke parse of all 139 readable files in `test/` with no throw, and a clean `npx tsc -p ./`.

- **2026-11-13 — Charge tables, scan rows and IRC labels now name the frame they belong to: nine charge tables landed on the wrong geometry, the scan list went dark on intermediate steps, and path frames carried labels that disagreed with the card** — Four frame-bound readings were misplaced or lost their link.

  **Charge tables** — `ChargeDraft.anchor` is a PRE-FILTER frame index: the table was recorded while every `Input orientation` block was still on the frame list. The final assembly clamped it to the POST-filter length first and only then applied `frameRemap`, so an anchor beyond the shorter list landed on an index the filter had renamed, backed up to a mid-file `Standard orientation` frame, and labelled the table with that geometry. The anchor is now clamped to the pre-filter count (`frameRemap ? frameRemap.length : preDropFrameCount`), mapped with `remapFrameIndex`, and only then clamped to the final list (whose trailing repeat a scan or IRC prints after its summary may have been dropped). Measured on `test/Nazarov/`: the dihedral scan's last Mulliken table is linked to frame index **78** (was 39), the distance scan's to **106** (was 53), and the six optimisation logs read cyclic_product **7** (was 4), final_product **6** (3), reactant_optimized **17** (9), transition_state **4** (2), ts_in_rotation **4** (2) and rotated_intermediate **6** (3). The APT pair a scan prints at the input pose still names that frame.

  **The scan list follows the frame on screen** — The charts' ring followed the current frame's `scanPoint`, but a list row was marked only when the frame index equalled the point's converged frame. On an intermediate step — the 79th structure is point 27's second step while point 27 converged on the 82nd — the ring was lit on point 27 and every row was dark. Each scan row now carries `data-point`, and `syncPathwayRows` lights the row whose point is the current frame's `scanPoint` when the frame itself is no row's frame; an IRC row keeps the frame match it always had.

  **Displacement on the scan card** — The README promised max/RMS displacement on the Scan card, but the card drew only energy and force although the parser and the page already carried the numbers. The card now draws a third chart (`Displacement of the last step (max + rms)`, log axis, threshold lines from the file's convergence targets) and the list gains a `max D` column beside `max F`.

  **The scan x axis is the file's coordinate** — Scan charts labelled their ticks with the point ordinal. `pwPointTick` now returns the `coord` value the parameter table printed (`3.0978 … 1.5978 Å`, or degrees) when a point states one, and the chart paints the coordinate's name at the right of the title line (`R(1,6)`) beside the list header's unit. An IRC chart keeps its row-name ticks (`R24`, `TS`, `F24`) and paints no coordinate title; an ordinary optimisation is unchanged.

  **An interrupted scan point keeps its printed geometry** — A scan header created its point with `frame = -1` and the frame was bound only when the Berny table arrived. A run cut off between the header and its table therefore dropped the geometry printed above the header even though the file held it. The `Step number … on scan point` branch now binds the frame immediately (`frame`, `scanPoint`, `scanStep`, `optStep = undefined`, the `Point N · step M` label), so the truncated point still has its structure; the table-time binding that follows remains the authority for completed points.

  **The opening input pose belongs to the first scan point** — The pose a scan starts from is printed before point 1's geometry and no table ever names it, so opening a scan file showed a frame no row described: no row highlighted and no ring on the first chart. The first point's header now marks the job's input frame with `scanPoint = point` (its `Input` label is kept as the file wrote it), so the point-1 row is lit and the ring sits on point 1 from the first frame.

  **IRC frames are named like the card** — A path point's frame kept whatever label its printed block had: the transition state, printed above the path banner with no point header, read `Input (Charge=1 Mult=1)` while the card called the same structure `TS`, and every other point read `Path N · point M` while the rows read `R24`/`F0`. `labelIrcFrames`, run after `orderIrcFrames`, rewrites each claimed frame's `stepLabel` and `title` from the path and run links: `TS`, `R12`, `F3`, prefixed with `Run N · ` only when a `--Link1--` file holds more than one IRC calculation, with the `(Charge=… Mult=…)` suffix kept. Frame order, energies and the charge tables are untouched.

  Verified with `verify_scan_irc.js` (**724 assertions**: the relabelled IRC frames and runs, the two-job and one-sided labels, the truncated-scan binding, the scan input frame, the coordinate ticks and the orbital frame links), `verify_log_charges.js` 180 (the nine corrected assignments among them), `verify_log_optsteps` 42, `verify_log_optstep_link` 31, `verify_parser_hardening` 89, `verify_orca` 173, `verify_fchk_edges` 21, `verify_load_progress`, the client syntax check and `npm run compile`; and the new headless-Edge `.workbuddy/tools/verify_pathway_page.js` (**48 checks**) renders the shipping page for the distance scan and the IRC and checks the three scan charts, the coordinate ticks and axis title, the `max D` column, the point-1 row on the opening frame, the point-27 row on an intermediate step, and the joined `R24 … TS … F24` IRC list and ticks.

- **2026-11-13 — An IRC opened on its transition state, not on the leftmost structure, so the frame arrows and Auto Play began in the middle of the reaction path** — The frame list of an IRC file followed the order Gaussian printed its geometries: the transition state first, then the forward half from point 0 outwards, then the reverse half from point 0 outwards. Frame 1 — the structure the page opens on, and the frame Auto Play leaves first — was therefore the barrier, and playback walked barrier → forward far end → reverse far end instead of along the reaction coordinate. The joined **IRC / Path** chart and its rows already read reverse far end → TS → forward far end; the frame list was the one place still in print order.

  `src/parsers/logParser.ts` now orders an IRC calculation's frames along its path, in `orderIrcFrames`, called once every frame-bound record has been built: the reverse half descending to its point 0, the transition state, then the forward half ascending from point 0. Frame 1 is the leftmost structure, the initial structure the host draws is that same point, and ◀/▶ and Auto Play run the path from one end to the other. Each reference to a frame moves with it — every path point's frame, each run's `tsFrame`, scan points and charge tables — so a row, a chart ring, a charge table or a saved frame still names the geometry it named before. A one-sided run keeps the barrier at the end its half belongs to: a forward-only run reads `TS, F0 … F24` (the barrier is its leftmost structure) and a reverse-only run reads `R24 … R0, TS`. A `--Link1--` file's runs are ordered independently, each placed where its own earliest frame stood, and a frame no run claims keeps its place. The move is all-or-nothing: if the run sequences do not partition the frame list exactly, the file order is left untouched.

  `media/viewer.js` builds a one-sided run's row list by the same rule, so a reverse-only file's rows now read `R24 … R0`, `TS` — the barrier last — and the row highlighted on opening is the first row.

  Counted on `test/Nazarov/irc.log` (51 frames): frame 1 is `Path 2 · point 24` (energy −269.651555296), frame 25 is reverse point 0, frame 26 the transition state, frame 27 forward point 0 and frame 51 forward point 24. Verified with `.workbuddy/tools/verify_scan_irc.js` (**691 assertions**: the new order on the single IRC and on a `--Link1--` file whose second run also starts at its own reverse far end, the forward-only and reverse-only synthetics, and the client's one-sided row order), plus `verify_log_optstep_link` 31, `verify_log_optsteps` 42, `verify_log_energy` 23, `verify_log_charges` 180, `verify_parser_hardening` 89, `verify_reader_fixes` 123, `verify_orca` 173, `verify_trajectory_companions` 10 and the client syntax check; and a headless Edge render of the IRC page opens on frame `1/51 Path 2 · point 24` with the R24 row highlighted and the ring on the chart's first point.

- **2026-11-12 — The convergence guide marked the first point only: a log that prints the input orientation as a frame of its own has one frame more than it has steps** — In the **Convergence** charts the ring and its vertical guide sat on the first point and nowhere else: stepping to any later structure left all three charts unmarked. The charts looked the step up by frame index — "frame k is step k" — which holds only for a log whose first geometry is a `Standard orientation` block. A job that starts from coordinates also prints an `Input orientation` block for that same geometry, and the frame filter keeps it as frame 0, so the list reads input pose, standard pose, … and from frame 1 on, frame k carries step k's energy while the guard compared it with step k+1's and refused to mark anything. Every one of the nine `test/Nazarov/` logs has this shape, so none of them ever moved the guide.

  `src/parsers/logParser.ts` now states the link instead of leaving it to be inferred: a frame of an optimizing job carries `optStep`, the step whose geometry it is — the input pose and the first standard pose are both step 1, every later frame one step on, and nothing at all for a frame no optimizer produced (an IRC path point, the copy an `opt freq` run hands to its `Freq` job). `src/webview/page.ts` forwards it and `convSelectedStep` in `media/viewer.js` follows it, keeping the energy guard that a step is marked only on a geometry printed with its own SCF energy; a structure the optimizer printed without a table of its own — the converged geometry at the end of a job, or the `Freq` job's copy of it — is still that step's structure, so it is matched by the single-point energy both were printed with. A payload that carries no link (ORCA, xTB, a trajectory, a checkpoint) keeps the old index identity, so nothing outside a Gaussian opt log changes.

  Counted on the reported file (18 frames, 15 steps): the two poses of the first geometry mark step 1, frames 3–16 mark steps 2–15, the last frame marks step 15, and only the one frame whose geometry has no SCF of its own stays unmarked — it is the converged structure repeated after the run, and marking a step with another energy is the one thing the guide must not do. Frame counts, labels, energies and the step series themselves are untouched. Verified with `.workbuddy/tools/verify_log_optstep_link.js` (**31 checks**: the link on two logs, the index identity kept on a log without an input-orientation block, the un-tabled last geometry of a cut-short run left unmarked, the client marking every frame of three real logs with no marked step carrying another energy, the guide never walking backwards, a payload without the link, a plateau of equal energies, and the host → page → client chain), plus `verify_log_optsteps` 42, `verify_log_energy` 23, `verify_log_charges` 180, `verify_parser_hardening` 89, `verify_orca` 173, `verify_reader_fixes` 123 and the client syntax check.

- **2026-11-12 — A Gaussian route card written without a print level is not read, so an optimisation's convergence series was dropped — and the `Freq` job of an `opt freq` run had its non-optimized table counted as a step** — Every optimisation in `test/Nazarov/` (Gaussian 16 on Windows) opened with no **Convergence** panel at all, although each log prints a full Berny run: 15 steps of energy, max/RMS force and max/RMS displacement for the reactant, 2 for the transition state, 5, 4 and 4 for the products and the rotated intermediate, 50 / 78 / 106 for the three relaxed scans. The cause was the card's shape test: `ROUTE_START_RE` accepted `#`, `#p`, `#N`, `#t` — a `#` followed by a single letter — but these files are written `#B3LYP/6-31G(d) opt freq`, with no print level and the method directly after the `#`. `B3LYP` starts with a letter followed by a digit, so the `[A-Za-z]\b` alternative had no word boundary to match and the card was invisible; the only card the reader found was the second job's `#N Geom=AllCheck … Freq`, which is why the **Route** panel offered a `Freq` card and an `opt` badge nowhere. Because the series was gated on "some route section in this file requests an optimization", every table in the file was then discarded — the parse was right about the tables and wrong about the file.

  **The shape test now admits the spelling Gaussian actually prints** — a `#` followed directly by the first keyword when that keyword carries a method/basis pair (a `/`, which every printed card has). Since that shape alone would also admit an IRC banner's `# OF POINTS ALONG THE PATH = 1`, a line is accepted as a card when it sits in Gaussian's dash box, and an unboxed one only when it states a method/basis pair or is closed by a rule. The IRC in the same folder used to open with **50** route sections — one per path point, each rendering a "Job n" block in the Route panel — and a **Summary** card listing 51 jobs; it now reads as the one job the file ran, with its own `irc=(forward,reverse,LQA,calcfc,maxpoints=25,…)` card.

  **The gate belongs to the job, not to the file** — `jobHasOpt` now follows the card of the job being read, so a job that never asks for an optimization contributes no step. This is not a refinement: the `Freq` job every `opt freq` run prints as a second job repeats the same four-row table once at the geometry it was handed, under `! Non-Optimized Parameters !`, and its forces are ~1e-6 — with the card read correctly, that table would have become a 16th point of a 15-step optimisation and drawn a drop to zero the run never made. A job that stated no card at all (a log whose head is gone) cannot be classified, so its tables are still taken: a truncated optimisation keeps its curve, and the `Freq` table after the cut-off card still does not count.

  **What did not change** — the geometry frames, titles, harmonic frequencies, charge tables and job summaries of every other bundled log are byte-identical, and the four existing `opt freq` logs lose exactly the `Freq` job's table and nothing else (steps 78 → 77, 22 → 21, 81 → 80, 16 → 15; in each the dropped point is the one printed after `Geom=AllCheck`, and in `tetramethylammonium.log` it is the table under `! Non-Optimized Parameters !`). Verified with `.workbuddy/tools/verify_log_optsteps.js` (**42 checks**: both cards of the reported file, its 16 tables against its 15 steps, the dropped one placed after the `Freq` card, step energies against the frames they were printed at, a synthetic `opt freq` whose `Freq` table must not count, a `freq`-only job that must yield no series, a headless fragment that must still yield one, the IRC banner, and the boxed / lost-opening-rule shapes that already worked) and `mutate_log_optsteps.js`, a four-row ladder on copies of the built reader: the original logic yields **0** steps (the report), the old shape with the new gate yields 15 steps but no `opt` card, the new shape with no per-job gate yields **16**, and the build yields 15. `verify_log_energy` 23, `verify_log_charges` 180, `verify_parser_hardening` 89, `verify_orca` 173 and `verify_reader_fixes` 123 stay green.

- **2026-11-12 — Five reader and symmetry defects: a PDB's unit cell discarded, an origin choice mistaken for a contradiction, five e-glide names missing, a sodium read as nitrogen, and a QE attribute QE never reads**

  **A PDB's `CRYST1` is its unit cell** — `src/parsers/pdbParser.ts` skipped the record with the metadata, so a file stating `CRYST1 5.640 5.640 20.000 …` was read as 16 loose ions while the same structure as a `.gro` or a `.cif` had periodic bonds, supercells, the cell editor and XRD. The six parameters are read from the record's fixed columns (a/b/c as 9.3 in 7-15 / 16-24 / 25-33, the angles as 7.2 in 34-54, the Hermann-Mauguin symbol in 56-66), with the numbers re-read as tokens whenever a column slice disagrees with them — a writer that collapsed the padding otherwise shifts `b` into the next field — and the cell is built through `cellToLatticeVectors`, the construction CIF and GRO already share. The operations stay the identity: a PDB model is the coordinates the depositor wrote (for a protein, the asymmetric unit), and expanding it under the stated group would multiply every molecule in the file; the group is kept for the cell panel instead. `CONECT` still states the connectivity and, in a file that has a cell, now travels as `crystal.fileBonds` — the field a CIF's `_geom_bond` uses — so it is merged over the perceived cell bonds exactly as a CIF's table is, while a PDB without a cell keeps its authored-bond path unchanged. Two records are refused rather than trusted: a cell with a zero axis or an angle outside 0-180, and the **1 Å placeholder** OpenBabel and several converters write for a structure that has no cell, which taken at face value folds every atom into a one-Ångström box. Both say so and leave the structure a molecule.

  **An unspecified origin choice is a family, not a contradiction** — `src/tools/spaceGroupTable.ts` answers a name without a `:1`/`:2` suffix with origin choice 2, the setting the International Tables recommend, and `src/parsers/cifParser.ts` compared a file's own operation list against that one choice. A correctly written `P n n n` file listing origin choice 1's eight operations was therefore reported as contradicting its own name — "8 operations expand the sites to 8 atoms, but its own 8 operations expand them to 8 atoms" — and the switch it offered applied origin choice 2 to coordinates written for origin 1, replacing the cell with a different structure eight positions at a time. The table now exposes every setting a name leaves open (`lookupSpaceGroupSettings`, the preferred one first, the rest in International Tables order) and the check reports a disagreement only when no member of that family matches; when one does, the file and its name agree and nothing is reported at all. A genuine contradiction is still reported and still offers the switch, but only when the name pins the setting (`P n n n:2` against origin-1 operations, `P 21/c` against a single identity): a name that leaves the origin or the axis triple open gives those coordinates no setting to be re-expanded under, so the report stands without a rewrite and the warning says which case it is.

  **Five groups answer to their 2002 e-glide names** — The table carries the IT Vol. B (2001) spellings, so `A b m 2`, `A b a 2`, `C m c a`, `C m m a` and `C c c a` were known and `A e m 2` (No. 39), `A e a 2` (41), `C m c e` (64), `C m m e` (67) and `C c c e` (68) were not: a name-only CIF in `C m c e` was read as P1 — one atom, "not in the built-in space-group table" — while the same structure written `C m c a` came out as its 16-atom cell, and a file that listed its operations was expanded correctly but could not take part in the consistency check at all. An `e` glide is what a `b` and a `c` glide become together in an A- or C-centred cell, so each of the five is the same group's newer spelling and is indexed onto the same row, operations and origin choices included.

  **`SOD NA` is sodium, not nitrogen** — `src/parsers/gromacsParser.ts` reads a `.gro` atom name through the protein rules unless the residue name is one or two letters that spell the same element, which is what keeps a C-alpha a carbon. CHARMM's ion residues are three letters (`SOD`, `CLA`, `CAL`, `POT`, `CES`, `BAR`, `IOD`) and its sodium's atom name is `NA`, so the shortcut could not fire and `NA` matched the amino-group pattern: a sodium was drawn, coloured, weighed and bonded as nitrogen. A small ion-residue table is consulted first now, and only when the atom name agrees with it — it repeats the residue (`CAL CAL`) or spells the ion's element (`SOD NA`, `CLA CL`) — so a residue that merely happens to carry that name and a carbon is left to the ordinary rules.

  **QE does not read `(alat= …)`, and neither does the reader** — The card header `CELL_PARAMETERS (alat= 5.43)` states a unit, not a scale: `card_cell_parameters` and `card_atomic_positions` in QE's `Modules/read_cards.f90` match `ANGSTROM`, `BOHR`, `ALAT` and `CRYSTAL` as substrings and never parse the number beside them, so QE scales the card by `&SYSTEM`'s `celldm(1)` (Bohr) or `alat`/`A` (Å). `src/parsers/qeParser.ts` believed the number and overrode the system's parameter with it — identical whenever the file repeated the same value, and silently different for a file that did not, drawing a cell the program never used. The system's parameter now wins, as in QE; the number is used only when the file states no lattice parameter at all, which is the one case where it is the only scale there is (QE itself would stop on that input), and that leniency is stated in a warning. A value that disagrees with the scale actually used is reported with both numbers. `alat` in `&SYSTEM` — the same parameter as `A`, in Ångström — is read as a lattice parameter too, which is also what tells the two cases apart.

  Verified with `.workbuddy/tools/verify_reader_fixes.js` (**123 checks** on the compiled readers): the bundled NaCl slab reads its 16 atoms, the 5.64 × 5.64 × 20 Å cell, `P 1` and unchanged coordinates, through both `parsePdb` and the `.pdb` dispatch; a PDB without `CRYST1` is still a molecule; a collapsed record keeps a = 5.64, b = 5.64, c = 20; a hexagonal record keeps β = 120° and its 10 Å b axis; the 1 Å placeholder and a zero cell are refused with their own warnings; `CONECT` becomes a one-bond `fileBonds` table with order 2; `P n n n` is silent against either origin's eight operations, `P n n n:2` against origin 1 is reported with the switch, a third operation set is reported without one, and a consistent `P 21/c` file stays silent; the five e-glide names resolve to Nos. 39 / 41 / 64 / 67 / 68 with the operations of their old spellings, and a name-only `C m c e` file expands to 16 atoms; `SOD NA` is Na, `CLA CL` Cl and `CAL CAL` Ca while a protein `CA`/`HG1`, water `OW`, `SOD O` and `BAR C1` keep their own elements; and the six QE cases (a matching alat, `celldm(1)` winning, no system parameter, a `(bohr)` cell with `(alat= …)` positions, `&SYSTEM alat`, plain and `(angstrom)` cards). No regression: `verify_space_group` **173**, `verify_cif` **275**, `verify_symmetry_check` **127**, `verify_crystal` **254 of 255** (the known pre-split source-text check 18.29), `verify_crystal_bonds` **44**, `verify_vesta` **66**, `verify_parser_hardening` **89**, `verify_trajectory_companions` **10**, `verify_mm_switch` **22**, `verify_orca` **173**. `npm run compile` is clean and `dist/` was rebuilt.

- **2026-11-12 — Seven reader, symmetry and SMILES defects: an IR table cut at row 400, a cube drawn off its origin, reduced masses read as elements, and four slips in bond order and folding**

  **An ORCA IR table is read to its end, not to row 400** — `src/parsers/orcaOutParser.ts` stopped the intensity table after 400 rows (`guard++ < 400`). A molecule with more than 400 modes (3N−6, so above 136 atoms) therefore kept the frequency of its second half and lost its intensity: the IR spectrum described only part of the molecule. The cap was a guard against a runaway scan, but the table already has an end — the first line that is not a `n: freq eps Int …` row — so the loop now stops there and nowhere else. A 450-mode synthetic output keeps all 450 intensities (mode 455 included), rows printed below a non-table line are still refused, and `test/orca_test/h2o.out` answers as before.

  **A cube is drawn in its own frame** — `src/parsers/cubeParser.ts` read the header origin and never used it: the lattice was built from (0,0,0) while every atom kept its absolute coordinates, so a cube whose grid is a sub-box (a non-zero origin, as an ESP grid or a region cut from a larger grid) showed its cell one translation away from its atoms. The origin is now converted with the same Bohr/Å factor as the coordinates and subtracted from every atom, which puts them in the cell the viewer draws. A zero origin subtracts zero and leaves the file's numbers exactly as they were, and the lattice fallback for an unreadable voxel block is unaffected (the atoms are recentred there anyway).

  **`units lj` does not name elements** — The type names a LAMMPS input states were taken from `mass` alone, and `elementFromMass` (0.35 g/mol tolerance) reads the reduced mass 1.0 as hydrogen, so every type of a Lennard-Jones melt was drawn, coloured, weighed and bonded as H. The mass-derived names are now held back until the script's final `units` is known — the command may come after the `mass` lines — and with `units lj` they are not applied at all: the type keeps its `TypeN` label. The unit system in force is carried across an `include` chain (a secondary script need not repeat `units`), a `units` line in the file itself overrides the inherited one, and a `dump_modify … element` list wins over an inferred mass in either command order. The data file's `Masses` section takes the same decision in both readers (`lammpsTypeElements(content, units)` and `parseLammpsData`), and its warning now says the mass is reduced instead of claiming the type has no mass, while a comment tag (`mass 1 1.0 # Ar`) still names the element in any unit system.

  **A value just below 1 folds to 0, not back to 0.999999** — `wrapFrac` folded with `r -= 1` and then `r += 1`, so −1e−6 became 0.999999 again: the duplicate image the fold exists to remove survived, exactly on the next-cell boundary. The fold is one-way now (`r >= 1 − 1e−6 → 0`); 0.25 and −0.25 are unchanged.

  **A leading-dot translation is half a cell, not five** — `parseSymOp`'s number pattern (`\d+\.?\d*`) could not match `.5`, so the dot was skipped and the digit read as `5`: a CIF/VESTA operation `x+.5` generated atoms five cells away. The pattern accepts `\.\d+` as well, so `x+.5` is +0.5 while `x+5` is still five; `1/2`, `0.5` and negative translations are unchanged.

  **An explicit `-` between aromatic atoms stays single** — `markAromaticBonds` upgraded every single bond whose two ends were aromatic, including one the SMILES had written as `-`. Biphenyl `c1ccccc1-c1ccccc1` therefore put its inter-ring bond at 1.5 (1.39 Å, the aromatic length) and counted it among the aromatic bonds, which also fed the MM parameters and the exported order; the molecule's H count was right, its geometry was not. A bond now records whether its symbol was written (`SmilesBond.explicit`, set by `addBond` from `pendingSet`, and from either end of a ring closure), and only an unwritten bond between two aromatic atoms is upgraded. The biphenyl inter-ring bond is order 1 at 1.54 Å, 12 of its 13 bonds are aromatic and the formula stays C12H10; `c1ccccc1c1ccccc1` (no symbol) still reads aromatic, and a ring closed with `-` keeps that single bond.

  **`$` is a quadruple bond** — The bond-symbol branch listed `- = # :` and let `$` fall into the direction branch, which both set `SINGLE_BOND` and wrote `$` into `pendingDir`, so a Cr–Cr quadruple bond was silently a single bond that also looked cis/trans-specified. `$` now sets order 4 and no direction, and `BOND_ORDER_SCALE` carries the order-4 contraction (0.72): `[Cr]$[Cr]` is 2.00 Å rather than the 2.78 Å of a single bond or the 2.17 Å of a triple, and the order reaches the bond detail table as 4. `media/viewer.js` draws that order as four parallel lines instead of falling through to the single-stick branch, and formats that cannot express a quadruple bond write the nearest multiple bond, as they already did for any order above three.

  Verified with `.workbuddy/tools/verify_parser_hardening.js` (**89 checks**: a 450-row IR table read to its end with the row after 400 present, a non-table line still ending it, the real `h2o.out` unchanged, a non-zero cube origin in Å and in Bohr against a zero-origin control and the bundled `218142.cube`, `units lj` before and after the `mass` lines and inherited across an `include` chain, `dump_modify element` beating an inferred mass, the data-file `Masses` section and a whole data file in both unit systems (including the warning that names the reduced mass), `wrapFrac` at ±1e−6 and interior values, `x+.5` / `-x+1/2` / `x-0.25` / `x+5`, biphenyl's inter-ring bond and formula, the unwritten and ring-closure controls, and `[Cr]$[Cr]` against `[Cr][Cr]`). No regression: `verify_orca` **173**, `verify_crystal` **254 of 255** (the known pre-split source-text check 18.29), `verify_cif` **275**, `verify_crystal_bonds` **44**, `verify_vesta` **66**, `verify_symmetry_check` **127**, `verify_trajectory_companions` **10**, `verify_addh_orders` **32**, `verify_mm_hybrid` **22**.
