Three older harnesses that still read the pre-split page template (`verify_crystal_edit`, `verify_crystal_diff`, `verify_crystal_export`) fail at load exactly as before and are not counted. `npm run compile` is clean and `dist/` was rebuilt; the shipped client script still parses (`syntaxcheck_client.js`, **20 224 lines**).

- **2026-11-12 — Twelve reader and search defects: a title that was a rule, frames with no energy, labels read as metals, a discarded unit, and the two states a pause could not agree on**

  **A Gaussian title is the card after the route, not the rule above it** — `src/parsers/logParser.ts` took the last non-empty, non-`%` line before the first `#…` route line. In a real log that line is the rule that *opens* the route card, so `test/168791_1_opt1.log` answered with **51 hyphens** — and `MD.title` is what Save As writes as a GJF/XYZ title line and what a Diff tab is labelled with. The title is now read from the card the file echoes just before `Symbolic Z-matrix:` (the box between two rules, which may be empty and may span lines), with the older anchor kept only for a file that has no Z-matrix section and with rules skipped there too. The scan is bounded to the first 400 lines, so a 60 MB log no longer pays a full-text regex pass for it.

  **A standard optimisation keeps the energy of every frame** — The same file prints a cycle's geometry (`Standard orientation`) *before* the energy computed at it (`SCF Done`), and the convergence table clears the pending value immediately afterwards: reading `pendingEnergy` when the frame was created therefore produced **139 frames with `energy === undefined`** while all 138 steps had one, and the page's frame↔step cross-check (`media/viewer.js`, `frame.energy` against the curve) could never fire. The value now binds backwards to the frames printed since the previous energy (`assignPendingEnergyToFrames`, bounded to eight frames and to the current job). `freq`-job geometry echoes that have no SCF of their own keep no energy rather than inheriting the step above, and `opt=external` runs still bind their `Energy= … NIter=` line the same way.

  **A `--Link1--` job no longer inherits its predecessor's energy** — A route card resets the charge, charges, orbitals and cavity of the job it opens but left `pendingEnergy` alone, so a job that ended without a convergence table (a single point) handed its SCF energy to the next job's first geometry — wrong number and a wrong "Step k" label. The value is cleared at the card, and the energy binding is floored at the first frame of the job.

  **A CIF label with no type column is a PDB atom name, not an element** — `src/parsers/cifParser.ts` fell back to `normaliseElement(label)`, which capitalises the leading one or two letters, so a polymer CIF without `_atom_site_type_symbol` read `CA`, `CD` and `HG` as **calcium, cadmium and mercury**: a protein came out as three metals, with no bonds, no valence and every radius/colour/mass lookup missing. A two-letter label is now read as a PDB atom name (its first letter is the element: `CA` is C-α, `CD` a δ carbon, `HG` a γ hydrogen) unless a lowercase second letter spells a symbol outright (`Ca`, `Zn`, `Cl`), or the file's own type column declares the two-letter element and never the one-letter one — which keeps a partially typed `Ca`/`Cd`/`Hg` file correct. A first letter that is not an element (`Z` in `ZN1`) still falls back to the two-letter symbol.

  **A QE card's own `alat=` value is used** — `src/parsers/qeParser.ts` recognised only the unit *name* in `CELL_PARAMETERS (alat= 5.43)` and discarded the value, so the cell was read in units of the namelist's `alat` — 1 Å when the file states none — and a cubic `(alat= 5.43)` block came out **5.43 times too small**, with fractional coordinates of 0.1 read as 0.1 Å instead of 0.543 Å and periodic bonds, volume and every supercell wrong by the same factor. The attribute (QE reads it in Ångström) is now parsed and used by both `CELL_PARAMETERS` and `ATOMIC_POSITIONS`, in either the `(…)` or the `{…}` spelling.

  **An ORCA frame is labelled with its own job's charge** — `src/parsers/orcaOutParser.ts` updated one global charge/multiplicity per input echo and then appended the *final* `(Charge=… Mult=…)` suffix to every frame's `stepLabel`/`title`, so in a compound run the first job's frames were labelled with the last job's values while their own `f.charge` said otherwise. The suffix is now built from each frame's own values, a new echo (`|  1>`, the only mark of a job boundary) resets the values and the end-of-file fill-in can no longer reach past the current job's first frame.

  **One unknown dump type no longer empties the element table** — `src/webview/trajectoryDocuments.ts` filtered every `T1`/`T2` label out and then required the remainder to cover all atoms, so a single unnameable type dropped **all** elements and every atom was drawn as `Xx` — while the warning claimed the types were shown as labels. The labels are kept position by position, only the unnamed ones fall back to `Xx`, and the warning names the count and the types that did.

  **A trajectory's LAMMPS data file keeps the bonds it states, and its `atom_style`** — Two companion gaps. `parseLammpsData` puts the `Bonds` section into `crystal.fileBonds`, but the trajectory template handed only `topology.data.bonds` (empty) to `ensureBonds`, so the file's own connectivity was replaced by a distance-derived set: a non-standard bond, or one longer than perception's cutoff, was drawn short and used by MM as two unconnected atoms. The template now takes the declared table as its explicit bond list (without the per-bond image shift, which belongs to the data file's own geometry). The script's `atom_style` is collected beside `units` and the type names and passed on: a six- or nine-column `Atoms` section without a `# style` header is otherwise guessed as `charge`, which reads the **molecule id as the atom type** and maps every element through the wrong column.

  **1.5 is only assigned on a bond that lies on a ring** — `src/tools/bondPerception.ts` brackets 1.34–1.44 Å in the C–C table with the aromatic order *before* the double order, and the first band containing the distance won, so **every alkene in that window came out aromatic**: a four-substituted C=C at 1.40 Å was read as 1.5, which the valence refinement then refused to raise (raising it worsens the length fit), and 1.5 travelled into the MM parameters, the drawing and a MOL2 export that writes `ar` — a ring. Ring membership is the fact the distance cannot state, so a bridge search (iterative Tarjan, O(V+E), no recursion) now takes 1.5 off every bond that is not on a cycle and replaces it with the best non-aromatic order its length supports. A phenyl ring keeps all six 1.5 bonds, a C=C between two rings is a double bond, and the same rule covers C–N, N–O and C–S. The rule is applied when the graph is built and once more before the result is collected, so a re-planning pass cannot put it back.

  **A paused search and the geometry it reports are one geometry** — `src/tools/mmOptimizer.ts` keeps the lowest-energy geometry it accepted, and a pause reports `finish()` from it, while the live progress and the resume point used the transient working coordinates; a rebuilt pair list can make those differ, so pausing **jumped the atoms onto another geometry**, resuming jumped them back, and the energy quoted for the pause was not the one the search resumed from. `coords()` and `step()` now report the same point `finish()` answers with, and `pause()` adopts it into the working arrays (rebuilding the pair list, dropping the conjugate direction and re-measuring the forces) so the pause, the resume and the eventual result describe one structure.

  **The 1-3 clash term switches on at its edge** — A 1-3 pair was re-admitted as pure repulsion only when a rebuilt pair list happened to find it below 0.45 σ, and the energy then appeared at full strength: a pair caught just inside the edge injected ≈(1/0.45)¹²·ε ≈ **1.1·10⁴·ε** between two evaluations of the same geometry, which no line search can step through (it reads as a failed optimisation, not as the clash it is). The term now ramps smoothly from zero at 0.45 σ to full strength at 0.35 σ — energy and force both vanish at the edge — and the list window is wider (0.6 σ) than the switch, so a pair is carried at zero before it can contribute. The switch's own slope is part of the gradient, so the force stays the derivative of the energy.

  Verified with `.workbuddy/tools/verify_log_energy.js` (**23 checks**: the title of every bundled log, 139/139 frame energies against 138 steps with frame *k* carrying step *k*, the `--Link1--` leak, `opt=external`, a file with no title card), `verify_trajectory_companions.js` (**10 checks**: `atom_style molecular` versus the charge guess, the declared `Bonds` table including an 8.7 Å bond, a partially labelled dump, a dump with no element column), `verify_mm_switch.js` (**22 checks**: the term's edge and monotone ramp, its force against finite differences, `step().coords === finish().coords` at every chunk boundary, a pause/resume round trip, and a squeezed H···H that still relaxes), `verify_log_charges.js` (**180**), `verify_orca.js` (**173**), `verify_cif.js` (**275**), `verify_crystal_bonds.js` (**44**), `verify_arom_dash.js` (**2 870**, so a phenyl ring keeps its aromatic plan), `verify_addh_orders.js` (**32**), `verify_mm_hybrid.js` (**22**), `verify_frame_fix.js` (**41**), `verify_space_group.js` (**173**), `verify_symmetry_check.js` (**127**), `verify_vesta.js` (**66**) and `verify_crystal.js` (**254 of 255**; the one failure is the pre-split source-text check noted above). Five harnesses that still looked for the page template inside `dist/webview/molecularViewer.js` (`verify_orca.js` section 6, `verify_crystal_bonds.js`, `verify_bvs.js`, `verify_arom_dash.js`, `verify_frame_fix.js`) now read the shipped `media/viewer.js` instead. `npm run compile` is clean.

- **2026-11-12 — A CIF loop is read as a token stream, a PDB states its bond orders again, and a cumulated double bond is linear**

  **A loop is a token stream, not a table of lines** — `src/parsers/cifParser.ts` assembled at most one record per physical line and threw the rest of the line away, so a file that wrote two atom records on one line showed one atom, a record wrapped over two lines came out short and was dropped, and two symmetry operations on one line discarded the whole table and read the crystal as P1 — half the atoms, with a "no readable symmetry operation" warning instead of the cell. A CIF loop states a *record* (one value per key) and leaves the line breaks to the typesetter, so the loop body is now read by a cursor that spans lines: a record is complete when it holds one value per key, two records may come from one line and one record from three. The key list is read the same way, so keys may share the `loop_` line; a value written on its key's own line is still the `_key value` item it has always been read as, and whatever is left of the line where the body ends is handed back to the line reader instead of vanishing. Quoted values may hold blanks, a `;` text field is one value however many lines it covers, and `#` starts a comment wherever a token could start — including after a value, which the old reader did not recognise. A `1 2 3 4` body for two keys is now two records; a record truncated at the end of the body is kept as the short row it is, so its consumer reports it.
  One thing moved the other way: a record cut short *in the middle* of a loop no longer resynchronises at the next line, because a line break is not a record boundary — the records after it shift, and the atom-site reader counts them as skipped instead of silently keeping the atoms whose columns happened to line up. `verify_cif.js` 3.13/3.14 state that, and the CCDC 142619 fixture stays atom-for-atom identical (294 atoms, both operations).

  **CONECT states an order, and the reader reads it** — `src/parsers/pdbParser.ts` wrote `order: 1` on every `CONECT` bond, whatever the file said. Repeating a partner's serial is the only way the format states a multiple bond, and those repeats were being discarded as duplicates; they are now counted, by direction, and the larger of the two counts is the order (a bond written from both ends lists each single bond once on each side, so summing would double every bond in the file). The records are collected while the file is read and emitted at the end, so a `CONECT` record naming an atom listed further down now bonds it instead of being dropped, and the nested duplicate scan the old reader ran per bond — quadratic on a ligand with a few thousand bonds — is one map lookup. `media/viewer.js` writes the order back: a multiple bond repeats the partner's serial, so a saved PDB no longer loses the double bonds it was opened with (an aromatic 1.5 still has no spelling here and stays a single edge, which is what every PDB of an aromatic ring writes).

  **A carbon with no stated order is read from its shape** — `src/tools/addHydrogens.ts` decided a centre's hybridization from its bond orders alone, and a `CONECT` table, a Gaussian `connect` section or any other connectivity-only source gives every bond as a single one: an aromatic CH with its hydrogen already present was called sp3 and given one more, and an amide carbonyl carbon was given one as well. When — and only when — no bond order is stated anywhere around a carbon, the shape decides: three bonds whose angles make a full turn (≥ 350°, against 328° for a tetrahedron) are planar sp2, so nothing is added; two bonds at ≥ 160° are linear sp; two at ≥ 115° are an sp2 centre one hydrogen short, which is what an X-ray PDB's bare aromatic ring is. Everything else keeps the order-based answer, so a centre that states its orders is untouched, and a tetrahedral centre — a methylene of a small ring included — still gets the hydrogens it is missing.

  **A cumulated centre is linear, and MM can straighten it** — `src/tools/mmOptimizer.ts` took an atom's hybridization from its largest bond order, so the carbon of O=C=O read as sp2 and every angle term around it was given a 120° target: a CO2 drawn bent stayed at 119.99° with an energy of 0.000 and a "converged" label, because there was nothing left to lower. Two or more multiple bonds on a two-coordinate centre are now sp — the ligands leave along one axis — and the angle term at such a centre is marked `linear`, which exempts it from the two caps that exist to leave a strained ring as drawn: its deviation is always negative, so the compressed cap would have silenced exactly the term that has to open it. The rule is stated for carbon, the element that has no lone pair to bend it back; a two-coordinate sulfur with two double bonds (SO2) keeps its sp2 target and its 119°, which is why this is not a general "two doubles means linear". `verify_mm_hybrid.js` covers CO2 (120° → 180.0°, energy 91.86 → 0.000), allene, SO2, an sp3 ring, an amine, a carbonyl, an alkyne and a run with only the oxygens mobile.

  Verified with `.workbuddy/tools/verify_cif.js` (**275 checks**, including the three reported failures: two records on one line, a record wrapped over lines, two operations on one line), `verify_addh_orders.js` (**32 checks**: CONECT orders by direction and by cap, a forward-referenced record, a complete/bare/one-short benzene, an amide carbonyl both hand-built and read from a PDB, a stated double bond, a tetrahedral centre, a cyclopropane methylene, a quaternary carbon, and a Kekulé ligand read then completed) and `verify_mm_hybrid.js` (**22 checks**). The PDB writer's round trip is exercised through the page's own `doSave` (`CONECT    1    2    2` written, order 2 read back). No regression on the suites that touch these paths: `verify_crystal_addh.js` 58, `verify_symmetry_check.js` 127, `verify_space_group.js` 173, `verify_vesta.js` 66, `verify_fchk_edges.js` 21, `verify_log_charges.js` 180, `verify_crystal.js` 254 of 255 (the one failure is a source-text check that still looks for the pre-split page template). `npm run compile` is clean and the shipped page still parses (20 213 lines).

- **2026-11-11 — Six small robustness gaps: the page payload is parsed once, Save As checks its extension, and the remaining silent fallbacks are gone**

  **The page payload is assembled once** — `src/webview/page.ts` decided whether to offer the Polyhedra display style by `JSON.parse`-ing the fully serialised structure payload while it was building the same HTML; a large log or trajectory paid a second full parse and a second copy of the object graph for one `<option>`. The renderer now reads the payload object it already holds.

  **Save As writes the extension the content has** — `saveFile` now carries the chosen format's extension from the page, and `src/webview/molecularViewer.ts` appends it to a name typed without one and asks before writing a name whose extension contradicts the content (`water.xyz` holding a Gaussian input). The suffixes a save filter offers for the same format (`.poscar`/`.contcar` for a VASP cell, `.com`/`.gjf03` for Gaussian input, `.dat` for XY data, …) and a conventional extension-less `POSCAR` are accepted unchanged. A dismissed question writes nothing; a format that has no extension of its own (STRU) is left exactly as named, and a page that sends no extension keeps the old behaviour. Content that is not a string is refused instead of being written as `[object Object]`.

  **A superheavy atom keeps its element colour** — `ATOM_COLORS` stopped at Hs, so Mt–Og fell back to the magenta "unknown element" colour, and the import path (which reads the same table) showed the same fallback. The ten missing elements now carry the continuation of the Jmol transactinide ramp, so an opened file and an imported fragment colour them alike.

  **The import limit covers a single copy** — `postImportCopies` used `Math.max(1, …)`, so a single structure larger than 20 000 atoms was imported without a word (the truncation branch could never fire for it), and a multi-copy request was reduced to one copy by a warning that never said the structure itself exceeded the limit. A single copy is still imported whole — trimming it would delete atoms the user asked for — but the user is now told it is above the limit and that the view may be slow; additional copies are still reduced to one, with the count named.

  **A mismatched FCHK charge table is partial, not absent** — an array named `… Charges` whose length differed from the atom count was skipped before `chargesOf` could keep it, so the scheme disappeared from the panel without a warning. Such a table is now collected, filled with `null` for the atoms it does not cover, marked `complete: false` and reported with the number of values the file stated; the warning distinguishes missing values from extra ones.

  **GJF aromatic bonds stay 1.5** — the connect writer's `toFixed(1)` was flagged as a compatibility risk. Gaussian's connectivity section states real bond orders, and a real input saved with `geom=connect` writes `1.5` for aromatic bonds (verified against `test/1051388_3.gjf`), so 1.5 stays: rounding it to 1 or 2 would break both the order Gaussian reads and the aromatic ring when the file is opened again. Both GJF writers now share one helper that also turns a missing or non-finite order into a single bond instead of throwing inside `toFixed` and aborting the whole save.

- **2026-11-11 — A formatted checkpoint works on every path, and the remaining parser losses are closed** — Eleven places answered with less than the file stated.

  **FCHK parity** — A `.fchk`/`.fch` was fully supported only by direct open: Import's file filter did not list it, so choosing one through **All Files** read the whole checkpoint as text and then failed; Diff refused it; and a VMD script loaded it through `structureLoader` → `parseFile`, whose dispatch had no checkpoint branch. Import and Diff now list the suffix and read the atoms through the same streaming `parseFchk` reader as direct open (no text copy of a multi-hundred-MB file), Structure Diff compares the checkpoint's structure, and `loadStructureFile` streams a checkpoint named by a TCL scene. `parseFile` also has an `fchk`/`fch` branch backed by the new `parseFchkText`, so a small checkpoint that reaches it as text is read by the checkpoint reader instead of the XYZ fallback.

  **Orbitals and charges** — The Beta channel is no longer filled with Alpha orbital energies when the file states beta coefficients but no beta levels; the channel is omitted and a warning names the missing block, so an isosurface can no longer be chosen from a list that belongs to the other spin. A charge table whose values were truncated is now kept with the unreadable slots as `null`, has `complete: false` and shows its `(n/total)` count instead of being dropped or presented as complete.

  **Gaussian input** — A multi-line route section is kept line by line (`routeLine` joins them), so Save As no longer drops every keyword before the last line. The charge/multiplicity line is recognised by shape rather than assumed: a file without it reports the missing line and keeps its first atom row, while `0 1`, `-1 2` and multi-fragment lists still parse.

  **Extended XYZ** — Only `pbc="T T T"` (or a file with no pbc field) builds a 3D cell now. A partially periodic cell such as `pbc="T F F"` is reported with the directions it names and drawn as the molecule it is, instead of inventing periodic bonds in the two non-periodic directions.

  **Binary `.trj`** — `.trj` remains a text trajectory for xTB, but a binary file whose content is not XTC/TRR/DCD is detected before decoding (`looksLikeBinaryTrajectory`) and reported as an unsupported binary trajectory on open, Diff, Import and the TCL loader; `parseFile` has the same guard for a decoded `.trj` that still carries control bytes. CHARMM/NAMD files that are DCD keep opening frame by frame.

  **One structured-output scan** — The open path, Diff and Import now call `parseStructuredProgramOutput`, which runs the shared detector list once and returns the parsed structure; `parseFile`'s `.log`/`.inp` branches pass `structuredProbed` so `tryAutoParse` does not scan the same text again. A large QE/CP2K/LAMMPS output is walked once for detection and once by its parser, not three times.

  **LAMMPS includes** — `scanLammpsChain` now tracks only the current include stack, not every file ever visited. A second, non-recursive `include` of the same file is executed again (with the variables in force at that point), as LAMMPS does; a real cycle still stops with a warning.

  **Add H** — The hydrogen count is no longer `Math.round(target − valence)`. Carbon is read from its σ bonds (sp 2, sp² 3, sp³ 4), so an aromatic carbon with two aromatic bonds receives one H and one with an aromatic plus a single bond also receives one, rather than a rounded half bond adding two or none. Other elements resolve each fractional bond order to a whole 1 or 2, preferring the higher order on a tie, so a 1.5 bond cannot overfill a centre.

  The Import path already kept the host's bonds (and their VMD `vmdR` radii) since the previous fix; that behaviour is unchanged and covered by the existing import checks. Verified with `npm run compile`, the two checkpoint probes (`probe_fchk.js`: 147- and 432-atom files, columns and HOMO/LUMO), a synthetic partial-pbc extended XYZ, synthetic GJF files with a multi-line route and no charge line, the `parseFchkText`/`parseFile` dispatch on `methane.fchk` and `methane_nbo.fchk`, and direct Add H checks on aromatic/single/sp³ carbon centres.

- **2026-11-11 — Element+number selections work again, a VMD molecule switch takes its whole state with it, an import keeps the bonds the host perceived, and four silent state/I-O losses are closed** — Six paths answered with less than the file stated.

  **Element + number** — `parseSelectInput` tested `^([A-Za-z]+)(d+)$` — a literal `d` — so `C1`, `O2` and `H581` never matched and were dropped without a message in Select Atoms and in both Rotate Group fields. The pattern is `(\d+)` now; pure element names, plain numbers and `1-5` ranges behave exactly as before.

  **A VMD molecule switch** — `switchMolecule` replaced only atoms/bonds/segments. The selection stayed behind, so the same indices in the next molecule remained highlighted and Delete / Select / Rotate acted on atoms nobody picked; `MD.charge`, `multiplicity`, `gjfMeta`, `warnings` and `hasExplicitBonds` stayed those of the previous molecule, so the info panel, a Save As route/charge line and a later import's perception described the wrong structure. `VmdMoleculePayload` now carries each molecule's own charge, multiplicity, authored-bond flag, GJF route/title card and parser warnings (`parseWarnings`, kept separate from the script's own `warnings`), and the switch adopts them, clears the selection and copies the atoms/bonds instead of aliasing the resolved payload — an edit no longer writes back into the script's cache, so switching away and back shows the molecule the file states.

  **Import bonds and ghosts** — When neither side had authored bonds, the page re-perceived the combined system with the current molecule's charge and discarded the host's answer, which had been perceived against the imported file's own charge. Perception gives the total charge only to a component that covers the whole structure, so an ion imported beside a neutral molecule was perceived as neutral. The imported bonds now travel with the import message (with the file's charge and multiplicity) and are appended as the host perceived them; the existing structure's bonds — including any VMD per-bond radii — are kept rather than re-derived. An import onto an empty canvas adopts the imported file's own charge and multiplicity. `buildImportBlock` copies the `ghost` flag into every copy, so a multi-copy import keeps `C-Bq` a ghost instead of turning it into a real atom that bonds and counts in the formula.

  **FCHK short reads** — `readColumn` handed a short `fs.read` straight to `readFchkColumn`, which filled unread or unparseable fields with 0: a truncated checkpoint drew a wrong MO isosurface with no message. The read now retries at the offset it stopped at, and the column reader refuses a block that ends inside the column or a field that is not a number, naming the orbital and the byte count; the failure reaches the page as the surface error it is.

  **Trajectory frame edits** — A trajectory frame carries only coordinates and was rebuilt from the template on every visit, so an edit was dropped by the next frame switch while a log frame keeps its edits in `f.atoms`. A trajectory frame now keeps its own atom/bond records when it is left in a state the template cannot reproduce (a moved atom, a changed bond, an added or deleted atom), returns them on the next visit, and drops them when the frame is back at the file's geometry (an undo) so the pristine frame is rebuilt. Untouched frames still store nothing, so playing a trajectory costs no memory.

  Verified: 17 checks on the VMD switch (copies, state, selection, undo, patches), 10 on import (host bonds kept and translated, no re-perception, ghost bonds dropped, the ghost flag in every copy), 11 on trajectory frames (an edit persists, untouched frames store nothing, an undo drops the kept records), 8 on `parseSelectInput` (`C1`, `O2`, `C1 O2 H3`, `1-3`, `C`, `2`, `Cl5`, out of range), 4 on the FCHK column reader (complete, truncated, non-numeric, D exponent) plus `verify_fchk_edges.js` (21, on the real 114 MB checkpoint), `npm run compile` and the client syntax check.

## [1.0.5] - 2026-11-08

### Added

- **2026-11-08 — A structure saved as MDL Mol opens again, and the reader takes the V2000/V3000 files other tools write** — Save As has offered **MDL Mol (`.mol`)** since the export batch, but `parseFile` had no `mol` branch: the saved file fell through to the XYZ reader, whose "first line is an atom count" rule read the title line and returned an empty structure. `src/parsers/molParser.ts` now reads the CTfile: the counts line is located by its `V2000`/`V3000` marker rather than assumed to be line four (the writer's own one-header layout and the standard three-header layout both work), atom and bond rows are read from their fixed columns with a whitespace fallback, bond type 4 becomes the aromatic order 1.5, `*`/`LP`/query atoms (`A`, `Q`, `R#`, …) become dummy points instead of an invented carbon, and a row that cannot be read keeps its own slot so the bonds after it still address the atoms the file numbered. V3000 resolves bonds through an explicit atom-ID map, because its IDs need not be contiguous. `.mol` joins the custom-editor selector, both Explorer/editor-title menus and the open dialog, and a file with no extension is recognised by the counts line plus `M  END`. The writer was also made valid MDL: it now writes the three header lines the format requires, pads the atom/bond counts to their three-column fields, and writes coordinates in **Angstrom** — they had been multiplied by ten, so every reopened file was ten times too large. Verified with hand-built V2000 (new and old layouts, 120 atoms and 119 bonds, `*` ghost), V3000, an empty/invalid file (warning, no atoms), and `parseFile` dispatch on `.mol` and on an extensionless file.

- **The view exports as a PNG snapshot, at up to 4× and on a white or transparent background** — A **PNG** button sits beside Save As in the toolbar and writes the structure as drawn: the orientation on screen, the display style, the cell's a/b/c edge labels, and any surface, plane or interaction guide. The dialog offers 1–4× the viewport, drawn at pixel ratio 1 so the file is exactly the pixel size it names on any display (a 1400×900 panel at 2× is 2800×1800); a request past the GPU's renderbuffer/viewport limit or the 20 MP / 8192 px export ceiling is reduced **evenly** — never stretched, never quietly a different size than the one stated — and the option says so. Background is the viewer's dark, white, or transparent (the renderer keeps an alpha channel and only the capture clears to alpha 0, so the screen is unchanged). Panels, the info box and the orientation axes are page furniture and stay out of the picture; a Diff capture keeps both halves as drawn, without the two file-name labels. The capture touches the renderer only inside one synchronous step — drawing buffer resized at ratio 1, one frame drawn by the same `renderFrame()` the animation loop uses (the Diff split, its two scissor boxes and both cameras included), the buffer read before the browser composites — and restores size, pixel ratio, background and clear alpha in a `finally`, so a failed capture cannot leave a resized or blurry view, and the loop's drawing path is one function rather than two copies. The save dialog starts beside the source file as `<stem>-snapshot.png` (`-diff` in Diff mode), a name typed without an extension gets `.png`, and the host checks the PNG signature rather than writing a corrupt file. Verified by two new suites. `.workbuddy/tools/verify_snapshot.js` (**84 assertions**) pins the pixel size at every factor, the GPU- and ceiling-limited reductions keeping the aspect ratio, a one-pixel and a collapsed viewport, all three background modes, exact restoration of ratio/size/background/clear alpha/clear-and-scissor state on success and on a read that throws, a read that is not a PNG being refused, the message carrying raw base64 under `savePng`, and one `renderFrame()` shared by the loop and the capture. `.workbuddy/tools/verify_snapshot_page.js` (**31 assertions**) runs the shipping page in headless Edge with the real WebGL renderer and reads the buffer at the moment it is encoded: a 2× transparent capture is exactly twice the view in both dimensions, its corner is `(0, 0, 0, 0)`, the file's IHDR declares the same 2× size and the PNG signature, a white capture is opaque white, the default keeps the viewer's `#1e1e1e`, the drawing buffer is back at its own size afterwards, and the captures that follow still draw the structure. The client suites that run without a browser stay green (autoplay 50, provenance 78, crystal 255, crystal edit 155, crystal export 209, CIF 265, ghost 237, display style 268, shortcuts 115, clipboard 102, selection 48, ADPs 92, VESTA 66, …), the toolbar layout suite stays at 192/192 with the new button in the actions row, and the built page's client script parses (18 489 lines).

- **xTB `.trj` files open as trajectories, and a text trajectory contributes a chosen frame to Diff and Import** — `.trj` is registered in the custom-editor selector, both context menus and the open dialog, and a multi-frame `.trj`/`.xyz` opens with the same frame navigation as `xtbopt.log` (a single-frame file keeps the ordinary structure path). Diff and Import no longer take the first frame of a multi-frame XYZ/xTB trajectory or of a multi-frame LAMMPS dump behind the user's back: they ask which frame, label every choice with its frame number and comment/timestep, and cancel cleanly without a result. A dump frame carries its lattice into the Diff cell panel as a trajectory cell — its box describes the frame, not a second crystal to compare — so a molecule on screen can still be compared with it. Verified (`test/test_trajectory_documents.js`: a two-frame `xtb.trj` opens with both frames and its energies, and the general-triclinic dump keeps its vectors) with the parser audit, robustness, input-reference, LAMMPS-reference and save-round-trip suites unchanged and green.

- **A trajectory frame is saved with the cell it was run in** — Save As on a trajectory frame wrote the frame's atoms but, because a trajectory is not a crystal, CP2K, GROMACS and Extended XYZ wrapped them in a new bounding box; the current frame's own cell (`TRAJ_CELL`) is now used wherever the format can state one, the dialog note says so, and the frame's coordinates are written against that box. Verified in `test/test_save_roundtrip.js`'s trajectory-frame section: a triclinic `[[10,0,0],[2,11,0],[1,3,12]]` frame round-trips through CP2K, GROMACS and Extended XYZ with its cell parameters and atom positions, and the suite stays at 0 failures.

- **A general-triclinic LAMMPS dump (`ITEM: BOX BOUNDS abc origin`) keeps its three edge vectors** — `dump_modify triclinic/general` writes one full edge vector and its origin component per line, not the restricted lower-triangular bounds; the reader treated the first three numbers as restricted bounds and rotated the box. The `abc origin` header (or three four-value lines) is now recognised and the vectors are taken exactly as written, on both the trajectory and single-frame paths, with scaled coordinates converted through those three vectors. Verified in `test/test_trajectory_documents.js` (cell and scaled positions across two frames) and the parser audit (a single-frame general box).

- **MD trajectories open frame by frame — GROMACS XTC/TRR, CHARMM/NAMD/LAMMPS DCD and multi-frame LAMMPS text dumps** — A binary trajectory is recognised by its magic before any text decode (XTC 1995 and TRR 1993 at byte 0; DCD's `CORD` behind a 4- or 8-byte Fortran record marker in either byte order), so a renamed `.xtc`, `.trr` or `.dcd` still opens, and the three suffixes join the custom-editor selector, both context menus and the open dialog. The XTC reader decodes the integer-compressed 3D coordinate blocks (per-frame precision, minimum integers, the small-integer table and its dynamic updates); TRR takes the coordinate block and box of every frame that carries positions; DCD reads the CHARMM/NAMD header, the fixed-atom free-index list and the unit-cell record, and accepts both record widths and both byte orders. XTC/TRR store nanometres and scale to Å; DCD and text dumps are scaled by the LAMMPS run's units found in the sibling input chain (`real`/`metal` 1, `nano` 10, `si` 1e10, `cgs` 1e8, `electron` 0.529177 Å, `lj` 1 with a warning that reduced units are shown unscaled). A topology is paired from the same-name `.gro`/`.pdb`/`.ent`/`.xyz`/`.extxyz`/`.data`, from the `read_data` file the LAMMPS script names, or — only when exactly one file in the folder has the same atom count — from that file, with a warning naming the guess; a dump pairs only a `.data` file, because its rows are written in atom-ID order and the data reader sorts by ID for this pairing. Elements, colours, occupancy and bonds (perceived when the topology states none) come from the template; a binary trajectory without a topology shows `Xx` and says so, while a dump whose element column names its atoms but has no data file keeps the elements and warns that no bonds are drawn.

  Each frame's box is drawn as a plain wireframe through the box builder a crystal uses, but the trajectory stays outside the symmetry, supercell and cell-editing machinery: a box that changes between steps is not a crystal. The first frame is the document's structure, so **Save As** writes the frame on screen and its provenance line names the frame; switching frames is a structure change that ends normal-mode playback, Move Atoms and MM sessions and clears undo, then rebuilds fresh atom records from the template and the frame's flat xyz, so an edit on one frame never leaks into the next (the template itself is a copy, not the frame's own array). The existing ◀/▶, jump field and Auto Play drive trajectory frames unchanged, labelled `Step 2500 · t = 5 ps` for XTC/TRR, `Step k` for DCD and `Timestep N` for a dump. At most **500** frames and **1.5 M** atoms across the kept frames are shown, sampled evenly with the last frame always included and a warning that says so; truncated, undecodable and atom-count-changing frames are skipped with their own warnings. A multi-frame dump takes the trajectory path; a single-frame dump keeps the periodic-structure path it always had. **Diff** and **Import Structure** also accept binary trajectories: instead of decoding the bytes as text, they ask which frame to use (a single-frame file is used directly).

  LAMMPS text dumps follow the format as LAMMPS writes it: the true restricted triclinic box (`xlo = xlo_bound − min(0, xy, xz, xy+xz)`, xhi with the maximum, ylo/yhi with yz) rather than the bounding box, one consistent coordinate triple (`x/y/z`, `xu/yu/zu`, `xs/ys/zs` or `xsu/ysu/zsu` — a scaled value is a fraction of the restricted box and is not translated by its origin), rows sorted by ID so all frames and the data file share one atom order, and types named by `dump_modify element`, `mass` or the companion data file. Verified on real files: water XTC/TRR (24 atoms, 11 frames, cubic 12.43 Å, `.gro` topology, 16 bonds), triclinic Mg HCP TRR (150 atoms; a = 16.047 Å and b = −8.0235/13.8971 Å, the tilted vector keeping its x component), LAMMPS Si dump (4 frames, 8 atoms, `data.si` topology, 5.431 Å cell), scaled triclinic Mg dump (2 frames, elements, 3.2094/−1.6047/2.7794 Å cell) and a single-frame dump remaining a periodic crystal; a synthetic DCD suite exercises both byte orders, 4- and 8-byte markers, fixed atoms and an orthogonal unit cell, and the page payload compiles as JavaScript (`test/test_trajectory_documents.js`, all checks). On the real page the frame field moved from frame 1 to frame 6 (`Step 2500 · t = 5 ps`), atom 1's coordinates and the coordinate sum changed, the 16 bonds and the cell stayed, and frame 1 restored the original coordinates; the parser audit, robustness, 81-file crystal-example read (0 without atoms), output-disambiguation, input-reference, LAMMPS-reference, save-round-trip and CIF suites are unchanged and green.

- **ORCA, ABINIT, ABACUS and CP2K main inputs open the structure file they name** — The LAMMPS `read_data` handling now covers the other supported programs whose input is a job script rather than a geometry. **ORCA** parses `* xyzfile <charge> <multiplicity> <file>` (spaced or `*xyzfile`, quoted names, paths with spaces) beside the existing inline `* xyz` block, and opens the external XYZ instead of the input text. **ABINIT** reads `xyzfile` (including dataset-suffixed `xyzfile1`) as an Angstrom XYZ while keeping the input's `acell`/`rprim`; when the input states no cell the XYZ is shown as a free molecule instead of being squeezed into ABINIT's 1-Bohr default box, and `include` files are spliced before the reference is scanned. **ABACUS** `INPUT` opens the `STRU` named by `stru_file` (default `STRU` beside the input), and the bare `INPUT` name joins the custom-editor selector and both context menus. **CP2K** resolves `COORD_FILE_NAME` for XYZ, PDB and CP2K/CRD coordinate files (the last definition wins, quoted names and trailing `!`/`#` comments handled), honouring `COORD_FILE_FORMAT` and preferring the stated file over an inline `&COORD`; `@INCLUDE`, `@SET`/`$VAR`/`${VAR}` and `@IF`/`@IFDEF`/`@IFNDEF`/`@ELSE`/`@ENDIF` are expanded first so a path or section kept in an include is found — even when the main file holds only directives — and every nested include's folder is searched for the companion as well. All formats resolve quoted, relative, absolute and variable-expanded paths the way the job is launched, report a missing reference by naming the file, and report include cycles, unreadable includes and chains deeper than eight files as warnings rather than hanging. When the referenced structure is the one shown, `data.filePath` points at it, so Save As starts in its folder with its name. Verified by a new host-level test (`test/test_input_references.js`, **31 checks**) that opens each file through the extension's own `buildParseOptions`/`openCustomDocument`: ORCA `xyzfile` (spaced, unspaced, a quoted name with spaces, the inline `* xyz` block, a `%coords` fallback and a missing file); ABINIT `xyzfile` in a stated cell, through an `include` with the companion beside the include, dataset-suffixed, without a cell, missing and cyclic; ABACUS `INPUT` with an explicit `stru_file`, the default `STRU` and a missing one; CP2K `COORD_FILE_NAME` through `@SET`/`${VAR}`, an `@INCLUDE` folder, a directives-only main file, `@IF`/`@ELSE`, `@IFDEF`, a PDB file with a trailing comment, the last of two names, an inline-`&COORD` fallback, a quoted missing name, an unsupported format, a missing include and a cycle; a QE `.in` that shares ABINIT keys; the parsers' standalone `externalStructureFile`/`externalCoordinateFile` entry points; and a directly opened `STRU`. The LAMMPS reference suite, the 73-file example read (0 without atoms), the save round-trip (0 failures), the parser audit and the CIF suite are unchanged and green.

- **A LAMMPS main input opens the structure it names, instead of drawing its own text** — `in.lammps`, any `*.lammps` script and a LAMMPS-style `.in` are now recognised as **inputs**, not coordinate files: the former fallback read the script as a one-atom XYZ fragment (the first line `units metal` counted as an atom and its words as coordinates). A `.lammps`/`.lmp` file that is neither data nor a dump is treated as the job's input even when it holds a single `include`. The document now follows `read_data` and shows that data file, with the input's `units`, `atom_style`, `mass` and `dump_modify element` lines describing its types. Paths are resolved the way the job is launched: relative to the main input's folder first, then to the folder of the `include` that named them, with a case-insensitive fallback for a name written on another machine and absolute paths used as written. `include` files are followed in order (including LAMMPS wildcard includes such as `include coords.*`, in alphabetical order), `variable ... string/index` definitions and `${var}`/`$var` references are expanded in command order, and quoted names containing spaces are kept whole. A name that still holds a variable, a cycle, an unreadable include and a chain deeper than eight files are reported as warnings, never followed literally or followed forever. A missing `read_data` target produces one warning naming the file and shows no atoms; a `read_restart`-only script says that a restart file holds no atom list. When the data file is the one shown, `data.filePath` points at it, so **Save As** starts in its folder with its name. Opening the bare `data` file directly still works and takes its type names and units from the sibling input chain, and the file picker, custom-editor selector, Explorer and editor-title menus now accept the bare `data` file. Verified by a new host-level test (`test/test_lammps_references.js`, **12 checks**) that runs the extension's own `openCustomDocument`/`buildParseOptions` against the shipped `0.2ethanol_solution_MH-1-omol_300w` example — **1050 atoms, C 100 / O 250 / H 700, a 21.8316 Å cubic cell, `filePath` on `data`** — plus a bare-`data` open, an include reached through a subdirectory and a variable, a wildcard include, a one-line `include` main file, an absolute path, missing-file and restart-style warnings, an unresolved variable, a run-time-built structure and the parser's standalone `externalStructureFile` entry point.

- **Ten more programs open as structures, from their input, their log or their trajectory** — The reader knew Gaussian, ORCA, xtb, XYZ, MOL2, PDB, MOPAC, Turbomole, CIF, VASP POSCAR, cube and VESTA; it now also reads the solid-state and MD codes the examples directory covers. **ABACUS**: `STRU` (ATOMIC_SPECIES/MASS, `LATTICE_CONSTANT`, `LATTICE_VECTORS`, Direct/Cartesian/`Cartesian_angstrom` blocks, per-site constraint flags), `running_scf.log` (the echoed cell in a0 and its `DIRECT COORDINATES` table) and `MD_dump` (one cell per MDSTEP, Angstrom). **ABINIT**: `.abi` and `.abo`, with `acell` × `rprim` (three different `acell` lengths honoured), `znucl`/`typat`, `xred`/`xcart`/`xangst`, the `N*value` repeat notation, and the converged block preferred over the echoed `-outvars` one. **CP2K**: `.inp` and `.out`, with `&CELL` given as `ABC` plus angles or as `A`/`B`/`C`, `&COORD` Cartesian/scaled, `COORD_FILE_NAME` companions, `&KIND` names and `PERIODIC NONE`. **Quantum ESPRESSO**: `.in`/`.pwi`/`.pwo`, with `ibrav` + `celldm` or `CELL_PARAMETERS`, `ATOMIC_POSITIONS` in crystal/alat/bohr/angstrom, and the final coordinates preferred over the echoed `crystal axes`/`tau` tables. **LAMMPS**: data files and dumps, with the true triclinic bounds of a data file versus the bounding box of a dump, `Masses`/`mass`/`dump_modify element` type naming, and the atomic/charge/full/molecular atom styles. **GROMACS**: `.gro`, nm → Å and the nine-number box in GROMACS's own `v1x v2y v3z v1y v1z v2x v2z v3x v3y` order; a zero or absent box is a molecule. **GPUMD/ASE**: extended XYZ, keeping `Lattice=`, `Properties=` and `pbc=` from the comment line and reading the property layout by name. **DeepMD/DP-GEN**: `coord.raw` and `coord.npy` datasets with `type.raw`/`type_map.raw`, `box.raw`/`box.npy` and `nopbc`, using the companion files the loader reads from the same directory. **PySCF** output (the last `New geometry`, the periodic `lattice vectors a1/a2/a3` in Bohr, the `[INPUT]` fallback) and **Molden**. VASP gains `XDATCAR` (first configuration shown, the rest counted in a warning), `OUTCAR` and `vasprun.xml` beside POSCAR/CONTCAR.

  Units are decided by the format and never by a guess: Bohr↔Å for ABACUS/ABINIT/PySCF/cube, nm→Å for GROMACS, and an explicit `angstrom`/unit marker where a file states one. What a file does not state is reported rather than invented — an unnamed LAMMPS type stays `T3` with a warning, a missing companion file is named, a trajectory with more than one frame shows the first and says how many follow, and a structural error that would otherwise draw a wrong cell becomes a warning on the panel. The same recognition routes `.log`/`.out` files: structured program output (QE, CP2K, PySCF, ABINIT, VASP, LAMMPS, ABACUS, XDATCAR) is detected before the Gaussian/ORCA fallback, so a mis-named file still opens with the right reader. The file open dialog, the custom-editor selectors, the Explorer and editor-title context menus, and the Diff picker now accept `.abi`, `.abo`, `.stru`, `.gro`, `.molden`, `.pwi`, `.pwo`, `.lammpstrj`, `.dump`, `.lmp`, `.lammps`, `.data`, `.xdatcar`, `.outcar`, `STRU`, `STRU_MD_*`, `MD_dump`, `XDATCAR`, `OUTCAR`, `vasprun.xml`, `coord.raw` and `coord.npy`. Verified by `test/test_crystalstruct_examples.js` on the shipped examples: **73 files, 0 without atoms**, each with its cell, atom count and title checked.

- **The unit cell can be written to six more simulation formats, and a molecule to three** — Save As gains **ABACUS STRU**, **ABINIT**, **Quantum ESPRESSO** and **LAMMPS data** for any loaded cell, and **CP2K input**, **GROMACS** and **Extended XYZ** for both a cell and a molecule. Every periodic writer states the *unit cell* — the symmetry-expanded `baseAtoms`, wrapped into [0,1) — never the displayed supercell: **STRU** writes vectors in Å with `LATTICE_CONSTANT 1.8897259886` and `Direct` sites (with the site's constraint flags when it has any); **ABINIT** writes `acell 1.0 1.0 1.0 Angstrom`, the cell as `rprim` and sites as `xred`; **QE** writes `ibrav = 0`, `CELL_PARAMETERS angstrom` and `ATOMIC_POSITIONS crystal`; **LAMMPS** re-expresses the cell in the restricted triclinic form (a along x, b in the xy plane) and maps the sites with the same basis, so a CIF or POSCAR cell does not reach LAMMPS as a sheared box. **CP2K** writes `&CELL A/B/C` and `&COORD` (a molecule gets a one-Å bounding box and `PERIODIC NONE`); **GROMACS** writes nm with the nine-value box in GROMACS's order (a molecule sits in a bounding box, since a .gro always states one); **Extended XYZ** writes `Lattice=` in Å with `pbc="T T T"`, or `pbc="F F F"` and no lattice for a molecule. Dummy points keep their identity wherever the format has a spelling for one: `X` in STRU/QE/GRO/extended XYZ, `znucl 0` in ABINIT, a zero-mass `# X` type in LAMMPS, and a `&KIND X` without a `POTENTIAL` in CP2K. The save dialog offers the formats in its filter list and suggests `STRU`, `*.abi`, `*.pwi`, `*.lmp`, `*.inp`, `*.gro` and `*.xyz` names. Verified by a new round-trip test (`test/test_save_roundtrip.js`): the writers are extracted from the compiled page script and their text is read back through the shipping parsers for cubic Si, triclinic NaCl, triclinic HCP Mg and a methane molecule — cell parameters, atom count, element set and periodic fractional sites all survive, GRO coordinates to its 0.001 nm precision, and a ghost added to the cell comes back as `X` from every one of the seven formats; **0 failures**. The 73-file read test is unchanged and green.

### Changed

- **Playback runs faster — 250 ms per trajectory frame and a 2.2 s vibration cycle** — Auto Play held every structure of an optimisation or trajectory for **350 ms**, which is longer than a step needs: a step's geometry is legible well before the tick arrives, so a run of a couple of hundred steps spends most of its time waiting rather than moving. The interval is now **250 ms** (1.4× faster), still a still per structure rather than a film frame — every step is a whole scene rebuild and has to be read on its own — and still the one `FRAME_PLAY_MS` constant the timer and the cadence reasoning share. A normal mode's cycle drops from **3 s to 2.2 s** (1.36× faster): one full swing (equilibrium → + → equilibrium → − → equilibrium) had come to read as the structure breathing rather than vibrating, while 2.2 s keeps a large skeleton followable atom by atom instead of blurring it. Only the speed moved: the 0.35 Å maximum displacement, the sine phase, the stop-on-the-last-frame rule, the restart-from-first when Play is pressed there, and every guard that ends an auto-play or a vibration (a frame or molecule switch, an Undo, an Import, an MM run, a Move Atoms or Edit Coordinates session) are untouched. Verified by `.workbuddy/tools/verify_autoplay.js` (**50 assertions**, now arming and stepping at 250 ms against the compiled client on a fake clock) and a new `.workbuddy/tools/verify_vibration_speed.js` (**15 assertions**) that cuts the shipped vibration block out of `dist` and measures the cycle: equilibrium at 0, ±0.35 Å at a quarter and three quarters, closed at 2200 ms, t=2250 repeating t=50, and the tick re-arming itself; `tsc -p ./` clean.

### Fixed

- **2026-11-08 — An output is read by what wrote it, and every structure table is read to its own declared end** — `tryAutoParse` tested the `* xyz` input syntax before structured output and ORCA output, so an ORCA `.out` renamed to a non-log suffix was claimed by the ORCA input reader through the input block the output echoes: zero atoms, no warning. Structured outputs and ORCA output are now detected **before** the input syntaxes, an ORCA output that holds no geometry frame says so instead of leaving an unexplained empty canvas, and a `.log`/`.out` that is not structured output falls through `tryAutoParse` before the frame reader — so an old MOPAC output with its `CARTESIAN COORDINATES` table and no version banner opens. The readers below no longer lose or invent atoms at table boundaries. An XYZ is read until its **declared** atom count: a blank row no longer drops the last atom, a short block reports how many rows could be read, and a numeric data row (`box.raw`) is not mistaken for an XYZ count. Turbomole `$coord angs` is kept in Å while plain `$coord` still converts Bohr. A cube whose header line 3 is unreadable (`NaN` atom count) returns a warning instead of an empty structure, a truncated atom table stops at the first grid-data row and reports the missing atoms instead of turning grid values into ghost atoms, and the orbital-record line is read immediately after the atoms. The Gaussian orientation-header scan is bounded and stops at the first atom row, so a table missing its dashed rules no longer consumes every later frame, convergence table and frequency section to EOF; the atomic-number map now covers 104–118 (Rf–Og) instead of turning them into ghosts. A PDB with a blank or hybrid-36 serial is no longer de-duplicated against every other `NaN` (`Set` treated them as one value), and without the element column the element is read from the atom-name **column alignment** — a right-justified ` CA ` (protein C-alpha) is carbon while a left-justified `CA  ` is calcium. A GJF's `hasExplicitBonds` is set only when a bond was actually added (an all-out-of-range connect table used to make the viewer skip perception and draw no bonds), and a multi-`--Link1--` file now states that only its first structure is shown. A POSCAR's coordinate-mode line is consumed only when it really states Direct/Cartesian/Selective; a missing mode line keeps its first coordinate row and warns instead of silently re-reading every site.

- **2026-11-08 — A V2000 `.mol` whose atom and bond counts are both three digits opens again** — `v2000Counts` read the two counts with a whitespace regex, but V2000 states them in fixed columns 1-3 and 4-6, so a file with 100+ atoms *and* 100+ bonds has no separator between the fields (`294340`) and the whole structure came back empty (the reader then reported bonds naming missing atoms). The fixed-width pair is now read first, with the whitespace form kept as the fallback for hand-edited or padded lines (`12 13`). Verified with synthetic V2000 files at 9/8, 100/100, 294/340 and spaced 12/13 counts, and with the export round-trip: 32 files captured from the live page (molecule and 294-atom crystal, all offered formats) re-read through the shipping parsers with the same atom counts, coordinates and bond tables, **0 failures**.

- **2026-11-08 — An over-filled selection starts over, crystal contact rows say whether they are drawn, and an H-bond row selects D-H-A** — `selectAtom` returned early when a required-count mode (Bond Length / Bond Angle / Dihedral / Add Atom / Delete Atom) already held the clicked atom, so Select All or a box drag left the tool looking dead: the click was swallowed and `checkSelectionComplete` only opens its dialog at exactly `requiredCount()`. A click in those modes with more than the required number selected now resets the selection to the clicked atom and re-runs the completion check. A crystal Neighbour/Interaction row whose periodic images are outside the drawn supercell was explained only after a click; the Interaction list now marks such rows (`ng-off` + an `image` tag and a title suffix), using one display map built per refresh (the Neighbour list already marked them). An H-bond row now selects its three atoms: a molecular row uses `dIdx/hIdx/aIdx`, and a crystal row resolves the drawn hydrogen image through `hShift` (`intDisplayHbPair`), falling back to D/A when that image is outside the drawn cell. Verified on the live page: molecular deep test **22/22** (the over-filled selection case now passes), water dimer **4/4** (`[0,1,3]`), 142619 disorder/interaction **12/12** (16 of 30 rows marked undrawn; a 2×2×2 row select returns `[9,15,2112]`), crystal deep test **32/32**, and the selection **48**, neighbours **64**, crystal **255**, shortcuts **116** and clipboard **102** suites.

- **2026-11-08 — The MD and dataset readers keep the identities their files state** — GROMACS atom names are no longer exact two-letter element matches: an ion residue whose name and atom name are the same element (`CA CA`, `CL CL`, `NA NA`) keeps its metal, while topology labels take their first letter (`CA`/`CB`/`CD`/`CE` → C, `HG`/`HG1` → H, `NE` → N) and `OW`/`HW1` still become O/H. An ABACUS log type whose `number of atom` was never read is no longer padded with one site (which shifted every later type→element assignment); the site stays the dummy `X` and the log says so. A DeepMD type index that `type_map.raw` does not list is reported with the offending indices instead of silently inventing `T3`. Extended XYZ frame counting walks the count/comment/atom-row boundaries and skips blank separators, so a multi-frame file separated by blank lines (or cut off mid-frame) still reports “N frames in the file: showing the first” instead of looking like a single frame.

- **2026-11-08 — VMD selections, scalar files, SMILES validation and MM answers describe what is actually drawn** — A `name`/`type` VMD selection is no longer resolved through the element table (`name CA` became calcium and selected nothing, hiding the representation; `name H1` widened to the whole molecule): it joins the metadata-dependent selections that widen to the whole molecule **with a reported warning**. The default representation `mol new` creates is tracked per molecule, and `mol delrep <rep> <mol>` addresses the molecule it names, so a later `mol new` no longer shifts the representation indices of an earlier molecule. A bare PDB is auto-coloured only when it carries the toolchain's own numbering marker — the atom serial written into the residue-number field and a single chain — beside the existing no-header and non-constant-column tests; a headerless PDB with real residue numbering and genuine B-factors keeps its element colours. An unrecognized representation style no longer reads its arguments as CPK's (`Tube 0.3 20 1` had produced an 8 Å cylinder); only CPK (and its spelling variants) takes the two numbers, and every other style draws the viewer's fixed default. `validateSmiles` now applies the same `max_atoms` check as generation (counting hydrogens) and returns the same message, so an input the keystroke validator accepts cannot be rejected later. `evaluateForceField` treats an explicitly empty selection array as “all atoms mobile”, matching its documented missing-selection semantics (it used to return energy 0 and a zero gradient). `MmSession.finish()` returns the **lowest-energy accepted geometry** together with the energy and force reading taken there, instead of falling back to the input coordinates while still quoting another geometry's `maxGrad` whenever a pause lands above the starting energy.

Verified with `tsc -p ./` clean; 67 targeted assertions covering the renamed ORCA output, old MOPAC `.log`, blank/short XYZ, `$coord angs`, unreadable/truncated cube, missing dashed rules, Z=118, blank PDB serials and CA/Ca alignment, GJF connect/Link1, missing POSCAR mode line, GROMACS ion vs topology names, deepmd/abacus/extxyz warnings, VMD selections/rep indices/Tube/scalar marker, SMILES limit and MM empty selection/best geometry; 55 real-file regressions (ORCA `h2o.out` opens every `CARTESIAN COORDINATES` table, `tetramethylammonium.log` gives its 17 standard-orientation frames plus the first input frame, `PEG600.log` gives one 73-atom frame per orientation table, `fragment.coord`, CIF, cube and POSCAR still open, every sample PDB parses with its stated element — `dForce4.pdb`'s scalar channel included — and `show_units.tcl` 28 reps, `show_eggs.tcl`/`show_scheme.tcl` 15, `force_error.tcl` 4 molecules and `force_error_delta.tcl` 3, each with its written scale); and a 176-file example-corpus read with **0 exceptions** (every zero-atom result is an input, metadata or auxiliary file that names no structure).

- **2026-11-08 — A dialog no longer commits the preview it replaces, and the mouse/keyboard latches cannot act behind it** — `showModal` overwrote the previous dialog's DOM without running its Cancel, so a bond-length/angle/dihedral preview that was still open was silently committed when Save As or a mode switch opened another dialog, its undo snapshot stayed in the stack, and the new dialog's own snapshot could be popped by the old Cancel. The old dialog is now dismissed through its own `.mbtn-cancel` before replacement (and before a measurement dialog takes its baseline snapshot), and a mode switch does the same instead of only hiding the overlay. While a dialog is open the page's keyboard door now returns after Esc and the shortcuts it deliberately serves (Ctrl+S — the Save dialog's own confirm, or a replacement of any other dialog through its Cancel — Ctrl+C, and Ctrl+Z guarded): Delete no longer opens a delete confirmation behind the dialog and the arrow keys no longer start a fragment rotation or a view spin. Leaving View clears the Auto Rotate toggle as well as its velocity, so arrow keys in another mode cannot keep a spin running with the switch hidden. Undo stops frame auto-play before restoring, so the next tick cannot put the old frame back. Ctrl-drag and Ctrl+arrow fragment movement refuse while a Molecular Mechanics run is active, because its next progress chunk would overwrite the manual edit. A blur (Alt-Tab) now ends `isRot`/`isPan`/box-select and every move/fragment/bake session, committing what the drag had already written, so returning focus and moving the mouse without a button no longer rotates or pans. Box Select collects its hits and applies the selection (and the Diff mapping between the two halves) once — the previous per-atom `selectAtom` re-highlighted the whole structure for every hit, which is O(k·n) and froze large selections. Verified by the shortcuts (**116**), selection (**48**), crystal (**255**), autoplay (**51**), view-card (**84**) and toolbar (**192**) suites, all green, and the built client script still parses.

- **2026-11-08 — The page and the host no longer lose a file to an unescaped payload, a blocked inline style, a missing asset or a bad path** — The structure payload was inserted into a `<script>` block unescaped, so a file title containing `</script>` closed the block early (blank page and injected markup); serialisation now escapes `<` as `\u003c` plus U+2028/U+2029, which JSON parses back to the original text. The CSP allows style only through its nonce, which silently discarded the five `style="display:none"` attributes on the Beta/Eggs buttons, the symmetry note, the XRD file input and the rotate slider; those initial states moved into the nonce'd stylesheet (and the Cavities Å unit gained a class), with runtime `style.display` changes still overriding them. A missing `media/three.min.js` no longer leaves the generic error panel: the read is guarded and returns a static diagnostic page naming the asset and the reason. A Save As whose structure has no source path now starts beside the opened document (then the workspace folder) instead of at the drive root, and an absolute companion path written for another machine (`/home/u/data.lmp`) falls back to its basename in the job's folder. LAMMPS data/dump files find their input script by the same rule the trajectory path uses (`in.*`, `*.in`, `*.lmp`, `*.lammps`), so a job named `run.lmp` keeps its `units`, `atom_style` and type labels; symbol-link companions are accepted with `type & FileType.File` instead of a strict equality. Text is decoded by BOM: UTF-16LE `.gjf`/`.inp`/`.data` files open as text instead of mojibake. Both `CancellationToken`s are honoured — `openCustomDocument` stops after its read and `resolveCustomEditor` checks the token before and after reading the renderer, assigning `html` in a guard so a closed tab no longer logs “Webview is disposed”. Host and page message entry points reject a message that is not an object with a string `command`, Diff-cancel restores the current mode's status line instead of hardcoding View Mode, Save As offers the source stem with the chosen extension, a trajectory edit/undo re-centres on its own geometry (only a frame switch, and the view-only style/colour/ellipsoid rebuilds, keep the fixed centre), and the powder XRD canvas redraws at the panel's current width after a resize or when restored from its chip, keeping the 2θ click mapping aligned with the bitmap. Verified by `tsc -p ./`, the client syntax check, the XRD (**213**), toolbar (**192**) and crystal (**255**) suites and the unchanged views/selection/shortcut suites.

- **2026-11-08 — A frame's bonds belong to that frame, a playback no longer eats the undo history, and a replaced crystal atom is perceived again** — `switchFrame` handed the page the frame's own bond array (`MD.bonds=f.bonds`) instead of a copy, so an edit that appended a bond (Add Atom, a re-perception) also appended to the file's stored frame; returning to that frame later drew bonds pointing at atoms that were not there. Every frame now gets its own bond records, the copy the trajectory branch already made. The switch cleared the selection only in Diff mode, leaving an ordinary frame change with a selection that named whatever atoms happened to hold the same indices in the new frame; every frame switch resets the selection now. A normal-mode playback starts by showing the last frame (the one the frequency job computed), and that frame switch cleared the whole undo stack, so pressing Play once discarded the edit history; the stack is carried across the switch when both frames describe the same atom list (a multi-job log that changes molecule still drops it, because a snapshot of one structure must not be applied to another). `removeDisorder` rebuilt the base cell without ending the selection or the rotate-group session whose indices it renumbers; both end before the renumbering. Crystal **Replace Atom** changed `baseAtoms` only, so C→O kept every C–H bond order of the carbon; the bonds touching a replaced site are now perceived again from the new element and merged with the file's remaining table, while bonds between untouched atoms keep their order.
- **2026-11-08 — Structure Diff compares bond orders too, keeps every ring angle and torsion, and no longer distorts percentages** — Three parts of the comparison were answering with less than the files state. The 0–20% threshold used `threshPct||2`, so the slider's leftmost position still filtered at 2%; it is now `null`-checked, and 0 means 0. The right-hand bond table recorded its order but only its presence was tested, so a single↔double change at the same skeleton reported “Structures are identical”; bond-order differences are now a section of their own (single/aromatic/double/triple wording, both sides highlighted, counted in the status line and listed at every threshold). Angles and dihedrals were deduplicated by sorting the whole atom set, which made a three-membered ring's three angles and a four-membered ring's four torsions share one key and silently dropped all but one; an angle now keeps its centre in the middle with only the two ends ordered, and a dihedral is canonicalised by its direction alone, so reversal still collapses but different ring paths do not. A dihedral's percentage used `(|d1|+|d2|)/2` as the denominator, which exploded for a small change near 0° and under-reported a real 60→75° change; the wrapped angle difference is now measured against 180°, and the panel says so. Verified by the crystal-diff page (**33 assertions**, still identical for a file against itself), targeted runtime checks on the threshold, bond-order section and ring dedup (a three-membered ring contributes three angles, a four-membered ring four torsions, a single↔double bond is one order difference and 60→75° is 8.33%), and `tsc -p ./`.
- **2026-11-08 — A crystal contact selects the cell it was measured in, a skew cell's free radius is the nearest image, and the powder pattern names the wavelength it used** — A crystal H-bond row is measured from the hydrogen's image, which can sit a whole cell away from the donor, but the display mapping used only the donor image plus the acceptor offset; clicking the row could select the wrong acceptor image or claim the contact was outside the drawn supercell. The hydrogen's own image shift is now part of the path (`want` adds `hShift`), so the atoms selected are the atoms measured. The two σ-hole branches in the crystal preparation stored the substituent's image with the other branch's sign, so a cross-face X–D bond put D on the far side of X and the D–X···A angle was reported upside down; both signs are now the opposite of what they were, matching the bond record's own `atom2 is s cells from atom1` direction. `cavFreeRadius` rounded each fractional difference separately, which picks the nearest image only in an orthogonal cell; a triclinic cell's shortest image can carry a neighbouring integer offset and the reported `r_free` was too large. The rounded offset's 3×3×3 neighbourhood is enumerated and the shortest displacement wins (an orthogonal cell keeps the single rounded image). The powder XRD panel and the `.xy` export printed the radiation table's **weighted** Kα mean (1.54184 Å for Cu Kα) while the calculation placed every peak at the Kα1 line (1.54056 Å); the summary, the radiation list and the export now state the Kα1 wavelength the pattern was built from and name the Kα2 line separately. Verified by the cavity and powder-XRD suites (**135** and **213 assertions**), targeted runtime checks on the H-bond display path (a donor at home, a hydrogen one cell over and an acceptor two cells over selects the drawn acceptor) and on both σ-hole branches (+1/-1 keeps the bond record's direction), and the crystal-diff page.
- **2026-11-08 — MOL2, CIF and LAMMPS readers keep the identities their files state, and bond perception stops inventing multiple bonds** — MOL2 bonds name atoms by `atom_id`, but the reader subtracted one from the id and ignored the file's numbering, so non-contiguous ids (or a coordinate row that had to be skipped) joined the wrong atoms or ran past the array; an id→index map is built from the atom rows actually accepted, each id is resolved through it, a bond naming an id the file does not list is left out with a warning, and the `atom.index` counter now runs across `@<TRIPOS>ATOM` blocks instead of restarting (the map itself stays block-local, because a second MOLECULE block usually restarts its ids at 1). A CIF that wrote each symmetry operation as a repeated plain `_symmetry_equiv_pos_as_xyz` item kept only the last one and expanded the cell under a single operation; repeated symmetry items are appended (one operation per line) and all of them are applied. Two different sites at exactly the same coordinates were merged without a line, so the second site's occupancy vanished from the cell; a merge that folds a different site (exact or within the 0.1 Å tolerance) is now reported, naming both sites. Cell angles of zero or a negative/180° value were replaced by 90° without a warning; every non-positive or out-of-range angle is named in the warning, and a zero-volume lattice is reported as degenerate. A site whose element could not be read was silently made a real carbon; it is now the `X` dummy point and the count is reported. `lammpsLengthFactor` returned 1 for an unknown or missing `units`, which drew `nano`/`micro`/`si` coordinates as Å (10× to 1e10 too small) with no explanation; it now returns the real factor for every unit it knows and `undefined` for one it does not, and the data, dump and DCD paths warn when the run's stated unit is unknown and fall back to unscaled coordinates (a completely absent unit keeps the old unscaled default without a line). Bond perception's last-resort radius ratio classified Na–Cl as a double bond and Li–F as triple; without a tabulated length for the element pair it now returns a single bond. A solver run over a multi-fragment structure gave every fragment the whole structure's total charge, so a +1 two-fragment system solved each half as a cation; only a component that covers every atom inherits the total, and a fragment of a larger structure is solved as neutral. Verified by the CIF (**267**), crystal (**255**), crystal-edit (**155**), crystal-export (**209**) and ghost (**237**) suites, the trajectory/save checks, targeted parser runs (non-contiguous MOL2 ids and two MOLECULE blocks, repeated CIF scalar operations, the X fallback and angle warning, nano/si/unknown LAMMPS units, Na–Cl and Li–F as single bonds) and `tsc -p ./`.

- **2026-11-08 — A saved structure round-trips through PDB, MOPAC and LAMMPS again** — Three writers and one reader disagreed with the formats they name. **PDB** wrote every fixed column two characters early (x began at column 29 instead of 31, resName at 16 instead of 18, the element at 75 instead of 77), so a strict reader took the numbers as the previous field and the largest allowed coordinates lost their high digits; the records now follow the spec column by column (13-16, 18-20, 31-38, 77-78), and a coordinate that no longer fits its eight columns gives up decimals rather than pushing y into the gap (values past the format's own 9999.999 are clamped to it). `CONECT` was written in the stored bond direction while the reader accepts only `a1 < a2`, so a reversed bond disappeared on reopen; the pairs are now sorted and de-duplicated. **MOPAC** wrote the title first and `PM7 CHARGE= MS=` second, while the reader takes line 0 as keywords and line 1 as the title — so `CHARGE` and `MS` were lost on the round trip; the keyword line is first again. **LAMMPS data** wrote only `Atoms`/`Masses`, so a re-opened cell had no bonds at all: the writer now declares the bond count and bond types, writes the `Atoms # full` form that bonded data files require (molecule ID 1, charge 0.0), and adds a `Bonds # order` table whose type is the chemical order (1/2/3/4 = single/double/triple/aromatic) — the label is deliberate, because a force-field file's bond type is a coefficient index and must not be relabelled. `lammpsParser` reads the `Bonds` section back (first four columns, IDs resolved through the Atoms rows, duplicate pairs collapsed) and recovers the periodic shift by enumerating the rounded fractional offset and its 3×3×3 neighbourhood, so a bond across the cell face keeps its stub; without the `# order` label the rows carry order 0, which leaves the perceived orders untouched. The file bond table travels through `crystal.fileBonds`, so the page's own perception merges it the way it merges a CIF's `_geom_bond` (file orders win where the table speaks). Verified by the save/reopen suites and by the real writer's own output read back through the shipping parsers: four sites, three bonds (order 2 among them) and a `shift: [-1,0,0]` cross-cell bond come back, and a data file that declares more bonds than it lists now says so as a warning.

- **2026-11-08 — A late answer cannot write into a structure it was not computed from** — Four state paths could still act on the wrong structure. **Add H** checked only the atom count, so an answer computed from one optimisation frame or one VMD molecule landed on the same-numbered atoms of another; the request now carries a **request id** plus a **structure version** (edit counter, frame, molecule, atom count), the host echoes both, and `switchFrame`/`switchMolecule` void the request and clear the pending flag — a late reply neither adds atoms nor blocks a new request. **Rotate Group** read an undeclared `maxD` and threw a `ReferenceError` on Apply, so the axis line never appeared and the feature was unusable; the reach is now derived from `MD.atoms` at call time. **Batch Delete** did not stop a normal-mode playback, whose next tick rewrote coordinates by the old indices onto the renumbered array; the playback now ends before any index moves. **Molecular Mechanics** could be stepped by two timer chains after a Resume of a run that never paused; `MmRunState` carries a `scheduled` flag, `mmSchedulePump` refuses a second wake-up and clears the flag as the queued one runs, and a Resume joins the existing chain. A **Restore Initial** followed by a late `mmResult` used to apply the optimised coordinates over the restored geometry; `applyMmResult` drops any answer once `restored` is set. Verified by the provenance, ghost, crystal, crystal-edit, crystal-export, CIF and ADP suites (all green) plus the parser checks above.

- **An optimisation animation holds the molecule still, so what moves is the structure** — A step-by-step geometry sequence is one system changing shape, but the program also re-poses it: Gaussian prints the geometry it was handed in the **input orientation** and every later step in the **standard orientation** (principal axes), and a symmetry-constrained run (`Opt=Symm`, whose frame is re-derived from the point group at every step) turns the whole molecule over between steps. In `tetramethylammonium.log` the pose alone moves atoms by up to **4.105 Å** while the relaxation itself is only **0.343 Å** — played back, the tumbling is all that is visible. Every frame of a log is now **superimposed on the first one** by its best rigid fit: one rotation plus one translation, from the closed-form unit-quaternion solution (Horn) with the eigenvector found by cyclic Jacobi rotations, applied over the atoms in file order. The fitted geometry is what the page draws, measures and saves, so every reading of a frame describes the picture on screen. The motion is rigid — all 36 interatomic distances of the test cluster are preserved to **1e-9** — so bond perception, the convergence charts, Diff (which compares distances, angles and dihedrals) and every measurement are untouched, and because the fit maps the frame's centroid onto the first frame's, the rotation centre the page derives from the displayed atoms is the same on every frame: the view does not drift either. It is applied **once per frame**, at the moment the frame is put on screen (a revisit reads the same numbers), and it costs **14–25 ns per atom** — 0.28 ms for a 20 000-atom frame, 0.11 ms for the 1224-atom sequence in `xtbopt1.log` — because the element and coordinate guards on the hot path take the cheap branch (the obvious `toUpperCase` and `isFinite` spellings made it 26× slower, 350 of 370 ns per atom), and it is **skipped entirely** when it would move nothing (**< 1e-6 Å**), so a file already written in one orientation keeps its coordinates **bit for bit** — an ORCA run (`h2o.out`: 0 of 4 frames touched) and a Gaussian `nosymm` run (`695979_0_xo.log`: 0 of 4) are unchanged.
