Refused, deliberately: a molecular-dynamics trajectory (its box is the reference the motion is read against), a crystal, any frame that states a cell, a frame whose atom count or element list is not the first frame's (a log can hold several jobs), and a coordinate that is not a finite number — a damaged frame is left exactly as it is rather than filled with NaN. The same pass serves every program whose frames arrive as a log sequence, so an xTB optimisation that re-orients its frames is corrected by the same code with no per-format rule. Measured on real logs (pose RMS over all frames, before → after): `tetramethylammonium.log` **2.568 → 0.201 Å** (17 of 17 frames re-posed), `PEG600.log` **5.957 → 4.983** (81 of 81), `168791_1_opt1.log` **0.485 → 0.363** (8 of 138 — the rest were already aligned). Verified (`verify_frame_fix.js`, **41 assertions**, with `mutate_frame_fix.js` catching all **9** mutations): a rotated and translated copy of the first frame comes back onto it to 1e-9 with every distance preserved and the first frame's centroid; an already aligned frame keeps its numbers bit for bit; a revisit does not apply the transform twice; frame 1 of the file is never touched; a trajectory, a crystal, a cell-bearing frame, a shorter atom list, a differing element list, a NaN coordinate and a non-finite reference are all left alone; an element written in another case is still the same list; and the degenerate geometries (one atom, a line of atoms, coincident atoms, a mirror image) produce no NaN. On the **real page** (`probe_frame_page.js`, 11 checks, headless Edge, the 18 frames of `tetramethylammonium.log`): the centre is identical on every frame (7e-15), atom 0 moves **1.51 px** where the file's own poses would swing it across the view (**4.105 Å → 0.343 Å**), the second frame lands in the first frame's orientation to **6.6e-7 Å**, the radius of gyration still changes (1.861 → 1.916 Å), **Auto Play** holds the same picture, and a revisited frame gives bit-identical coordinates. `verify_autoplay.js` (50) and every other suite are unchanged and green.

- **A supercell view turns about its own centre, and Reset gives back the view it took** — Enlarging the bounds left the pivot on the small cell, so a 2×2×2 block swung around a corner of the cell it was built from instead of turning in place, and a 4×4×4 block swung around a point a quarter of the way in from its corner. The centre was the plain atom centroid of the displayed atoms, but the bounds path explicitly **kept** the previous centre while every routine re-centre (leaving Rotate Group, a deleted atom, an undo, the WebGL context coming back, a restyle) derived the displayed one — two rules for one point, which is why a restored cell could also come back turning about the enlarged block's centre. There is now one rule: **the centre is the centroid of the atoms on screen**, derived in one place (`recomputeRotationCentre`) and used by every path, and no rebuild holds an old point any more — the bounds door, a cell edit, a disorder switch and a symmetry change all re-derive from what they just built, and the one caller that keeps the picture still is a trajectory frame, whose box is the reference the motion is read against. The bounds door also re-frames the camera, so each set of bounds gets its own pivot and its own framing. Since the rebuild is deterministic, a round trip returns to the **exact** point and camera distance the earlier view had (measured difference 0.0 — bit-identical, not merely close), which is what makes "enlarge, then Reset" safe now that the centre follows the bounds. Re-framing uses the load's own formula (farthest atom plus the cell corners, `camDiffInit` in Diff), so a bigger block arrives filling the viewport the way a freshly opened one would; the view's orientation and pan are the user's and are left alone, and a molecule still centres on its atom centroid exactly as before. Measured on the real page in headless Edge with a probe that reads `CX/CY/CZ`, `camDist` and atom 0's pixel: **0..1** `C=(3.3691, 6.6268, 8.3630)`, `camDist 38.617` → **2×2×2** `C=(7.5047, 13.2537, 16.7260)`, `63.323` → **Reset** identical to every digit; 3×3×3 `(11.6403, 19.8805, 25.0890)` at `95.306` and 4×4×4 `(15.7758, 26.5074, 33.4519)` at `127.699`, each returning exactly; the −1..1 slab turns about its own point `(−0.7665, 0, 0)` and returns exactly; a view rotated 90×55 px comes back to the same pixel (`545.8839434892152, 252.7522944882066` before and after a 2×2×2 excursion), and leaving Rotate Group while the block is up leaves the pivot where the block put it. **24 probe checks**, and the crystal suite grows to **255 assertions** (section 20 rewritten: 0..1 equals the displayed centroid, 2×2×2 answers its own point, the derivation is idempotent, a rebuild of the same bounds reproduces the view, the round trip is exact, six further bound sets each answer their own centroid and return exactly — 3×3×3, 4×4×4, −1..1, a shifted 1..3 block, a single axis 0..2 and a half-cell slab — a centre left on another view is repaired, a molecule and an empty structure unchanged, and the source pins that keep one derivation, three call sites and a bounds door that re-derives). Every other suite is unchanged and green: display-style **268**, autoplay **50**, crystal-edit **155**, export **209**, add-H **58**, ADP **92**, ghost **237**, CIF **265**, symmetry-check **127**, VESTA **66**, BVS **168**, cavity **135**, lattice planes **53**, neighbours **64**, interactions **135**, space-group **173**, powder XRD **213** and its overlay **102**, aromatic dashes **2 870**, selection **48**, clipboard **102**, shortcuts **115**, provenance **78**, ORCA **164**, crystal diff **33**, pick styles **12**, view card **84**, atom instancing **16**, toolbar **192**, polyhedra page **58** and panel layout **925**.

- **A saved structure states its charge and spin multiplicity wherever the format can carry them** — Saving a structure read from an optimisation wrote the format's fallback electronic state instead of the structure's own: a Gaussian input got the fixed `0 1` (a log carries no `gjfMeta` to copy, and a stored header was emitted verbatim even after the state changed), so a doublet cation was saved as a neutral singlet and a later job would have computed a different system. The Gaussian writer now builds the charge/multiplicity line from the structure in **both** paths, and a structure read from a `.gjf` that still matches keeps its own line byte for byte — including the extra per-fragment pairs a multi-fragment input lists after the whole system's pair, which are preserved when the first pair is rebuilt. A Gaussian log's values are now read **per job** as the scan passes each `Charge = … Multiplicity = …` line, so a `--Link1--` file gives every frame its own job's state instead of stamping the first one onto all of them. Every frame (ORCA multi-`* xyz` files included) carries its charge and multiplicity to the page and a frame switch adopts them before bond perception and the info panel refresh, so the structure on screen — and anything saved from it — describes that frame's electronic state. **Turbomole coord** now uses each keyword's own definition: `$spin` is the number of unpaired electrons (a doublet is `$spin 1`; the writer now emits `mult−1` and the reader adds one) while `$mult` states the multiplicity itself — previously a saved radical round-tripped into a closed-shell system. **CP2K** inputs state the system's `CHARGE` and `MULTIPLICITY` in `&DFT`: the parser now reads both (a saved input no longer loses them), and the writer emits them only when they differ from CP2K's neutral-singlet defaults, so existing neutral files stay byte-identical. MOPAC, ORCA input and cluster exports already carried the state and are unchanged. Verified: `npm run compile` clean; `test/test_parser_audit.js` all checks green (new: per-job linked Gaussian state, `$spin`/`$mult` definitions, CP2K `CHARGE`/`MULTIPLICITY`); `test/test_save_roundtrip.js` 0 failures (new electronic-state section: a charged CP2K molecule reads back −1/2, a neutral one writes neither keyword, and the Gaussian line is built from the structure, kept verbatim when unchanged, rebuilt without losing fragment pairs, and defaulted only when the state is unknown); `.workbuddy/tools/verify_provenance.js` **78/78** assertions, including a log-derived save writing its own `-1 2` and a changed state rewriting only the first pair of a stored multi-fragment line.

- **The new readers now handle the format variants and damaged input they can meet** — a POSCAR's three-value scale block scales one lattice vector each (and Cartesian sites per axis) instead of applying the first number to all three; `vasprun.xml` spaces around `name = "basis"` no longer lose the basis and positions; ABACUS `LATTICE_PARAMETERS` (the five-parameter triclinic form) builds its cell and `Cartesian_au` positions are read in Bohr; ABINIT applies the stated length unit to `acell`/`xcart`/`xangst` and shows a cell-less Cartesian input as a molecule rather than inside a 1-Bohr cube; a MOPAC input's first line is read as its keywords (`CHARGE=`, `MS=`) with the title on line two; a truncated PySCF `New geometry` table no longer replaces the last complete geometry; LAMMPS data rows are read with their trailing comments stripped before the style guess; a LAMMPS script is recognised only with lowercase commands, so Gaussian's `Compute`/`Fix` section lines are not a script; VESTA's newer section headers (`SITET`, `SHAPE`, `CELLT`, …) end `STRUC` instead of being read as sites; an XTC compressed block that ends early fails its frame instead of yielding zeros; a DCD frame's time is `(ISTART + k·NSAVC) × DELTA`, a damaged DCD header opens with a warning rather than throwing, and a non-finite time is omitted from every binary frame. Verified: `test/test_parser_audit.js` grows to **25 checks** (from 17; the eight new ones cover POSCAR scaling, `vasprun.xml`, ABACUS parameters/Bohr, ABINIT units, MOPAC keywords, a truncated PySCF geometry, LAMMPS comments and a general-triclinic box), the synthetic DCD suite now asserts every decoded frame, its cell, the truncated file, damaged magic and a NaN `DELTA`, and the parser robustness, input-reference, LAMMPS-reference, 81-file crystal-example and save-round-trip suites stay green.

- **A trajectory's topology fallback is deterministic and never parses a dump as its own topology** — the folder scan iterated a directory map whose order depends on the filesystem, so two same-named hint candidates could change which `units` and type labels were applied; script and candidate names are now sorted. The same-atom-count topology fallback also parsed every candidate, including a multi-hundred-MB dump; a candidate over **64 MB** is skipped, and the single-candidate and ambiguity rules are unchanged. Opening a binary trajectory no longer decodes its bytes through a text string before discarding them, and the dump trajectory/crystal decision uses the file's stated frame count rather than the sampled list, so a very long dump sampled down to one frame is still a trajectory. Verified by the trajectory document suite (the Si dump pairs `data.si`, water XTC/TRR pair `water.gro`, and the single-frame dump still takes the crystal path) and the LAMMPS-reference suite.

- **The trajectory's cell wireframe holds still while the frames play** — Each frame switch rebuilt the scene around that frame's own atom centroid, and the rotate-group teardown centred it a second time, so a drifting MD system made the box slide around the viewport (up to 4 Å between frames in `water.xtc`) even though the box is the reference the motion is read against. A trajectory now keeps the centre the first frame was framed with: the teardowns that run before the swap put that centre back, the rotate-group teardown skips its recentre, and the new frame is built against it; ordinary optimisation frame sequences still follow their structure's centre. Verified on the real page: in `water.xtc` the centre and every box vertex are identical across all 11 frames (`CX=4.5192, CY=4.6296, CZ=4.43`), while atom 0 moves from (1.50, 1.50, 1.50) to (5.25, 0.28, 3.02) and every wrapped bond piece stays ≤0.505 Å; the triclinic `mg_hcp.xtc` box is identical across its 11 frames with atoms moving in 10, no piece longer than 2.13 Å, and the real **Auto Play** button advances frames with the box unchanged.

- **A bond across a trajectory frame's boundary is drawn as the short stub a crystal draws** — A trajectory document is a molecule list with a box, not a crystal, so it has no bond shifts, and its atoms are wrapped into each frame's cell: two bonded atoms that landed on opposite faces were joined straight across the whole box instead of toward each other's periodic image. The far end of every bond is now resolved at draw time against the frame's own box, taking the nearest periodic image of the second atom — the same 27-repeat enumeration the crystal bond search uses, so a skewed triclinic cell is handled rather than trusting the rounded fractional difference — and the bond is drawn as the short stub that leaves one face and enters the other, exactly as a crystal split bond is. The inverse cell is cached per frame cell, and the aromatic dash planner reads the same resolved endpoint, so dashes follow the corrected line. Verified on the real page: in `water.xtc` frame 9 an O–H pair 12.36 Å apart across the box is drawn 0.996 Å long, five wrapped bonds get 1.0 Å stubs, no drawn bond piece is longer than 0.505 Å, and frame 0 plus the direct `.gro` view are unchanged; across all 11 frames of the triclinic `mg_hcp.xtc` example (**4598** bond-frame pairs) the ±1 search picks the same image as a brute-force ±4 scan every time (the longest raw pair spanned 23.481 Å while every drawn bond stayed within 4.256 Å, no disagreements), and the page drew no bond piece longer than 2.13 Å in any frame.

- **An output that shares its suffix is read by what wrote it, not by the suffix** — `.out`/`.log` are written by every electronic-structure code, and `.in`/`.inp`/`.dat` are shared by inputs, outputs and data files. A **MOPAC** optimisation ends with a `CARTESIAN COORDINATES` table whose rows put a serial number before the element (`1 C -4.1918 2.5617 1.3518`); the input reader skipped every such row, and the `.out`/`.log` path sent the file to the Gaussian/ORCA reader, so the final geometry was lost behind "no structure frame". MOPAC output is now recognised by its version banner and Cartesian table, the **last** table is the structure (the earlier ones are intermediate geometries), serial-numbered rows, element symbols, atomic numbers and dummy `X`/`XX` are read, and the same reader serves `.out`, `.log` and an output renamed to `.in`/`.inp`/`.dat`. An **xTB** run log ends with a `final structure:` XYZ block; it is now read as the final geometry (the comment/version lines after the atom count are skipped by row shape, not by counting lines), while `xtbopt.log`/`xtb.trj` trajectories keep the frame pipeline. In the `.in`/`.inp` branch, structured and log outputs are sniffed **before** the ORCA/Gaussian input readers: a **CP2K output** is no longer read as its own echoed `&GLOBAL`/`&COORD` input (its `isCp2kInput` now yields to the output banner), and Quantum ESPRESSO, PySCF, ABINIT, GROMACS, MOPAC, xTB and Gaussian/ORCA outputs, plain XYZ and Turbomole `$coord` renamed to either suffix open with their own reader. A LAMMPS **data** file named `.dat` is data, not an empty MOPAC input, and the host now reads the sibling LAMMPS scripts in that case too, so its units and type labels are not lost. Verified (`test/test_parser_robustness.js`): synthetic MOPAC/xTB outputs open under five and four suffixes respectively and choose the final block; no corpus file is claimed by either detector; CP2K outputs are not CP2K inputs; the corpus outputs (QE, PySCF, ABINIT, CP2K, GROMACS) still open as `.in` and now as `.inp`; an xTB trajectory stays off the run-log path; and the full corpus still parses without throwing.

- **A VESTA site table is read to its end, and a POSCAR counts its own sites** — Two readers could lose sites a file plainly states. VESTA ended `STRUC` at the first blank line, so a file with a blank between two sites showed only the sites above it; a name column carrying an oxidation state (`Fe3+`, `O2-`) failed the bare-symbol test and took the whole table with it; and a damaged row was treated as the end of the section. The reader now treats a blank line as whitespace, normalises the name column (`Fe3+` → Fe, `O2-` → O, VESTA's `X`/`Bq`/`Du` → the dummy element X, `D`/`T` kept as the isotope the file states), skips a damaged row with one `STRUC row skipped` warning instead of dropping every site below it, and quietly steps over the numeric detail lines so they are not counted as sites. `SYMOP` now ends at the next section keyword even when a file omits its `-1 -1 -1` terminator (which used to walk into the following sections), and accepts an operation written without VESTA's trailing kind column (12 numbers). In a POSCAR, a zero-count element between two non-zero ones advanced the type counter on the *next* row, so a `1 0 1` cell lost the second element's atom; the zero-count types are now stepped over before the coordinates are assigned, and a coordinate table shorter than the counts line reports "declares N sites but only M coordinate rows were readable" instead of drawing the part that happened to fit. The zero-volume warning a negative target volume pushes is also kept beside the degenerate-cell message instead of being replaced by it. Verified (`verify_vesta.js`, 11 new assertions: blank line, charged and dummy names, damaged row, 12-number SYMOP, missing terminator; `verify_crystal_export.js`, 6.40–6.45: zero counts in the middle and at the end, and the truncated-table warning).

- **A file's own bond table follows its atoms through edits, an undo and a comparison** — `_geom_bond` rows are keyed by the expanded base-cell indices. Deleting a site (single or batch) or removing low-occupancy disorder sites renumbered `baseAtoms` and `baseBonds` but left `CRY.fileBonds` on the old numbering, and an undo restored the sites without restoring the table; the merge that reads the table could then point at the wrong atoms. Every renumbering path now passes both lists through one remap that drops a bond whose end is gone and keeps its order, periodic shift and stated valence, the undo snapshot carries `fileBonds` with `bondSource` and `bondsDetected`, and `doUndo()` restores them with the cell. In a crystal comparison the right-hand cell also merges the compared file's own table (the payload used to omit it), so both halves read `_geom_bond` by the same rule. Verified (`verify_crystal_edit.js`, section 8, **14 assertions**): the remap drops, renumbers and preserves shift/valence, undo restores the table and its counts, and the delete paths, snapshot and restore are pinned in the source.

## [1.0.4] - 2026-10-31

### Added

- **The saved title names an edited cell, the disorder group on screen and its occupancy choice — and the occupancy switch now rides the undo snapshot** — Editing the cell, switching the disorder alternative and toggling **Selected group fully occupied** changed what every export writes but left no `MV:` clause, so a file saved after them described a structure different from the one the title traced. A new stateful provenance kind (`provSet`) records the **cell parameters** the edit applied ("cell edited to 6.000 x 6.000 x 6.000 A, 90.0/90.0/90.0 deg"), the **group shown** ("disorder group 2 shown") and the **occupancy choice** ("disorder sites counted at full occupancy"), replacing its own earlier clause instead of stacking ("2 x cell edited" would be wrong), and choosing All or switching the choice off takes the clause back. The same switch is now part of the undo snapshot, so an undo restores it with the group view it belongs to. Verified (`verify_provenance.js`, **76 assertions**, from 67): the latest cell is stated once, the two disorder clauses read in order, the unmark path removes the occupancy clause, an undo restores the old cell detail from the deep copy, and the wiring of `applyCellEdit`, the disorder select and the occupancy switch is pinned against the source.

- **The Interactions panel finds π–π stacking and CH–π contacts, in a molecule and through a crystal's cell faces** — The panel's four point-to-point types now stand beside **π–π** and **CH–π**, each with its own checkbox, count, tag, colour and dashed guide. Aromatic rings are perceived from the same bond graph the rest of the viewer uses: chordless 5–7 cycles found once from their smallest atom with chord pruning and a work budget, kept only when planar within 0.30 Å and either every edge carries the perceived aromatic order 1.5 or — for a file that states only single bonds — a planar 5/6-ring whose full degree is at most 3 and whose element-pair lengths fit a Kekulé alternation or an all-short-single ring; saturated sp3 rings fail the length bands (cyclopentane 1.54 Å C–C, THF 1.43 Å C–O) and a 2.6 Å closest-atom floor keeps bonded and fused rings from being called a stack. **π–π**: plane angle ≤ 30°, centroid distance ≤ 4.5 Å, face gap 2.8–3.8 Å, slip ≤ 2.5 Å and closest atom contact 2.6–3.9 Å — **P** below a 1.2 Å slip, **PD** above; a **T**-shaped pair needs planes ≥ 60° apart, centroid distance ≤ 5.5 Å, one centroid 3.0+ Å above the other ring's face and within 2.5 Å laterally, and a contact ≤ 4.0 Å. **CH–π**: H···centroid < 3.0 Å and C–H···centroid > 120°, the H at least 1.0 Å above the ring plane with its projection inside the ring radius + 0.8 Å; an H or C bonded into the ring never counts. For a crystal the same search runs on `baseBonds` with each bond's image shift, so a ring wrapped across a cell face closes as one ring and a ring stacked on its own image one cell away is found as the ±t pair it is; ring pairs are enumerated in fractional space behind a plane-width lower bound, self-images are canonicalised, and CH–π walks a wrapped fractional grid of the hydrogens and their C–H image instead of testing every H against every ring. A row names compact rings (`ring(C1,C2,C3+3)`) and keeps both full atom lists and image offsets in its tooltip; a click selects every drawn atom of both sites in one translated cell, and a row whose image is outside the drawn supercell says so rather than selecting the wrong atoms. Verified on the real page (`verify_interactions.js`, **123 assertions**, up from 43): two stacked benzene dimers give one **P** at 3.536 Å and one **PD** at 3.981 Å with both toggles independent; a T-shaped pair gives one **T** at 4.7 Å/90° plus the two edge C–H···face contacts at 2.843 Å/124.3°; a 2.5 Å/180° CH–π counts while an off-target C–H on the same methane is rejected; planar sp3 rings (degree 4) give nothing; crystals give a **PD** at 3.874 Å selecting 12 atoms, a self-image **P** at 3.6 Å labelled `[0,0,1]`, a wrapped-face pair at 3.684 Å and a CH–π at 2.5 Å/180° selecting 8 atoms. The interaction, toolbar (**192**), panel-layout (**780**), display-style (**268**), view-card (**84**) and neighbours (**64**) suites are all green, and compile and lint are clean.

- **The unit cell reports its voids: how many, how large, and where** — The crystal panel gains **Cavities...**, a report of the space a probe sphere can reach inside one unit cell. A probe of the chosen radius (default 1.2 Å, 0–20 Å) inflates every visible van der Waals atom; the clearance field is sampled at 0.25 Å (at most 128 divisions per axis, 128³ = 2 097 152 voxels, the whole budget) and the free voxels are split into six-connected regions that wrap through the cell faces. A region that contains a path winding once around the crystal is an **open channel**; a region whose lift never winds is a **closed cavity**, including one split across a face or centred on a cell corner, whose unwrapped centroid is reported in fractional coordinates. Volumes are **per unit cell** whatever supercell is on screen, and every row lists its volume, centre and **r_free** — the largest sphere that fits at its deepest point, found with exact minimum-image distances rather than the far-field stand-in the isosurface interpolates. Clicking a row extracts that region's f = 0 isosurface with the same marching tetrahedra the VMD surfaces use and draws it as one translucent lavender mesh (opacity 0.30, `depthWrite` off); a region past 150 000 free voxels reports its volume and declines to draw rather than freezing the tab. The analysis is read-only — no undo step, no coordinate change — and it drops itself when the cell, an edit or a live drag makes it stale. Verified (`.workbuddy/tools/verify_cavity.js`, **114 assertions**): a single atom's free volume against the analytic cell-minus-sphere value, dense rock salt at **zero** cavities and zero channels, an 84-atom cage against an independent full-scan flood fill (±2%) with its centre, r_free and extracted surface radius, the same cage translated half a cell (same volume, centre at the corner), the 128-division cap, a skew cell whose voxels tile it exactly, the disorder filter, and supercell invariance. The floating-panel suite grows to **780 assertions** with the report's fold/restore round trip.

- **Every floating card folds into a chip, and the chip puts it back** — The crystal panel, the View and Edit Coordinates cards and the Select Atoms, Neighbours and Rotate Group panels now carry the same **×** in their header: it hides the card and leaves a small named chip at that card's own screen edge (**Crystal**, **View**, **Coordinates**, **Select Atoms**, **Neighbours**, **Rotate Group**). Clicking the chip restores the card at the same place and size with every control and its contents intact — the gesture the MM Optimize, Convergence, Modes, Route and Diff results cards already offered, now shared by all the remaining cards. The powder XRD × folds the pattern into its **Powder XRD** chip (the crystal panel's button and the chip both reopen it with the radiation and range it was left on), and the egg list's × folds it into its **Eggs** chip. A chip follows its card's mode exactly — the View, Coordinates, Select, Neighbours and Rotate chips step aside when their mode closes — while the crystal card's chip stays reachable in **every** mode, so a card folded away in one tool can be brought back in another. Chips are layout participants, not decorations: the same pass that places the cards places them, so a chip never lands under a panel or another chip. Verified on the **real page** with a new suite (`verify_panel_layout.js`, **693 assertions** across 1400×900, 1100×800, 1000×760, 800×650, 700×600, 620×620 and 560×540): every card's ×→chip→card round trip returns the exact rectangle and keeps the card's controls alive, two chips stand together without touching, and the crystal chip restores its card from the Select Atoms mode.

- **The unit cell now has a powder X-ray pattern, and the pattern is computed from the cell** — The crystal panel gains **Powder XRD Pattern…**: pick the radiation (Cu Kα1/Kα, Mo Kα1/Kα, Co Kα1, Fe Kα1, Cr Kα1, Ag Kα1), the highest 2θ (60/90/120/150°), and the panel draws the curve, lists the strongest lines and exports the drawn curve as `.xy`. Nothing is looked up: every (h k l) in the reciprocal sphere is enumerated — one loop, index ≤ 40, at most 20 000 reflections — each reflection gets a structure factor over the cell's own atoms with the ITC Vol. C Cromer–Mann coefficients, reflections that share a 2θ add in the same bin, and the merged lines are broadened with a Caglioti variance (U = 0.004, V = −0.002, W = 0.008) on a 0.02° grid and normalised to 100. A systematic absence is therefore arithmetic, not a rule applied: bcc's (100) and fcc's (200) are zeros the atoms cancel to. An element outside the coefficient table is left out of the pattern and named in the panel's note (deuterium reads as hydrogen); the pattern belongs to the **unit cell**, so the supercell bounds do not change it; × or Escape closes the panel. Verified in a real page (`verify_xrd.js`, **117 assertions**): the engine against an independent brute-force powder calculation for simple cubic, bcc, fcc and a triclinic cell, peak for peak, plus the controls, peak list, export, reopening the panel, Escape and the narrow-window layout.

- **Lattice planes: a (h k l) spacing calculator that also draws the plane** — The crystal panel gains **Lattice Planes (h k l)**: two integer triples, the d-spacing of the first read from the reciprocal lattice, and the acute angle between the two plane normals. **(0 0 0)** is reported as `-`, not as a division by zero, and a cell with no volume says so. **Show Plane** draws the plane through the centre of the drawn box as a translucent blue sheet (opacity 0.22, `depthWrite` off, so it never hides an atom) with a solid border, cut exactly to the box the bounds define; the plane follows a bounds change and a rebuild, and is removed in Diff. Verified (`verify_hkl.js`, **53 assertions**): d-spacings and angles against hand-known orthorhombic, hexagonal and triclinic cells, the polygon against the box, the page's readouts and Show/Hide round trip (screenshot hashes), the zero-index case, and the narrow-window layout.

- **Neighbours: what is around an atom, nearest first** — A new **Neighbours** mode answers the contact question directly: click an anchor atom and the panel lists everything within a radius (default 3.5 Å, 0.1–12 Å), nearest first, with **skip bonded** on by default. For a crystal the search runs in fractional space through integer lattice images (the image count sized by the cutoff, not guessed), so a contact across a cell face is found even when that image is not drawn; each row carries the image's `cell (i,j,k)` offset, and a row whose image lies outside the drawn supercell says so instead of selecting the wrong atom. Clicking a listed row selects the anchor and that atom, **Select listed** takes the whole shell, and drawn contacts get dashed amber guide lines. An atom hidden by the disorder filter is not offered as a contact. The mode closes cleanly (Escape, View) and leaves no panel or guides behind. Verified in a real page (`verify_neighbours.js`, **64 assertions**): the six images outside a one-atom cubic cell, 142619's C1 shell to 4 Å compared row for row with an independent enumeration, clicking a drawn and an outside row, a radius inside the bonds, and the mode's cleanup.

- **A fourth display style: coordination polyhedra** — **Display → Polyhedra** (offered on a crystal page, where a coordination shell exists) keeps the ball-and-stick picture and adds the coordination hull of every metal or metalloid centre: translucent faces in the centre's colour with darkened edges, all centres merged into one geometry (faces and edges are two draws, not two per centre). The hull is the convex hull of the neighbour positions; coplanar vertices are collected per face and fanned, so a square face of an octahedron is two triangles, not four overlapped ones. The style only draws where it says something: a centre needs at least four neighbours and at least one non-centre neighbour, and a planar shell, an all-metal shell, a ghost centre/neighbour or too many centres (> 8000) falls back to ball and stick; a shell wider than 24 neighbours is trimmed to its nearest points, and a periodic bond's image is placed by its own lattice shift before it enters the hull. The comparison's right half has the same hull path in its own layer, but a crystal comparison's right-hand cell carries no periodic bonds, so choosing Polyhedra during Diff is refused and both halves stay in one style. Nothing else changes: picking is still the analytic ray–sphere test, and the hull is translucent with `depthWrite` off, so it cannot hide an atom or steal a click. Measured in a real engine: a 7-atom FeO6 octahedron costs exactly **two more draw calls** than ball and stick, and removing the style puts the frame back. Verified (`verify_display_style.js`, now **248 assertions**, and a real-engine `verify_polyhedra_page.js`, **17 assertions**): tetrahedron/octahedron/cube face and edge counts, per-vertex colours and opacity, the fallbacks, periodic image placement, 24-neighbour trimming, rebuild replacement and disposal, the diff right half, and the draw-call/selection round trip.

- **Edit Cell: the box can change while every site keeps its fractional coordinates** — The cell panel's **Edit Cell…** opens a six-parameter dialog (a, b, c, α, β, γ). Applying it validates that the lengths are positive, the angles lie strictly between 0° and 180°, and the three vectors enclose a nonzero volume; then every base site's fractional coordinates are held fixed while its Cartesian position is recomputed against the new lattice, the base bonds keep their fractional shifts (the same two sites in the same neighbouring cells are still joined), the picture, panel and exports read the new cell, and the change is one undo step. A cell that would build more than `MAX_CRYSTAL_ATOMS` in the current bounds is refused before anything is built, with the estimate and the limit named. Verified (`verify_crystal_edit.js`, **137 assertions**, of which the Edit Cell section is 37): a 5% longer *a* moves the along-*a* bond and leaves the across one alone, the fractional sites are invariant, an invalid length/angle/NaN is refused, the ceiling applies, and the button is wired.

- **Disorder alternatives are a view switch, not a deletion** — A CIF site that names a disorder group (`_atom_site_disorder_group`) is now kept as an alternative: the cell panel gains an **All / Group n** select, and choosing a group draws (and saves, and counts) the ordered part plus that group only. `CRY` keeps every site at its own index, so switching back to All loses nothing, an edit still commits to the site it names, and the selection travels in the undo snapshot and the view state. A group that disappears after an edit falls back to All, and a file with no disorder shows a disabled All. Exports follow the view: CIF, POSCAR, VESTA and cube write the visible alternative and its formula. Verified in the crystal suite's disorder section (**29 assertions**) and the export suite's section 7 (7.1–7.15): group listing, filtering, the cell retaining all sites, the removed-group fallback, the disabled empty state, the snapshot, and each format's atom and formula counts.

- **Every image can name the asymmetric-unit site and the operation that placed it** — An atom expanded from a CIF or VESTA asymmetric unit now carries its file site label and the index of the operation that produced the image, and the tooltip shows both (`site C1 (op #3: -x+1/2, y, -z+1/2)`) beside the fractional coordinates, image and occupancy. The structure panel gains a collapsed **Asymmetric unit** table — site, element, occupancy, multiplicity and the first three operations, with the rest counted — so the file's own list can be checked against the expanded cell. POSCAR atoms (no symmetry) simply have no operation to name. Verified in the CIF suite's provenance section (**14 assertions**): one row per `_atom_site` row, the identity and special-position operation lists, the expansion reproducing the cell, and the tooltip/table showing the same trace.

- **The Bond Length dialog says which periodic image it is measuring** — In a crystal the two atoms a reader picks can be two images rather than the two ends of one bond; the distance on screen is what the dialog edits, but the structure's own distance is the shortest periodic image of that displacement. The dialog now shows a read-only **Shortest image: X Å** row, and appends **"these two images are not the nearest pair of their atoms"** when the drawn pair is not that image. Editing semantics are untouched — the cross-face stub is still refused by the same dialogs. Verified by the bond-dialog assertions (`verify_crystal_edit.js`, 8.10a/b): a nearest pair reports 1.5000 Å with no warning, and a farther image pair names the shortest distance.

- **A space-group name alone now supplies the operations it names** — A CIF that states only `_symmetry_space_group_name_H-M` used to be read as P1, showing one asymmetric unit as the whole cell. A Hall-symbol table (International Tables Vol. B, Table A1.4.2.7 — 230 groups, 530 settings including origin and rhombohedral/hexagonal choices) now produces the operations by closing the group, with translations carried in exact 1/24ths and spelled through the same helper the file readers use. The table is consulted **only** when no operation tag exists: a file that states its own operations is still the authority, and a file whose operation tag is present but unreadable is reported as P1 instead of being given another group's operations. The info panel says when the operations came from the table (`(ops from built-in table)`). Verified (`verify_space_group.js`, **173 assertions**): every row decodes with the right order and proper count, every setting is reachable and belongs to one group, the name spellings (settings, origins, axes, reductions, junk), special-position multiplicities in known structures, and the rhombohedral settings against their hexagonal counterparts; the CIF suite adds 51 assertions for the reader path.

- **A CIF's own bond table is read — with its orders and its periodic images** — A `_geom_bond` loop (`_geom_bond_atom_site_label_1/2`, optional `_geom_bond_site_symmetry_1/2`, `_geom_bond_order`, `_geom_bond_valence`) now supplies the connectivity the depositor refined. Each endpoint's site-symmetry code is applied to the site, the resulting position is matched to an image already in the expanded cell (round-off tolerance), and the pair is stored in the cell's own form — `atom1 < atom2` plus the integer image shift — so a bond across a face is the right length and the right image. A stated order (1–3) or valence wins; an unstated one leaves the bond to the perception module exactly as a file with no table would, rather than calling everything single. Duplicate labels, a label the atom list does not have, an unknown symmetry code, an image missing from the cell and a self-bond are each reported with a count and an example. Verified in the crystal suite's bond-table section (**47 assertions**): stated orders win and unstated ones do not, the shift points at the right image, and each malformed row produces its warning.

- **The crystal verification suite now pins the whole chain, and a mutation harness proves every pin is load-bearing** — `verify_crystal.js` grows from 80 to **137 assertions** and covers the rules that were only described in code comments before: the **minimum image against a 27-image exhaustive reference** (80 random triclinic cells whose nearest image lies inside the cutoff, shift and distance equal to the brute force, plus one fixed skewed cell where rounding answers 4.423 Å instead of the true 3.207 Å; an orthogonal cell still answers exactly what rounding did), **fractional boundaries** (one site on a face is one image; the wrapped image below the cell; a slab with neither image; two cells hold two images), **unwrap completing every molecule** (294 atoms, no face-cut bond, Z = the finite components), the **CIF asymmetric unit → full cell → P1 save round trip** (2 sites × 2 operations = 4 atoms; saved as P1 with one operation and the audit line; read back 4 atoms, not 8; a second round keeps 4 and the composition), **CIF/POSCAR consistency** (the same three sites and cartesian positions from both writers against the same cell), the **supercell ceiling** (the full ±5 range refuses before anything is built, names 294000 atoms and the 20000 limit, leaves the bounds and six inputs alone; a two-cell range goes through the steady-centre rebuild), the **undo snapshot volume** (a 2×2×2 snapshot is byte-identical to a 1×1×1 one, no displayed atom or bond in it, the displayed supercell many times larger), and the **added-site wrap** (the cell bond carries the image shift; the wrapped site is drawn once with the requested bond length; no unshifted bond spans the cell). A new `mutate_crystal.js` applies **9 plausible mistakes** to the compiled page — the minimum image rounded (3 assertions fail), the upper face closed again (7), whole molecules not copied (14), the CIF writing part of the cell (5), the POSCAR writing part (2), the supercell ceiling removed (7), the undo snapshot storing the display (3), the added bond's shift dropped (1), the added site's wrap dropped (1) — and all 9 are caught. The boundary fix and the two format mutations are each caught by the assertion written for them, so none of the new checks is vacuous.

- **The region on screen can be saved as a molecule — the cluster a QM job starts from** — A crystal could only be saved *as a crystal*: every one of its four formats writes the cell, so the cage, pore or fragment a quantum-chemistry run needs had no way out of the viewer except deleting atoms by hand. Save As now offers two **cluster formats** for crystal structures, **XYZ − displayed cluster** and **Gaussian Input − displayed cluster**, which write the atoms on screen as a molecule instead of as a periodic cell. The title line is the file's record of where it came from: the source file, the **cell parameters** (a/b/c and the three angles), the **supercell bounds** that cut it (`bounds a[0,1] b[0,1] c[0,1]`), which whole-molecule rule produced it and how many atoms it holds — so a cage cut out of a framework is traceable back to the region it came from, and the wording rides the existing provenance line rather than a second one. A **Keep whole molecules** switch decides how the bounds cut: on (the default) the displayed molecules are written complete, exactly as the builder draws them, so a molecule whose centre is inside the bounds keeps the atoms that lean outside it; off, atoms whose displayed fractional position is outside the bounds are dropped and a bond that crosses a cell face is not written at all (it is the stub the renderer draws towards an image the file does not contain, and writing it as a bond between two atoms of the cluster would invent a length across the cell). The GJF gets the same default route header a fresh structure gets (a crystal has no Gaussian route of its own to preserve), the charge and multiplicity the structure carries, GaussView-aligned coordinates and a connect section with **one line per atom and one entry per bond**, the convention the molecular GJF writer already uses; the XYZ keeps the viewer's own `X`/element spelling. The two options only exist where they mean something — the formats appear only for crystal data, and the switch and the cube-grid control appear only while their own format is selected — and a molecular structure's dialog is byte-for-byte what it was. Verified against the **compiled page** with a new suite (`.workbuddy/tools/verify_crystal_export.js`, **123 assertions**): a synthetic cell of two molecules, one of them straddling a face and drawn with its carbon outside the box, exports **4 atoms and 2 bonds whole** and **3 atoms cut at the bounds** with the correct atoms surviving; the title carries the cell, the bounds, the rule and the count and stays inside the 190-character cap; the GJF's connect section carries the bond of the straddling molecule; CCDC 142619 exports its 294 displayed atoms (and 2352 at 2×2×2) with the bounds of the view; a molecular structure still gets no cluster formats and an unchanged XYZ.

- **Whole molecules at the cell boundary — the crystal builder now unwraps every component and copies it complete** — A CIF wraps each atom into the cell before the viewer ever sees it, so a molecule straddling a face owns a bond that crosses that face, and the old grouping test — "does any bond of this component cross a face?" — put such a molecule on the periodic shelf: drawn as fragments cut at the boundary, with stub bonds pointing at images that never arrive. On CCDC 142619 that was **every one of its ten molecules**, and none was ever seen whole. The grouping now asks the question that actually separates a molecule from an infinite chain: **are the component's bond cycles consistent?** Each component is walked once with a periodic-image offset per atom (the unwrap), and a cycle that comes back to a shifted image marks the whole component genuinely periodic — that is what diamond and a polymer chain are, and they keep the old per-atom boundary behaviour and their face-cut bonds. Everything else is a **finite molecule**, and it is copied whole: each image whose **unwrapped centroid** falls inside the supercell bounds is placed complete, atoms outside the bounds included, so the boundary cuts nothing and splits no bond. The test is half-open on the upper face, so a centrosymmetric molecule sitting exactly on the boundary is one image, not two; a copy margin constant (`CRYSTAL_COPY_MARGIN`, default 0, convertible per axis to an Angstrom expansion) is there for when a slab should keep the molecules that only just lean out. Each placed atom records **which periodic image of its base site it is** (`cellI/J/K`, exact integers from the unwrap), the field the selection remap and the tooltip build on. The bounds-remap identity is pinned: every atom shown at 0..1 exists at 0..2 under the same `(baseIdx, cell)` key at the same cartesian position, so a selection recorded before a bounds change finds the same atom after it.

- **Supercell bounds gain presets, Reset and Align Origin — and changing the bounds no longer jumps the view or loses the selection** — The panel's six inputs stood alone: no way to ask for the standard 2×2×2, no way back to the unit cell, and every change re-computed the structure centroid, so the whole picture leapt sideways exactly when the user was comparing before and after. **One door now serves every way the bounds change** — the six inputs, the **2×2×2 / 3×3×3 / 4×4×4 presets** (the origin-aligned 0..N supercell, so 2×2×2 is eight cells, not nine), **Reset** (back to 0..1) and **Align Origin** (same spans, low corner moved onto the lattice origin) — and the door does three things in order. It **guards the size** (below). It **keeps the old rotation centre** (`rebuildScene(true)`): the atoms the previous bounds already showed keep their exact screen positions and the new material appears around them, instead of the camera's framing being redrawn around a new centroid. And it **remaps the selection by lattice identity**: each selected atom is recorded as its base site plus image cell, and after the rebuild the same identity is looked up in the new atom list — the selection still points at the same atoms, and only the images the new bounds no longer contain drop out of it. The **cell wireframe draws the subdivision**, not just the outer shell: every integer plane between the bounds becomes a division line (quieter than the box), so a 3×3×3 supercell shows its 27 cells, while 0..1 yields exactly the twelve edges it always drew; the three edges meeting at the low corner are labelled **a, b, c** as sprites in the axes indicator's colours, so the wireframe and the corner gizmo cannot disagree about which direction is which. A bounds change is still a property of the view, not an edit: no undo step, and the save-time provenance reads the bounds as before.

- **A supercell that would be too big is refused before it is built** — The bounds inputs clamp to ±5, so a large structure at the full range could be asked to build `baseAtoms × 1000` spheres — minutes of mesh building for a picture nobody asked for. `applyBoundary` now estimates `baseAtoms × cells` (the same count the per-atom pass would enumerate) and refuses anything past **`MAX_CRYSTAL_ATOMS` = 20 000** — the same ceiling the clipboard refuses fragments over — with a dialog that says what the bounds would build and what the limit is. The refusal changes nothing: the structure, the camera and the six inputs (which keep showing the bounds still in force) are exactly as they were, and the same guard covers the presets, so no button can walk around it.

- **A crystal atom's tooltip and the structure panel now say what it is** — The hover tooltip gave an element, a display index and a Cartesian position — for a crystal that is half the answer. A crystal atom's tooltip now also carries its **fractional coordinates**, **which periodic image it is** (`cell (i,j,k)` — so the different images of one site are finally tellable apart), the file's own **site label** (`C12`, `O1W` — the CIF parser now records, per expanded atom, the `atom_site` label it was expanded from, and the label travels on the atom through deletion and disorder removal), and the **occupancy**. A molecular structure's tooltip is byte-for-byte what it always was — the extra fields simply do not exist there, and the comparison's right half is excluded too. The **structure panel** gains the crystallographic constants of the unit cell itself: the **volume** (the lattice determinant), **Z** — the number of whole molecules the cell contains, which is exactly what the unwrap pass already counted; a cell whose only component is an infinite network has no meaningful Z, and the line stays out rather than showing a number that would be one — and the **density** implied by the cell's occupancy-weighted mass over its volume, from a standard-atomic-weight table the page carries beside the atomic numbers; an element the table does not know leaves the line out rather than guessing a mass. Every line reads only what the page already has, so a panel driven on a stub crystal without a lattice shows none of them and the molecular layout is unchanged.

  Verified against the **compiled** page — `rebuildCrystal`, `detectCrystalBaseBonds` and `computeCrystalInv` cut out of it and driven against real CIF fixtures — **80 assertions**: 142619 at 0..1 showing one atom per base site with no cut bond and its displayed molecules equal to the base molecules size for size (no fusing, no splitting); image accounting at 0..2 (8 whole images per molecule, 8× the base bonds); a synthetic molecule wrapped across a face copied whole into one image (and a slab holding none of its centre drawing no fragment of one); diamond and a polymer chain staying per-atom per cell with their split bonds and no Z; the bounds-remap identity at 0..1 → 0..2; the wireframe's twelve edges at 0..1 and fifteen division lines at 2×2×2; the panel lines (volume, Z, density — and none of them invented for a lattice-less stub or an unknown element); the site label travelling from the CIF through the payload; and the refusal path restoring the six inputs. The existing suites are unchanged and green: 140 CIF (with 20/20 mutations caught), 157 display-style, 237 ghost, 2 870 aromatic-dash, 101 clipboard, 67 provenance, 192 toolbar, 48 selection, 105 keyboard, 44 autoplay, 159 ORCA, 80 view-card and 12 pick-style assertions, and the CIF baseline snapshot's atom arrays are byte-identical.

- **The asymmetric unit a CIF lists is kept, not only the cell it expands to** — `CrystalData` gains `asymAtoms`: one entry per `atom_site` row, carrying the site's element, label, cartesian and fractional position, occupancy, and **the indices of the symmetry operations that actually placed an image of it** — a site on a special position lists only what survived, a site that duplicated an earlier one lists none. `baseAtoms` remains the expanded cell the viewer draws, atom for atom what it was before, so the two now answer different questions: what is on screen, and what the file says. Nothing but the parser writes the field; a CIF save-back can now be written from the asymmetric unit rather than from the expanded cell under the full operation list (which re-expands on the next read), and that is the next batch's job. Verified on 142619: 147 sites for 294 atoms, every base atom accounted for by exactly one site/operation pair, every site's own position present in the cell, the occupancies carried both ways, and a re-parse giving an identical asymmetric unit.

- **Parser warnings reach the structure panel** — `MolecularData` gains `warnings`, the short list of decisions a parser had to make, and the page shows the first of them — plus a count of the rest — as one amber line at the end of the structure panel. This is the channel the CIF work needed, because a structure that is not what the file literally says must not look like one that is; it is deliberately not a log, so a file that parsed cleanly adds nothing and the panel reads exactly as before, character for character. A warning is HTML-escaped before it is shown, since a site label or a space-group name is the file's own text, and the space-group line was escaped at the same time.

### Changed

- **The powder pattern is computed in slices, shows its progress and can be cancelled** — The panel ran the whole calculation in one call: enumerate the reciprocal sphere, sort it, evaluate the structure factor of every reflection, profile and normalise the curve. On a large cell that is seconds of blocked main thread every time an edit commits, a radiation or range changes, or the panel reopens — the cavity scan was given time slices, the pattern was not. The same engine is now a **resumable job** (`xrdPatternStart` / `xrdPatternStep` / `xrdPatternResult`): each call works for **14 ms** and returns, the phase moves from enumeration to sort, structure factor, the absence cut, the Caglioti profile and normalisation, and the panel's summary line repaints as "Calculating the powder pattern… 46% (10352 of 20000 reflections)". While it runs the **Recalculate** button becomes **Cancel**: cancelling leaves "Calculation cancelled. Recalculate to start again.", disables the export and draws nothing, and a pending slice from the abandoned job is inert (every job carries a sequence number); **Recalculate** starts a fresh job and completes the pattern. Closing the panel, a new radiation or range, an edit or a mode change aborts the job the same way, and no late slice can overwrite the newer state. A cell that fits in the first slice still finishes inside the call that started it, so the old synchronous behaviour — and `xrdPattern()`, which drives the job to completion with an infinite deadline — is byte-identical. The **sort** is sliced too: a bottom-up merge over the job's own reflection order, merging a run at a time and checking the deadline every 512 writes, so no slice runs an uninterruptible `Array#sort` over the whole sphere, and its progress is counted in passes (3–10%). While the job runs the summary line is held to **one line** (`white-space:nowrap` with an ellipsis), and the class is dropped when the pattern is drawn, an error is shown or the job is cancelled, so a repainting progress text never changes the card's height under a layout that is only taken when the job ends. Verified (`verify_xrd.js`, **213 assertions**, from 154): the job handed an expired deadline returns after at least one unit of work, its sort spans several slices and its progress never runs backwards, the running summary's computed style is one line and its class leaves on completion and on cancel, its finished line list and drawn curve are **bit-for-bit** the one-call result, and a **30 Å, 320-atom cell** on the real page shows 18 distinct progress lines over **33 timer ticks** in 543 ms (a one-block port would show none and freeze the timer), with the button Cancel → Recalculate, export disabled → live, a cancel that leaves no rows and no later overwrite, and a Recalculate that restores the same 30-line table. The multi-panel layout suite now also opens **XRD, Cavities and Interactions together** and folds/restores the powder card through its chip there (**925 assertions**).

- **The comparison readout says why a measured peak sits beside the calculated line** — The peak table and a click on a calculated line read the **Kα1** position, while an experimental maximum is the Kα1/Kα2 blend the tube emits; at high angle the two differ by a fraction of a degree, and the delta reported as "calc − exp" carried that systematic part without saying so. The overlay's readout now adds one sentence when the curve carries the doublet: "The calculated value is the Kα1 line while the experimental maximum is the Kα1/Kα2 blend, so part of the delta is systematic (it grows with 2theta and depends on the line width)", and the panel note says the same about the curve's own 2:1 doublet. Nothing about the numbers changes — the table stays Kα1, the curve stays the blend. Verified (`verify_xrd_overlay.js`, **102 assertions**, from 94): a doublet source leaves the line list unchanged, the note names the doublet, the click readout names the Kα1 line and the growing systematic offset, and switching back to Kα1 removes the note.

- **Interactions are indexed per structure and cached per type, so a checkbox no longer rescans 30 000 atoms** — The panel's H-bond and σ-hole search tested every X–H bond against every acceptor and every periodic image of it, and ran the whole scan again whenever a type checkbox was toggled; at 30 000 atoms that is on the order of 10⁸ candidate tests per toggle, and the molecule path had the same shape. The acceptors are now indexed once per structure — a Cartesian grid in a molecule and a fractional grid that wraps the cell in a crystal, sized by the search radius — and each donor walks only its own and the neighbouring bins, with the same distance and angle arithmetic as before. The fixed data (donors, σ-hole centres, acceptor grids, perceived rings) and each type's rows are cached under the scene's structure revision (`intStructRev`), so turning a type off and on reads its rows back, and only a rebuild (edit, undo, frame, supercell change, style) drops the cache; starting or ending the mode clears it too. Verified with the interaction suite's new source pins (**135 assertions**, from 123): the cache check, the per-type row guards, both grid builders and the checkbox→`intRefresh` path are all asserted, and every geometric, crystal-image, π–π and CH–π case from the existing suite stays green.

- **π–π ring pairs are enumerated through a grid with a hard work budget** — Ring pairs used to be all-pairs: O(R²) centroid tests before any image was considered, so a large aromatic framework could lock the tab. A molecule now buckets its ring centroids into a Cartesian grid at the T-distance cut-off, and a crystal into a fractional grid whose pairs are enumerated behind the same plane-width lower bound the contact search uses; each pair still merges its images into one row. Every image tested is charged to `INT_PP_WORK` (**2 000 000**); when the budget is spent the walk stops and the panel's summary says so ("pi-contact search hit its work budget, some pairs were not checked") rather than freezing or pretending the list is complete. The CH–π search already walked a wrapped hydrogen grid and is unchanged. Verified in the same suite (135 assertions), which pins the budget constant, the stop branch and the summary line.

- **The cavity analysis runs in slices, paints progress, and can be cancelled** — **Analyse** used to run the whole job synchronously: sample up to 128³ clearance points, group the free voxels, deepen up to 512 components and refine their deepest points, all before the page could paint, so on a large or crowded cell the tab froze for seconds with no sign of life. The same engine is now a resumable job: the clearance field is marked atom by atom, then the components are grouped, the coarse deepest points are found and the refinements run, each pass working for **14 ms** and yielding to the event loop; while it runs the report line shows "Analysing: marking the atoms (n%)"… and the button becomes **Cancel**, which aborts the job and puts the previous report (and its note) back. A cell edit, an undo or a probe change aborts it the same way, and a pending slice after a cancel is inert. The synchronous entry point (`cavityCompute`) is kept and drives the same state machine to completion, so the engine tests can compare the two paths on the identical report. Verified (`verify_cavity.js`, **135 assertions**, up from 114): the async run matches the synchronous free volume, cavity count, centres, r_free and channels exactly; a cancelled job drops its result, the previous report returns, and a late tick cannot revive it.

- **Powder XRD applies the Debye–Waller factor from the file's ADPs, and Cu/Mo Kα profile the Kα1/Kα2 doublet** — Two omissions stood between the calculated pattern and what a diffractometer records. The structure factor used the file's sites as point atoms, so high-angle lines kept an intensity the thermal motion takes away; and a "Kα" entry was still a single wavelength even though the tube emits both lines. A site that carries an anisotropic displacement tensor (the parser's rotated `uAniso`, Cartesian Å²) now damps its form factor by **T = exp(−2π² qᵀUq)** with q = h a* + k b* + l c* for that reflection; a site without a usable tensor keeps T = 1, because the file states no thermal motion and a guessed B would invent one. **Cu Kα** and **Mo Kα** now carry both lines of the doublet (1.54056/1.54439 and 0.70930/0.71359 Å): the curve sums them at their own 2θ (λ2 through 2asin(λ2/2d)) with the tube's **2:1** weight, while the peak table and the strongest-line list stay the Kα1 reading; the panel note and the exported `.xy` header name both lines when the curve carries them. Cu Kα1, Mo Kα1 and the four other radiations are single-line, unchanged curves. Verified (`verify_xrd.js`, **144 assertions**, up from 117): T against exp(−2π²Uq²) and B = 8π²U for isotropic and anisotropic tensors, the U12 cross term and the non-positive-definite guard, every line of a damped pattern scaled by T² with a stronger high-angle loss, the doublet profile equal to an independent 2:1 summation at the two angles, its resolved high-angle splitting, the unchanged Kα1 peak table, and the panel wiring (tensor carried and validated, Cu Kα doublet; Cu Kα1 single). The overlay (**94**), hkl (**53**) and ADP (**83**) suites are unchanged and green.

- **Paste accepts the 30 000 atoms a crystal can display** — The crystal builder's ceiling was raised to 30 000 atoms but the fragment clipboard still refused a fragment over 20 000, so a structure that could be cut could not be pasted back. `PASTE_ATOM_LIMIT` is now **30 000** — a named constant beside `MAX_CRYSTAL_ATOMS`, with the refusal message quoting it — and the clipboard's sanitizer and the paste guard read it. Verified (`verify_clipboard.js`, **102 assertions**): the limit is the same 30 000 the crystal suite pins, and the existing overflow, placement, selection and undo cases are unchanged.

- **The contact tools open the second toolbar row, and Display offers Polyhedra only on a crystal page** — **Neighbours**, **Interactions** and **Diff** used to be scattered (the first two at the end of the first row, Diff beside the hidden beta/egg buttons); the three now stand together at the head of the second row, left of a separator, with **Select Atoms** and **Box Select** on the other side of it, so the contact tools read as one group and no longer stretch the first row. The Display menu in the View card no longer lists **Polyhedra** when the loaded structure is a molecule: the style draws coordination hulls, and a molecule has no periodic coordination shell to draw. The template decides from the same load payload the page is built with (a crystal in `jsonData` adds the fourth option, a molecule leaves three), so the menu, its option list and its width follow what the page can actually show. Verified: the toolbar suite (**192 assertions**, both rows at 1400×820, 1000×760, 700×600 and 560×540, the regrouped row in the source and every mode still wired), the display-style suite (**268**, molecule page offers three styles and no `polyhedra` value, crystal page offers four), the view-card suite (**84**, the three-option card and a crystal card with the fourth option still unclipped), and the polyhedra page suite (**58**, which drives the style through the engine entry point on a molecular page and asserts the menu has no Polyhedra there).

- **A periodic crystal's coordination polyhedra are complete at the cell faces, and every centre costs two draw calls** — A polyhedron needs its whole coordination shell, but the bonds of a periodic structure are drawn only inside the bounds, so a metal centre on a block face saw only the neighbours inside the box and its hull was built from an incomplete shell (an edge site could lose its polyhedron altogether). The hull pass now completes the shell from the cell's own `baseBonds`: it enumerates the periodic images each pair can reach with the same exact search the bond detector runs (the plane-width lower bound and the `ceil(cutoff/width)` repeats, capped at four) and re-adds the images the drawn bond list omits — only for a periodic component, because a finite molecule is already drawn whole. Each completed bond carries its partner's base site and the integer image it points at, and the hull vertex is placed by that image's lattice shift, so one centre keeps one hull while the picture crosses a face. The geometry is still one indexed `BufferGeometry` for the faces with one shared position attribute for the edges (flat colours per centre, the centre's own colour), rebuilt only when the structure or the style changes; a drag rewrites the vertex positions from `MD.atoms` through the hull's stored relative offsets — zero rebuilds during a drag, exactly one at the commit. Verified on the real page in headless Edge, counting WebGL draw calls on the context: every one of the **32** sodium sites of a 2×2×2 NaCl cell now gets its octahedron, boundary ones included, and the polyhedra frame is ball and stick **+2 draws** at every size — 7→9 at 2×2×2 and 6→8 at 4×4×4, both well inside the 20-draw budget, 2→4 for a 7-atom FeO6 complex — with the frame rate of ball and stick (900 ms: 54 vs 55 frames; 4×4×4: 54/54). `verify_polyhedra_page.js` grows to **58 assertions** (completed shells, the 32 centres, draw calls, frame counts, drag synchronisation and picking through the translucent hulls); the display-style suite reaches **268**, panel-layout 780 and view-card 84.

- **A supercell may build up to 30 000 atoms, and the cavity probe up to 20 Å** — The atom ceiling was 20 000, low enough that a large cell's 4×4×4 preset or a wider custom slab was refused outright even though the scene now draws instanced atoms in about a dozen calls; `MAX_CRYSTAL_ATOMS` is **30 000**, and every estimate and refusal message reads the constant, so a bounds change or an Edit Cell that would exceed it is still refused before anything is built and still names the count it would build and the limit. The cavity analysis probe was capped at 4 Å, too small to trace the wide channels and large cages a big-pore cell can hold; its input now accepts **0–20 Å** and `cavityAnalyse()` clamps at 20, with the grid and surface ceilings unchanged. Verified: the crystal suite (**223**) pins the 30 000 ceiling and its refusal wording, the cell-edit suite (**137**) exercises the ceiling through Edit Cell, and the cavity suite (**114**) expects the 20 Å clamp.

- **A disorder group is saved and counted as the ordered variant it shows, at full occupancy** — Choosing **Group 1** drew only that alternative, but its sites kept the file's occupancy, so a 0.6/0.4 cell still exported as half-filled: the info panel's `Unit Cell:` formula counted every alternative, the density weighed all of them at 0.6/0.4, and the CIF/VESTA occupancy column wrote 0.600 — a cell that is on screen a fully ordered variant read everywhere as a disordered average. The disorder select now carries a **Selected group fully occupied** switch (on by default, inert in All). With it on, `crystalOccOf` counts the selected group's sites as occupancy 1 for the info panel's formula and density and for the CIF/VESTA occupancy columns; with it off the file's own values are written back. The formula and density follow the **visible** base sites either way, so they describe the same variant the exports write. All is untouched: `crystalVisibleBaseAtoms()` returns the base array itself and `crystalOccOf` returns the stated occupancy, leaving the formula, density, CIF and VESTA **byte-identical** whichever way the switch stands. Verified (`verify_crystal_export.js`, 7.16–7.31, suite **203**): the 0.6/0.4 cell in group 1 saves CIF/VESTA at 1.000 and reports 0.431 g/cm³ for the ordered C2O2 formula in its 216 Å³ cell (0.600 / 0.332 with the switch off), while All keeps 0.600/0.400, the C2N2O2 formula and 0.418 g/cm³, byte for byte. The crystal suite (**223**) covers the switch's enabled/disabled state, its own state across a rebuild and the panel wiring (18.30–18.38).

- **The atom spheres are instanced, so a supercell costs a handful of draw calls instead of one per atom** — The bonds were instanced long ago; the atoms were not. Every atom was its own `THREE.Mesh`, so CCDC 142619 at 2×2×2 was **2352 draw calls for the spheres alone**, on top of the bond pool, and the larger the cell the worse it got. An atom is now a **record** — `atomMeshes` holds a lightweight handle (position + `userData`, never added to the scene) instead of a Mesh — and one shared unit sphere draws every atom of an appearance, scaled per instance by its drawn radius and coloured through the **instance colour buffer**. The material cannot vary inside one instanced draw, so atoms are grouped by the appearance its material decides (ordinary / ghost / occupancy) and, within that, by highlight state (plain / selected / difference-marked) into **three instanced meshes per appearance**: plain, selected and difference-marked atoms are separate draws, so a highlight change is two `setMatrixAt` writes and **never adds an object or a draw call**. Every other pass keeps the per-atom contract it always had (`position`, `userData`, `visible`), and the picking path became the analytic ray–sphere test the ray always meant: the nearest sphere along the ray in the group's own frame, answered without walking a geometry. The comparison's right half is instanced the same way, in its own layer (a marked atom stays the comparison's orange). Measured on the **real page** (headless Edge, WebGL calls counted on the context itself): the 294-atom cell renders in **13 draw calls**, the 2352-atom 2×2×2 view in **14**, and a selection repaint stays at **15** — the supercell adds atoms, not calls. Picking and selection are unchanged: 12 pick-style assertions (every atom reachable in all three display styles, a click selecting the atom the hover resolved, ball/space-filling point-for-point identical to a build without the stick cone) and 48 selection assertions stay green; the display-style suite grows to **248 assertions** (the scene build hands every atom to the layer with the radius the current style asks for, no per-atom Mesh is created, the right half is a layer of its own) and a new real-engine suite (`verify_atom_instancing.js`, **16 assertions**) measures the draw calls and the click. Every other suite is unchanged and green: 137 crystal, 44 crystal-bonds, 86 crystal-edit, 123 crystal-export, 142 CIF, 237 ghost, 105 keyboard, 101 clipboard, 67 provenance, 48 selection, 2 870 aromatic-dash, 159 ORCA, 44 autoplay, 192 toolbar, 80 view-card and 12 pick-style assertions.

- **The page-reading verification suites use the DevTools protocol instead of `--dump-dom`** — Three suites (`verify_toolbar.js`, `verify_view_card.js` and `verify_pick_styles.js`) collected their measurements by running headless Edge with `--dump-dom` and scraping the probe's output from the dumped document. On this machine that path returns an empty document, so the suites reported "no measurement output" / "`__plReady` never appeared" even though the page itself was fine, and a check that only passes on some machines is no check. The spawn + **remote-debugging CDP** pattern the crystal diff, polyhedra page and panel-layout suites already used now drives these three as well: each run spawns Edge with its own temporary profile and debugging port, polls `Runtime.evaluate` for the probe's finished `#out` element, then kills the browser and removes the profile. `verify_panel_layout.js` already worked this way and is unchanged. All four suites pass on the same machine with no manual rerun — toolbar **192**, view card **84**, pick styles **12**, panel layout **925** assertions — and no assertion's meaning changed.

### Fixed

- **The powder pattern counts a disorder group at the occupancy the formula, the density and every save use** — With a group selected in the cell panel, the panel, the exports and the pattern must describe the same ordered variant. `crystalOccOf` is the one reader of that choice — the info panel's formula and density and the CIF/VESTA occupancy columns all go through it — but `xrdCellSites` read `baseAtoms[i].occupancy` directly, so with **Selected group fully occupied** on, the composition and density counted the chosen group at 1 while the pattern still weighted it at the file's 0.5: one screen state, two chemistries. The site builder now takes its occupancy from `crystalOccOf` too, so the pattern and every other reading of the cell cannot disagree. Verified (`verify_xrd.js`, new assertions in the disorder section): the selected group at full occupancy gives the same line intensities as a file that literally states occupancy 1 (to 1e-12), a half-filled variant scatters less, and **All** keeps both alternatives at their stated occupancies.

- **Diff no longer leaves one-cell analyses over a two-structure comparison** — An overlay belongs to one unit cell, but starting a comparison only hid the interaction and neighbour panels: the powder pattern, the cavity report and the bond-valence table stayed open, covering the right-hand structure while every number described the left one. The three tools now refuse in Diff like the others — **Powder XRD** and **Cavities** write "… is not available in diff mode" to the status line instead of opening, and **BVS** opens a small modal that says the sums read one unit cell and points back to View — and entering Diff with a card open **folds it into its chip** (a cavity scan still running is cancelled with its panel), so nothing is left over the comparison and the chip brings the card back after the comparison ends. The BVS hover tooltip is suppressed in Diff as well, so no surface reports a sum about half the picture. Verified (`verify_crystal_diff.js`, **33 assertions**): entering Diff closes the open pattern and cavity cards and stops the scan, the three buttons refuse with their exact messages, the BVS refusal is a modal and closes, leaving Diff reopens the pattern with the chip gone, and the display style steps in and out of the comparison as described below; the BVS suite (**168 assertions**, new 5.52–5.58 and 6.11–6.12) drives the tooltip and the refusal directly, and the cavity suite (**135**) stays green.

- **A crystal comparison keeps both halves in one display style** — Polyhedra draws coordination hulls from a bond list, and the comparison's right-hand half is the compared file's wrapped cell, drawn atom for atom with no periodic bonds of its own; choosing the style during Diff therefore drew hulls on the left supercell and left the right side as balls — one picture, two models. **Display → Polyhedra** is now refused while the comparison is up: the select snaps back to the style in force and the status line says "Polyhedra is not available in Diff mode", with no scene rebuild, no right-half rebuild and no relayout. Entering Diff with the style already on puts the picture back into **Ball & Stick** before the right-hand model is built, so the comparison never opens half-hulled; the style can be chosen again as soon as the comparison ends. Verified (`verify_display_style.js`, **268 assertions**, from 262): the control refuses in Diff, restores the select, rebuilds nothing and writes the message; `verify_crystal_diff.js` (**33 assertions**, from 29) drives a real page with Polyhedra already chosen, watches Diff open in ball and stick, tries the style through the menu during Diff and reads the exact status line.

- **A Neighbours radius wider than the image box says so instead of returning a short list** — The crystal search enumerates integer lattice images up to `CRYSTAL_MAX_IMAGE_REPEAT` (**4**) per axis, sized from the cutoff and the plane width. An ordinary cell needs no more than four at the 12 Å maximum, but a layered cell whose planes are under about 3 Å apart reaches past the cap: a 2.5 Å *c* asks for five images along *c*, the box holds four, and contacts between 10 and 12 Å were silently missing from a list that read as complete. The computation now records which axes were capped and the **radius every axis still covers** (`min lim_k × width_k`), and the panel summary appends "image search capped at 4 cells along c: contacts beyond 10.00 A may be missing". An uncapped search — the cubic cell at 12 Å, whose box exactly reaches the radius, and every molecule — adds nothing, so an honest list reads exactly as it did. Verified (`verify_neighbours.js`, **64 assertions**, from 54): the layered cell at 12 Å reports "along c" and "beyond 10.00 A", the cubic cell at the same radius reports no notice, a molecule never does, and the requested radius is still stated.

- **Polyhedra holds the thermal ellipsoids back instead of drawing them over its centre marker** — The ORTEP switch only asked whether the structure carried anisotropic displacement parameters, not how the atoms were being drawn: under **Polyhedra** the centre atom is a small marker under its hull, so an ellipsoid did not replace a ball — it stood in for the marker and filled the hull with a 50% probability blob. The switch is now inert in that style (disabled and unchecked, its title naming Polyhedra), the ellipsoids are not drawn there, and the earlier choice is **remembered**: switching back to Ball & Stick brings them back without touching the checkbox again. Ball & Stick, Space-filling, Stick and a structure without ADPs are unchanged. Verified (`verify_adp.js`, **92 assertions**, new 4.27a–g and 6.6a/b): the polyhedra state leaves the switch off and disabled with its title naming the style, the ellipsoid is absent from the matrix writer while the choice survives, the ball style restores both, and the source guards pin the `displayStyle` tests in `rebuildScene` and `syncOrtepControl`.

- **The cavity clearance field marks every periodic image a probe can reach, however far that is** — The field walk clamped each axis to ±3 images. A probe whose reach R = r_vdW + probe, projected onto an inverse-lattice row, exceeds three cells — a probe over ~10 Å in an ordinary cell, or a strongly skewed or small cell at any probe — left unmarked images that really touch the cell, so the scan reported free voxels and even cavities inside solid space. The walk is now sized exactly: an image at repeat s is at least (|s| − 1) · (plane width) away, so marking repeats |s| ≤ ceil(R · r_i) is complete, with r_i the row norm of the inverse lattice; and when R reaches half the cell's edge-length sum, every voxel is inside the probe's reach and the field is filled with the blocked sentinel in one pass instead of walking anything.
